# Instructions

All rules in this file are mandatory; violations are penalized. Rules marked **CRITICAL** take priority.

## General Instructions

**CRITICAL — Kubernetes rules are triggered by STEP TYPES in your output, not the prompt's project type.** A "wave deployment", "canary", "progressive", "LLM", "orchestration", or "Argo CD" project gets every Kubernetes rule below (never skip them because "this is not a Kubernetes project") once its output contains `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`.

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over templates) — omit template default accounts (`account_octopussamples_azure_account`, `account_aws_oidc`) when the prompt names an account.** "Add an Azure OIDC account named 'X'" / "Create an Azure account called 'X'" / "Add an Azure Service Principal account called 'X'" → no `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal` named `account_octopussamples_azure_account`. "Add an AWS account named 'X'" → no `octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account` named `account_aws_oidc`. Repoint ALL references (step `execution_properties`, variables such as `Project.Azure.Account`) to the prompt's account.

**CRITICAL — use the prompt-specified package feed for packages.** For "Add a Docker container registry feed called 'X'" / "use the 'X' feed for deployment": create feed X AND use it as `feed_id` in all relevant `packages` blocks. `feed_github_container_registry` is only allowed as a step `container` (worker tools) feed, never as the package feed when the prompt names one; keep it alongside X if Kubernetes worker tool containers need it. Same for a prompt-named Maven/Helm feed, even if template `octopusdeploy_maven_feed` `feed_octopus_maven_feed` exists. Self-check: each "Add a feed named 'X'" / "use the 'X' feed for..." has a feed with `name = "X"` and is the `feed_id` of its packages.

**CRITICAL — a prompt-named feed MUST actually be referenced.** Templates that hardcode the image inline (`image: octopussamples/randomquotes`, `Packages: []` — Progressive Deployment, canary and wave templates; the Kubernetes template already does it right) have no `feed_id` to check, so the feed ends up orphaned. When the prompt names a feed for an image (e.g. `Create a Docker container registry feed called "Commerce Registry" ... and use it for the container image "octopussamples/randomquotes" in the Kubernetes deployment step`), ADD a `packages` block on that feed and reference it in `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` instead of a literal tag:

```hcl
packages = {
  randomquotes = {
    package_id           = "octopussamples/randomquotes"
    feed_id              = "${...feed_commerce_registry...}"
    acquisition_location = "NotAcquired"
    properties           = { Extract = "False", Purpose = "DockerImageReference", SelectionMode = "immediate" }
  }
}
```
```yaml
image: "octopussamples/randomquotes:#{Octopus.Action.Package[randomquotes].PackageVersion}"
```

* **CRITICAL — the `packages` map KEY is a reference name, not the package id.** Only letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores, hashes; `/` and `:` are illegal (fails at CREATE: `Package name 'octopussamples/randomquotes' contains invalid characters`, dropping that step and all later steps). Put the id in `package_id`; key = last path segment minus illegal chars: `octopussamples/randomquotes` → `randomquotes`, `com.octopus:ledger-batch` → `ledger-batch`. The same key MUST be used in `#{Octopus.Action.Package[<key>].PackageVersion}`.
* Generally, an account, certificate, feed or worker pool the prompt names but no step, variable or target references has not been "used".
* Self-check: search for each prompt feed's resource address; if only its `resource` and `data` blocks mention it, add the `packages` block and `#{Octopus.Action.Package[...]}` reference to the step the prompt tied it to.

**CRITICAL — create every prompt-specified account** ("Add an AWS OIDC account called 'X'", "Add an Azure account called 'X'", "Add an ... account called 'X'", "Create an ... account named 'X'") as a top-level `octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account` / `octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect` with the exact name, regardless of template (including Kubernetes). E.g. "Add an AWS OIDC account called 'AWS Deploy Account' with the role ARN 'arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/OctopusDeployRole'":
```
resource "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account" "account_aws_deploy_account" {
  name                              = "AWS Deploy Account"
  role_arn                          = "arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/OctopusDeployRole"
  account_test_subject_keys         = ["space"]
  execution_subject_keys            = ["space"]
  health_subject_keys               = ["space"]
  session_duration                  = 3600
  environments                      = []
  tenant_tags                       = []
  tenants                           = []
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
}
```

**CRITICAL — every `#{VariableName}` MUST have a matching variable** (a dangling `#{Foo}` silently resolves to literal text). Check every non-`Octopus.` `#{...}` token in `execution_properties`, scripts and inline YAML — including ones you invented, ones copied from community step template defaults (`BlueGreen.Octopus.Api.Key = "#{Project.Octopus.Api.Key}"` needs `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`), and ones INHERITED FROM THE PROJECT TYPE TEMPLATE. Satisfied by an `octopusdeploy_variable`, a linked library variable set, or a project tenant variable template. Never create `Octopus.` system variables.
* Known case — AWS Lambda template: `Validate Setup` checks `IsNullOrWhitespace("#{Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName}")` but the template variables (`Project.AWS.Lambda.S3.BucketName`, `Project.AWS.Lambda.S3.FileName`, `Project.AWS.Region`, `Project.AWS.Account`, `Resources:ProductsMicroservice:Properties:CodeUri`, `Project.WorkerPool`, `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`) omit it. Emit `Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName` as a `String` = the function name (from the project name if not given). Generally, any variable a template script checks is configured MUST be emitted.

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over templates and prompt silence) — any project with an `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` step MUST create a "Mock K8s" target.** Applies even when the prompt only names a tag ("on targets tagged `insight-cluster`"), a cluster, or a Kubernetes/canary/progressive/wave/blue-green deployment — a tag doesn't create a target. Emit:

1. `octopusdeploy_token_account` "Mock Token" (with `data "octopusdeploy_accounts"` lookup and `count`); not an AWS/Azure/username-password account. Use a prompt-named Token account instead if given. When the prompt mentions no account (most common reason this is skipped), invent "Mock Token" anyway; a missing account never justifies omitting the target.
2. `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry` "Docker Hub" at `https://index.docker.io`, NO credentials.
3. `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` "Mock K8s": `cluster_url = "https://mockk8s.octopusdemos.com"`, `roles` = EVERY target tag of the Kubernetes steps plus `"Kubernetes"`, `environments` = every lifecycle environment, `endpoint { communication_style = "Kubernetes" }`, `authentication { account_id = … }` → token account, `default_worker_pool_id` = "Hosted Ubuntu".

Self-check: if output contains `Octopus.Kubernetes` or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` appears exactly once; `environments`, `roles`, `endpoint` NON-EMPTY (`MinItems: 1`, else `Not enough list items`); each environment is a real id from a `data.octopusdeploy_environments.*` / `octopusdeploy_environment.*` pair (`environments = [""]` fails apply: `The EnvironmentIds field is required.`). Canonical HCL: `## Kubernetes target` in `# Patterns`.

**CRITICAL — EVERY `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` step (when using "Mock K8s") MUST set these (overwrite the template's `ResourceStatusCheck = "True"` / `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "True"`):**

```
"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck"      = "False"
"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait" = "NoWait"
"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled"  = "False"
```

* Otherwise the deployment fails at runtime. Includes PROMPT-ADDED raw-YAML steps (e.g. "Add a 'Deploy raw Kubernetes YAML' step called 'Deploy Canary'", often emitted with only `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml`, `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource`, `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer`). "the Kubernetes steps" / "the K8s steps" / "the Kubernetes step" covers every raw-YAML step in the final process.
* NEVER put these three on `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` steps (those still need the target).
* Self-check: walking `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`, raw-YAML step count = triple count with exactly `"False"`, `"NoWait"`, `"False"`; delete `ResourceStatusCheck`, `DeploymentWait`, `ServerSideApply.Enabled` from script/Helm steps.

**CRITICAL — "Mock K8s" `container { feed_id = … image = "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04" }` MUST use the "Docker Hub" feed.** Never empty, never `Octopus Server (built-in)` / `data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_server_built_in`, never the prompt's package feed, never template `feed_ghcr_anonymous` / `feed_github_container_registry` (GHCR can't serve this Docker Hub image). Create "Docker Hub" even if the prompt names no feed or another Docker feed (even one at `https://index.docker.io`). Feeds by ROLE: package feed = prompt's; step `container` = GitHub Container Registry feed; target `container` = "Docker Hub".

**CRITICAL — tenanted projects:** if project `tenanted_deployment_participation` is `Tenanted` or `TenantedOrUntenanted`, "Mock K8s" uses the same value and its `tenants` (or `tenant_tags`) include every prompt-linked tenant; link any Token account created for it to those tenants too.

**CRITICAL — EVERY `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` (including a channel-only "hotfix" one) MUST have `count` and a matching `data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles"` lookup**, following `## Lifecycle Phase` / `## Retention Policy for New Project Creation Prompts` literally.
* Terraform's `lifecycle { }` meta-block takes ONLY `prevent_destroy`, `ignore_changes`, `create_before_destroy`. NEVER put `release_retention_with_strategy` / `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` in it (`Blocks of type "release_retention_with_strategy" are not expected here`):

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "My Lifecycle"

  # CORRECT — NOT inside the Terraform lifecycle {} meta-block. Always write these MULTI-LINE, never on one line.
  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  tentacle_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  phase { ... }

  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true      # ONLY prevent_destroy / ignore_changes / create_before_destroy belong here
  }
}
```

**CRITICAL — retention must never make the plan fail; if unsure, OMIT IT.** Preference: (1) complete project with correct retention; (2) complete project with none (Octopus defaults); never (3) nothing. If unsure of `release_retention_with_strategy` / `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` syntax, omit BOTH; likewise if a prior attempt failed with `Unsupported block type` naming them. Same for runbook retention.

**CRITICAL — never emit `retention_policy` on `octopusdeploy_runbook`** (deprecated: `Runbook Retention Policies will soon require strategy`). `quantity_to_keep` + `should_keep_forever` together fail plan; `quantity_to_keep = N` with `should_keep_forever = false` is normalised to `0` and aborts apply. If truly unavoidable: `should_keep_forever = true` alone. Prompt retention instructions apply to the LIFECYCLE (`octopusdeploy_lifecycle`), not runbooks.

**Only when the prompt asks to keep N RUNBOOK RUNS** (e.g. `Add a runbook called "Recycle Claims App Pool" ... and keep the last 100 runbook runs`), use `retention_policy_with_strategy`:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_recycle_claims_app_pool" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "Recycle Claims App Pool"

  retention_policy_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 100
    unit             = "Items"
  }
}
```

* **CRITICAL — the name is `retention_policy_with_strategy`, NOT `run_retention_policy_with_strategy`** (nonexistent; plan fails). Example configs use `retention_policy_with_strategy { strategy = "Forever" }`.
* `unit`: `"Items"` for runs, `"Days"` only for days. Never combine with `retention_policy`. Unrequested retention belongs nowhere.

**CRITICAL — `quantity_to_keep` only with `strategy = "Count"`, and must be ≥ 1** (`quantity_to_keep = 0` rejected under every strategy). `strategy = "Default"` / `strategy = "Forever"` take nothing else: `retention_policy_with_strategy { strategy = "Default" }`. EXPORTED Terraform (`retention_policy_with_strategy { strategy = "Default", quantity_to_keep = 0, unit = "Items" }`, e.g. the progressive template's `Deploy Release` runbook) is NOT valid input — drop those two lines.

**CRITICAL — `minimum_environments_before_promotion` MUST NEVER exceed the environment count of its OWN `phase`** (`automatic_deployment_targets` + `optional_deployment_targets`; a 1-environment phase allows only `0` or `1`). Violations abort the apply early (`The phase "Claims Staging" requires a minimum of 2 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have been added.`) and nothing is created. A "deployments" count never sets it: `a "Claims Staging" phase that automatically promotes releases after 2 successful deployments to "Claims Dev"` → Octopus has no "promote after N successful deployments" feature; write `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0`. See `## Lifecycle Phase `minimum_environments_before_promotion` Instructions`.

**CRITICAL — a wrong or missing `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer` aborts the apply**; Octopus recomputes it on save, and the failing step and everything after it is never created.
* **Decision order**: step has `worker_pool_id` / `worker_pool_variable` → `"true"` (even a package step with target roles). Else package-deployment step type (`Octopus.TentaclePackage`, `Octopus.WindowsService`, `Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.TomcatDeploy`) whose SAME step declares `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` → `"false"`. Else (incl. `Octopus.Script`, manual, webhook/curl) → `"true"`. In doubt → `"true"`. Never copy `"false"` between steps.
* **EVERY runbook step** (any `octopusdeploy_process_step` whose `process_id` is a runbook's `octopusdeploy_process`) → `"true"`, even when it acts on a machine ("restart the service", "drain connections from the target", "recycle the app pool").
* **"runs on a worker" means `"true"`**: "on a worker", "on a worker rather than the target", "off the target", "on the Octopus Server" → `"true"` plus `worker_pool_id`/`worker_pool_variable`.
* **NEVER OMIT the key** (`.execution_properties: new element "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" has appeared.`). Every `Octopus.Script` and runbook step states `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` explicitly.
* Self-check: re-verify every `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "false"` against the decision order (and not in a runbook); add the key as `"true"` to every `Octopus.Script`, `Octopus.Manual`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` or runbook step lacking it.

**CRITICAL — no `""` values in `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` `parameters`** (discarded by Octopus → `.parameters: element "Run.Runbook.PromptedVariables" has vanished.`). Set only parameters with real values, omit the rest (no `"Run.Runbook.PromptedVariables" = ""`, `"Run.Runbook.Tenant.Name" = ""`, `"Run.Runbook.CustomNotes" = ""`). All community step templates, not just "Run Octopus Deploy Runbook".

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over templates) — an unscoped `octopusdeploy_variable` has NO `scope` block**; `scope {}` or an all-`null` scope aborts the apply (`.scope: block count changed from 1 to 0`).

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over the prompt) — `actions` in a variable `scope` is ALWAYS `null`.** For `scoped to the "<Step Name>" step`: `octopusdeploy_process_step.<name>.id` is the STEP id, not the ACTION id `Scope.Actions` needs, so `actions = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_deploy_fraud_lambda.id]` silently matches nothing.
* Keep the variable (losing the step scope is expected), scoped only by environments/channels/target tags/tenant tags the prompt also gave, or with no `scope` block. Don't drop it; don't invent a `roles` scope.
* `processes` is no escape hatch: only a PROJECT id (`octopusdeploy_project.<name>[0].id`) or RUNBOOK id (`octopusdeploy_runbook.<name>[0].id`).
* NO scope attribute (`actions`, `processes`, `machines`, `roles`) may hold an `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` id. Remove any `scope` left with no non-null attribute.

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over templates) — tenant variable TEMPLATE ≠ VALUE.** "add a project tenant variable template called 'X'" → only a `template` block on `octopusdeploy_project`. Create `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` only for a prompt-given value for a named tenant (empty `value` fails: `Variable was created but ID not returned in response`).

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over templates) — "project tenant variable" = `template` block on `octopusdeploy_project` + `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` values, NEVER `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`** (that sets library variable set templates). Use it only when the prompt explicitly puts the template in a library variable set and you emit the `octopusdeploy_library_variable_set`. Never hard-code `library_variable_set_id`, `template_id` or `included_library_variable_sets` to literals like `"LibraryVariableSets-5"` or a GUID.

**CRITICAL (ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over the prompt's environments and templates) — Blue/Green needs TWO DISTINCT production environments.** `Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment` needs `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name` ≠ `BlueGreen.Environment.Green.Name` (never both `Production`). If the prompt lists only `Production`, replace it with `Blue Production` and `Green Production`, each in its own `phase` (one environment each, both `is_optional_phase = true`); never one merged `Production` phase.

**CRITICAL — sequential `depends_on` lists ONLY the immediately preceding step**: step N → `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.step_N_minus_1]`; never all previous steps.

**CRITICAL — ALL resources in a new project creation MUST use `count` with data source lookup**: `count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_<TYPE>s.<data_name>.<attribute>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"` plus a matching `data "octopusdeploy_<TYPE>"` (idempotent re-apply). Applies to:
- `octopusdeploy_project`, `octopusdeploy_process` (ternary `project_id`); `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` (ternary `process_id`)
- `octopusdeploy_variable`, `octopusdeploy_lifecycle`, `octopusdeploy_environment`, `octopusdeploy_channel`, `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger`, `octopusdeploy_community_step_template`, `octopusdeploy_project_versioning_strategy`, `octopusdeploy_project_group`, feeds (`octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry`, `octopusdeploy_maven_feed`, `octopusdeploy_nuget_feed`, etc.)
- when requested: `octopusdeploy_tenant`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` / `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`; `octopusdeploy_tag_set` / `octopusdeploy_tag` (tenant tags)

```hcl
# Data source to lookup existing project
data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_my_project" {
  ids           = null
  partial_name = "My Project"
  skip          = 0
  take          = 1
}

variable "project_my_project_name" {
  type         = string
  nullable     = false
  sensitive    = false
  description = "The name of the project"
  default      = "My Project"
}

# Resource with count for idempotent re-apply
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_project" {
  count                            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                             = "${var.project_my_project_name}"
  lifecycle_id                     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_devsecops.lifecycles) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_devsecops.lifecycles[0].id : octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_devsecops[0].id}"
  project_group_id                 = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_kubernetes.project_groups) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_kubernetes.project_groups[0].id : octopusdeploy_project_group.project_group_kubernetes[0].id}"
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
  # ...
  default_worker_pool_id          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools[0].id : octopusdeploy_worker_pool.workerpool_default_worker_pool[0].id}"
  default_tool_version            = "ToolVersionLatest"
}

resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  depends_on = []
}

resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_first_step" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name         = "First Step"
  type         = "Octopus.Script"
  process_id   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"
  # ... other attributes ...
}

resource "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" "process_step_my_project_community_step" {
  # count, name, process_id: same as octopusdeploy_process_step
  template_id    = "${data.octopusdeploy_step_template.steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.step_template != null ? data.octopusdeploy_step_template.steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.step_template.id : octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities[0].id}"
  # ... other attributes ...
}

resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "my_project_connection_string" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  owner_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  name         = "Connection.String"
  # ...
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes = [sensitive_value]
  }
  depends_on = []
}

data "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" "communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities" {
  website = "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/a38bfff8-8dde-4dd6-9fd0-c90bb4709d5a"
}
data "octopusdeploy_step_template" "steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities" {
  name = "Scan for Vulnerabilities"
}
resource "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" "communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities" {
  count                              = "${data.octopusdeploy_step_template.steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.step_template != null ? 0 : 1}"
  community_action_template_id       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.steps) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.steps[0].id : null}"
}
```

* Environments (`data "octopusdeploy_environments" "environment_development"`, `partial_name = "Development"`; `count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`) and lifecycles (`count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_devsecops.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`, name e.g. "DevSecOps") also need `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`.
* Channels (e.g. `channel_my_project_hotfix`, "Hotfix": `lifecycle_id` ternary on `data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_hotfix` / `octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_hotfix[0].id`, `is_default = false`, `tenant_tags = []`) and `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger` (e.g. `projecttrigger_my_project_daily_scan`, "Daily Scan") base `count` on `data.octopusdeploy_projects` (NOT a channels data source) and use the same ternary `project_id`; triggers add `space_id   = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"`.
* Worker pools: data source `data "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" "workerpool_default_worker_pool"` (`partial_name = "Default Worker Pool"`, same shape) + ternary, as in `default_worker_pool_id`. A step's `worker_pool_variable` is `"Octopus.Project.WorkerPool"`.
* Community step templates use BOTH the step_template and community_step_template data sources.
* Self-check: every listed resource has `count`; every `count`/ternary references a data source present in the output; `data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools` exists for any worker pool lookup.

* Create all required resources and valid Terraform; ignore any mention of the current project.
* A prompt-named target space beats the current space: `Create a project called 'Example Project' in the 'Example Space'. The current project is 'My Web App'. The current space is 'Default'.` → 'Example Space'; `Create a project called 'Example Project'. The current project is 'My Web App'. The current space is 'Default'.` → 'Default'.
* **CRITICAL — report inexpressible requirements as omitted, never as built** (e.g. "automatically pre-scale using the burst predictor", "refuse to deploy when the queue depth exceeds 10000 messages", "abort when the p99 latency oracle reports a regression"). Build the rest; in the `description` of `octopusdeploy_project` / `octopusdeploy_runbook` and step `notes`, state the shortfall; never invent Octopus features or claim the behaviour in present tense.
  * CORRECT: `"Deploys an Azure Function for notification dispatch. NOTE: automatic pre-scaling with a burst predictor, and refusing to deploy on queue depth, have no Octopus Deploy equivalent and are not implemented by this configuration."`
* **Spinnaker migration prompts only:** for an instruction to add a new step, use the example lifecycle, runbooks, steps and variables, then add the new step.
* **New project creation prompts** ("Create a [type] project", e.g. "Create a Kubernetes project", "Create an Azure Web App project", with "Add a step to..." instructions): template steps, variables and triggers are the baseline, and EVERY "Add a step called 'X'" step MUST ALSO exist as its own `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` with `name = "X"` and the exact configuration. **CRITICAL: a template step is NOT a substitute** (template "Deploy Azure Web App Container" doesn't satisfy "Add a step called 'Deploy to Azure Web App'" with its package and account). Self-check each "Add a step" instruction.
* **CRITICAL EXCEPTION:** requested tenant variables → `template` blocks in `octopusdeploy_project` (variable definitions, not steps) — the ONLY case for `template` blocks in new project creation.

**CRITICAL — naming your own steps NEVER suppresses the template baseline**. "Add an inline PowerShell script step called 'Verify Canary Health'" still emits the template's steps, environments, variables and runbooks; named steps are **additions to**, never a **replacement for**, the template — for every type (Tomcat, IIS, Azure Web App, Kubernetes, Lambda, Script, Terraform).
* E.g. "progressive deployment" / "canary" / "wave" / "rollout" with two named script steps still emits the four Progressive Deployment template steps, `prod_10` / `prod_50` / `prod_100`, and `Project.Release.Id`, `Project.WorkerPool`, `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`, `Project.SimulateFail`.
* A prompt lifecycle, project group, feed or account replaces the template's for the project; template environments, variables, steps remain.

**CRITICAL — "the project uses the prompt's version" means `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id` MUST point at the prompt's lifecycle**, else it is an orphan.

* Most likely with templates that ship a named lifecycle — `Blue Green` (VM Blue/Green), `DevSecOps` (Kubernetes, Tomcat, IIS, Windows Service), `Application` (Claude): the template lifecycle lands in `lifecycle_id` and the prompt's is added beside it.
* **MERGE, don't emit two lifecycles.** Build ONE lifecycle with the prompt's NAME and retention plus the template's REQUIRED PHASES; point `lifecycle_id` at it; emit no template lifecycle. E.g. Blue/Green + `Payments Lifecycle` → one `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` `Payments Lifecycle` with mandatory `Development`, `Test` and optional `Blue Production` / `Green Production` phases.
* Same for project group: prompt-named group → `project_group_id` points at it.
* Self-check: for each `Create a lifecycle called "X"` / `using a new lifecycle called "X"` that is the project's (not a channel's) lifecycle, `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id` resolves to it, no template-named `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` remains, and it has every phase the type requires.
* Tell of a dropped baseline: no step deploys anything (only verification/rollback/notification/health-check scripts), or every step is a script step.

**CRITICAL: KEEPING THE TEMPLATE'S DEPLOY STEP IS NOT KEEPING THE TEMPLATE BASELINE.** Most common failure (passes the tells above): named steps + ONE template step, the deploy step (`Deploy to Tomcat via Manager`, `Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML`, `Deploy Azure Web App Container`, `Deploy to IIS`, … — not limited to these), with the other steps, template variables (`Project.WorkerPool`, `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`, `OctopusPrintVariables`, …) and `Daily Security Scan` trigger gone. Worse: ZERO template steps (e.g. Windows Service prompt naming `Stop Collector`/`Start Collector` → only those two, no `Octopus.WindowsService` step).
* A renamed deploy step (e.g. `Deploy Insight API`) is still one template step.
* Most often lost: `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check Targets Available`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Print Message when No Targets`, `Deployment Success Notification` — none optional. Community templates need `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` + their own data sources.
* Judge only by the name-by-name list, never by whether it "looks like a reasonable process" (small templates hide losses).
* Prompt-supplied triggers (e.g. `Weekly Claims Refresh`) and variables (e.g. `Claims.AppPool`) are SEPARATE from and do not replace template ones (`Daily Security Scan`, `Project.WorkerPool`); both must exist. A passing variable check says nothing about steps.

**CRITICAL: every project type's DEFINING STEP TYPE (`ActionType`) must appear in the process:**

| Prompt says | Process MUST contain a step of type |
| --- | --- |
| Windows service project | Octopus.WindowsService |
| IIS project | Octopus.IIS |
| Tomcat project | Octopus.TomcatDeploy |
| Azure Web App project | Octopus.AzureWebApp |
| Kubernetes / canary / wave / progressive project | Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml (or Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade) |
| AWS Lambda project | Octopus.AwsUploadS3 + Octopus.AwsRunCloudFormation |
| Terraform project | Octopus.TerraformApply |
| Script project | Octopus.Script |

* Prompt-requested script steps never satisfy this; "stop the service"/"start the service" steps go AROUND the deployment, not replace it.
* Only exception: prompt explicitly wants no steps ("an empty project", "a blank project", "with no steps" and no subsequent "Add a step" instructions).
* Self-check FIRST: read type from the prompt's first line, find its `ActionType` in the output; none → STOP and add template steps.

**TEMPLATE BASELINE STEP LISTS (complete, in order). Copy your row, don't reconstruct from memory.** Output EVERY step in the row PLUS every prompt-named step (ADDED, never SUBSTITUTED: two named steps on Windows Service → FIVE steps; restoring only the defining step is not enough).

| Project type | Template steps that MUST ALL be present |
| --- | --- |
| Script | Hello World |
| IIS | Deploy to IIS, Print Message when No Targets, Scan for Vulnerabilities |
| Windows Service | Deploy a Windows Service, Print Message when No Targets, Scan for Vulnerabilities |
| Terraform | Plan to apply a Terraform template, Approve Plan, Apply a Terraform template |
| Kubernetes | Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check Targets Available, Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML, Scan for Vulnerabilities |
| Kubernetes LLM | Approve Production Deployment, Deploy a Kubernetes LLM Web App via YAML, Smoke Test, Print Message When no Targets, Scan for Vulnerabilities |
| Tomcat | Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check Targets Available, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Deploy to Tomcat via Manager, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Deployment Success Notification |
| Azure Web App | Manual Intervention Required, Validate setup, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Deploy Azure Web App Container, Smoke test, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Send deployment failure notification |
| Azure Function | Manual Intervention Required, Validate setup, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Deploy the Function App, Smoke test, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Send deployment failure notification |
| AWS Lambda | Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Attempt Login, Validate Setup, Upload Lambda, Deploy Lambda SAM template, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Deployment Success Notification |
| Argo CD Modify/Update Manifest | Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Octopus - Check for Argo CD Instances, Update Argo CD Application Manifests, Link to repo, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Send an Email on Deployment Failed |
| Argo CD Image Tag Update | Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check for Argo CD Instances, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Update Argo CD Application Image Tags, Scan for Vulnerabilities, Send an Email (Deployment failed) |
| VM Blue/Green | Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment, Approve Production Deployment, Octopus - Check Targets Available, Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured, Transfer a Package, Block Release Progression, Send an Email on Success |
| Progressive / canary / wave | Deploy App, Simulate Failure, Run Octopus Deploy Runbook, Sleep |
| Orchestration | Run a Script, Deploy a Release |
| Claude | List Commits, Run Claude Agent, Extract JSON, Manual Intervention Required, Perform Deployment |

* Self-check: for EACH name in your row confirm a matching `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` (same role; one step can't satisfy two entries). Same for template environments (e.g. IIS: `Security`, `DevSecOps`), variables and scheduled trigger.

* **CRITICAL:** "with no steps" in a project creation line is a **template placeholder** always present in migration prompts. Any following "Add a step" instructions take **absolute precedence** — create ALL of them. Take it literally only with **zero** "Add a step" instructions.

**CRITICAL: project names, step names and step notes/descriptions from the prompt are identifiers — reproduce verbatim.** Words like `api`, `key`, `token`, `service`, `auth`, `credential` do NOT make them sensitive; never omit, shorten or replace any part with `*` or a placeholder (e.g. keep `"[PROD] api-syncer canary"`, `"Deploy api-syncer"`, `"Scale Down Canary"`, and `api-syncer` in `"Original stage type: undoRolloutManifest. This step rolls back deployment/api-syncer to the previous revision in namespace app-0004-prod."`).
* Self-check: scan all `name`, `notes`, `description` and `execution_properties` values (esp. `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` — kubectl namespaces, resource names, label selectors are never redacted) for `*****`; replace any not literally `*****` in the prompt with the verbatim original.
## Step Execution Order and Parallel Steps

* `Set the start trigger to "Run in parallel with the previous step"` → the `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` MUST have `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`.
* **CRITICAL:** the parallel instruction counts in ANY wording, not just the literal annotation — all of these MUST produce `StartWithPrevious`:
  * "and must run in parallel with the previous step"
  * "this step runs in parallel with the previous step"
  * "run this at the same time as the previous step"
  * "run concurrently with <the previous step's name>"
  * "this step and <previous step name> run together / simultaneously / side by side"
  A numbered list item saying "…, and must run in parallel with the previous step" is as binding as the annotated form. Do NOT require the exact phrase `Set the start trigger to ...`; leaving it `StartAfterPrevious` makes the process sequential and is always wrong.
* Converse wordings mean `StartAfterPrevious` (a convergence step): "must wait for all previous steps to complete before it starts", "wait for all previous steps to complete, then start", "only after the previous steps finish".
* Self-check: search every step description for "parallel", "concurrently", "at the same time", "simultaneously", "together"; each step using one about the preceding step has `StartWithPrevious` and is consecutive with it in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`.
* `Set the start trigger to "Wait for all previous steps to complete, then start"` → `start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"`, set explicitly even though it equals the default.
* No start trigger annotation (and no parallel wording above) → default `start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"`.
* `The step must be disabled.` → keep the step in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` and keep any explicit `start_trigger` annotation; disabled steps still participate in ordering.
* **MANDATORY:** every `octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` of a project MUST appear in its `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` `steps` list EXACTLY ONCE (missing steps are silently not executed; duplicates run twice). List length must equal the step resource count; add missing ones in correct topological order, remove duplicates.

**CRITICAL — two "Add a step" instructions with identical names** (e.g. two "Deploy Service-A"): separate resources with distinct labels (e.g. `process_step_..._deploy_service_a` and `process_step_..._deploy_service_a_2`), and the `name` attribute must also be unique — append the same suffix:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_...deploy_service_a" {
  name = "Deploy Service-A"  ← CORRECT: matches first prompt instruction verbatim
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_...deploy_service_a_2" {
  name = "Deploy Service-A 2"  ← CORRECT: both name and label are unique
}
```

**CRITICAL — two "Create a/an <resource>" instructions asking for an IDENTICALLY-NAMED account, environment, tenant, feed, lifecycle, project group, tag set, certificate, library variable set, worker pool, or project.** Unlike step names (project-scoped, Terraform-only), these resource types have a space-wide unique-name constraint enforced by the Octopus SERVER itself: emitting two `resource` blocks with the same `name` passes `tofu plan` but fails at `apply` with `Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [This account name has already been used. Please choose a different name.]` (observed for two `octopusdeploy_token_account` resources both named "GitHub Bot"). Apply the SAME fix as the step-name rule above: append ` 2`, ` 3`, etc. to the `name` attribute of the second and subsequent resources (never to the first), keep distinct resource labels (`account_github_bot`, `account_github_bot_2`), and note the rename in the project description or the resource's own `notes`/`description` field if it has one — never emit two resources of one of these types with an identical `name`.

**CRITICAL — parallel groups and convergence:**
* Evaluate each step's `start_trigger` independently, relative to the step immediately before it in the ordered list. A "Run in parallel" step is `StartWithPrevious` even if the step before it is `StartAfterPrevious` (e.g. `Deploy -canary-` "Wait for all previous steps..." then `Rollback -internal-` "Run in parallel..." → `deploy_canary` = `StartAfterPrevious`, `rollback_internal` = `StartWithPrevious`).
* The first step of a group (and the first step of the project) is `StartAfterPrevious` ("start immediately" for the first project step — never change it to `StartWithPrevious`).
* EVERY later member, including the LAST one — whether the group has 2, 3, 7 or more members — is `StartWithPrevious`. The last member is NOT a convergence point; only the step AFTER the group (annotated "Wait for all previous steps") is. Mistaking the second or last member for the entry/convergence step is the most common error.
* Convergence step (after the group) is `StartAfterPrevious`, never `StartWithPrevious`.
* **CRITICAL:** `is_disabled` and `start_trigger` are independent. `The step must be disabled. Set the start trigger to "Run in parallel with the previous step"` → both `is_disabled = true` AND `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`. Never change a trigger because a step is disabled (re-enabling must not break the order).

Example — prompt: `Deploy Service-A` "Wait for all previous steps to complete, then start"; `Deploy Service-B` … `Deploy Service-G` each "Run in parallel with the previous step" (G is the last of 7); `Final Approval` "Wait for all previous steps to complete, then start":
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_deploy_service_a" {
  start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"  ← entry into 7-step parallel group
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_deploy_service_b" {
  start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"  ← 2nd parallel step
}
# ... service_c to service_f: StartWithPrevious ...
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_deploy_service_g" {
  start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"  ← CORRECT: 7th parallel step STILL uses StartWithPrevious
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_final_approval" {
  start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"  ← CORRECT: convergence step AFTER all 7 parallel steps
}
```
Disabled parallel member:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_rollback_canary" {
  name          = "Rollback -canary-"
  is_disabled   = true
  start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"  ← CORRECT: disabled but still parallel with previous step
  # ... other attributes ...
}
```

Self-checks:
* Every step with `"Run in parallel with the previous step"` uses `StartWithPrevious`; every step with `"Wait for all previous steps to complete, then start"` uses `StartAfterPrevious`.
* Count of steps annotated `"Run in parallel with the previous step"` MUST equal the count of `octopusdeploy_process_step` resources with `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`; a mismatch means a parallel step wrongly uses `StartAfterPrevious`.

**CRITICAL — `steps` list ordering in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`:**
* Parallel members are CONSECUTIVE: each `StartWithPrevious` step sits EXACTLY one slot after the step it runs in parallel with; no step from another group or later phase in between. Group {A, B} (B parallel with A) where A has continuation steps C1, C2 → `[..., A, B, C1, C2, ...]`; `[..., A, C1, B, C2, ...]` is FORBIDDEN (e.g. a leaf sibling B placed after A's continuations). A = `StartAfterPrevious`, B = `StartWithPrevious`, C1 = `StartAfterPrevious` (waits for BOTH A and B).
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" "..." {
  steps = [
    octopusdeploy_process_step.step_a.id,       # A: first in {A, B} group
    octopusdeploy_process_step.step_b.id,       # CORRECT: B immediately after A
    octopusdeploy_process_step.step_c1.id,      # C1: first continuation, converges after BOTH A and B
    octopusdeploy_process_step.step_c2.id,
  ]
}
```
* A convergence step following group {A, B, C} goes at position N+1, N = position of the LAST member; no member may appear after it. All `StartWithPrevious` steps must be in the list, each right after its partner.
* Sequentially linearized branches (A1→A2→A3, then B1→B2→B3): all of Branch A appears CONTIGUOUSLY and IN ORDER before ANY of Branch B. Most common error: B1 placed between A1 and A2. Self-check: for every pair where Y is "sequential after" X in the same branch chain in the prompt (no "Run in parallel" annotation, only "Wait for all previous steps" or none), X has a lower index; if not, reorder (with any same-branch steps between them).
* Multi-phase projects (Phase 1 {A, B, C} → convergence D → Phase 2 {E, F} → convergence G): all Phase 1 members before D; all Phase 2 members after D and before G; none crossing D. A Phase 1 step after Phase 2's convergence is a critical error.

**CRITICAL — `notes` as a parallel signal:** if a step's `notes` (its prompt description) contains "runs in parallel with the deploy step", "runs in parallel with", or "parallel with the previous step", set `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"` even if the annotation was omitted. Notes and trigger must agree. Self-check: any step whose `notes` contains "runs in parallel" but has `StartAfterPrevious` is a definite error — fix it.

**CRITICAL:** the verbatim `notes` rule applies equally to `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`: `Set the step description to "..."` on a community step template step → `notes = "<verbatim description text>"`; do NOT truncate, paraphrase, or omit migration NOTEs.

## Process Step Terraform Resource Creation Order

* Step resources (`octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`, same pattern) MUST NOT be created with uncontrolled parallelism (API rate limits/race conditions). Each step MUST have `depends_on` pointing to the IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING step resource in the process order.
* The first step needs no `depends_on` (its `process_id` already waits for `octopusdeploy_process`).
* Parallel groups (`start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`): all members may share `depends_on` on the last sequential step before the group (the group's "entry step"); they need not depend on each other.
* **CRITICAL:** a sequential step's `depends_on` lists ONLY the single immediately preceding step, never all previous steps.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_step_1" {
  name       = "Step 1"
  process_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"
  # ... other attributes ...
}

resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_step_2" {
  name       = "Step 2"
  process_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_step_1]
  # ... other attributes ...
}
```

WRONG (lists all previous steps):
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_step_4" {
  name       = "Step 4"
  depends_on = [
    octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_step_1,  ← WRONG: not the immediately preceding step
    octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_step_2,  ← WRONG: not the immediately preceding step
    octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_step_3,  ← correct: immediately preceding step
  ]
}
```

## Inline Kubernetes YAML Indentation

* YAML in `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` (in `execution_properties`) MUST use 2-space indentation at every level. Never embed flat YAML (all keys at column 0) in any `execution_properties` value.
* **CRITICAL:** If the prompt's YAML is already flat, re-indent it with proper nesting before embedding; flat source is no excuse. If you cannot reconstruct indentation correctly (or cannot serialize cached manifest YAML with both correct indentation and verbatim values), use a TODO placeholder instead of malformed or partially redacted YAML. For a flat Deployment+HPA or any multi-document manifest you cannot re-indent, use `# TODO: replace with correctly indented manifest` and set `is_disabled = true` on the step.
* **CRITICAL:** Manifests MUST use `metadata.name`, NOT `metadata.generateName` (the API rejects it: "The provided YAML is not formatted correctly, or is missing a 'kind' or 'metadata.name' field."). Replace `generateName` with a static descriptive lowercase-hyphenated `name` (e.g. `generateName: shift-traffic-` → `name: shift-traffic`), even if the prompt says to preserve the field verbatim.
* Keys under `data:` / `stringData:` are indented two spaces beneath the parent, never at column 0.
* Lists (`containers`, `env`, `envFrom`, `volumeMounts`, `ports`, `args`, `imagePullSecrets`, `tolerations`, `volumes`): the list key is at its schema depth, the `-` is indented beneath its parent key, and item child fields are 2 spaces deeper than the dash.
* Multi-document YAML: keep each separator line of exactly `---`; each new document restarts at column 0 with nested keys still indented.
* Re-indentation algorithm: place keys by Kubernetes schema depth (column = 2 × depth); verify every key is at a deeper column than its parent.

**MANDATORY CONTAINERS COLUMN CHECK** (Deployment): `containers:` must be at **8 spaces in the rendered YAML value** (`spec` at 2 → `template` at 4 → inner `spec` at 6 → `containers:` at 8); with an indented `<<-EOT` heredoc the Terraform source may show extra leading spaces before stripping. A `containers:` line at column 0 means the whole pod-spec is flat — always wrong; re-indent or use the TODO placeholder.

**Deployment depth table** (spaces):
* `apiVersion`, `kind`, `metadata`, `spec` → 0
* `metadata.name`, `metadata.namespace`, `metadata.labels`, `annotations`; `spec.replicas`, `spec.selector`, `spec.template` → 2
* `spec.template.metadata`, `spec.template.spec` → 4
* `spec.template.spec.containers` (list key), `spec.template.spec.containers[-]` (dash), `spec.template.spec.tolerations`, `spec.template.spec.tolerations[-]`, `spec.template.spec.nodeSelector`, `spec.template.spec.imagePullSecrets`, `spec.template.spec.imagePullSecrets[-]`, `spec.template.spec.volumes`, `spec.template.spec.volumes[-]` → 6
* `spec.template.spec.containers[0].name`, `.image`, `.env`, `.envFrom`, `.volumeMounts`, `.resources`, `.ports`, `.livenessProbe`, `.readinessProbe`; `spec.template.spec.containers[0].env[-]`, `spec.template.spec.containers[0].envFrom[-]`, `spec.template.spec.containers[0].volumeMounts[-]` (dashes); `spec.template.spec.tolerations[0].key`, `.operator`, `.effect`, `.tolerationSeconds`; `spec.template.spec.nodeSelector.cloud.google.com/gke-nodepool`; `spec.template.spec.imagePullSecrets[0].name`; `spec.template.spec.volumes[0].name`, `.secret`, `.configMap` → 8
* `spec.template.spec.containers[0].env[0].name`, `.value`; `spec.template.spec.containers[0].envFrom[0].secretRef`, `.configMapRef`; `spec.template.spec.containers[0].volumeMounts[0].name`, `.mountPath`, `.subPath`; `spec.template.spec.volumes[0].secret.secretName` → 10
* `spec.template.spec.containers[0].envFrom[0].secretRef.name` → 12

`tolerations`, `nodeSelector`, `imagePullSecrets`, `volumes` are children of `spec.template.spec` (alongside `containers`), NOT of the container. CORRECT example (flat prompt YAML re-indented):
```hcl
"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml" = <<-EOT
  apiVersion: apps/v1
  kind: Deployment
  metadata:
    name: worker
    namespace: app-0117-dev
  spec:
    replicas: 2
    template:
      spec:
        containers:
          - name: worker
            image: registry.example.invalid/image-0250
            envFrom:
              - secretRef:
                  name: worker-secrets
            volumeMounts:
              - name: config-volume
                mountPath: /config
        tolerations:
          - key: dedicated
            operator: Equal
            value: worker
            effect: NoSchedule
        nodeSelector:
          cloud.google.com/gke-nodepool: worker-pool
        imagePullSecrets:
          - name: registry-creds
        volumes:
          - name: config-volume
            configMap:
              name: worker-config
  EOT
```

**CronJob depths** (extra `jobTemplate` level): `spec.schedule`, `spec.concurrencyPolicy`, `spec.successfulJobsHistoryLimit` etc., `spec.jobTemplate` → 2; `spec.jobTemplate.spec` → 4; `spec.jobTemplate.spec.template` → 6; `spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec` → 8; its `containers` key and dash → 10; container fields (`name`, `env`, env dash) → 12; env item fields → 14.
```hcl
"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml" = <<-EOT
  apiVersion: batch/v1
  kind: CronJob
  metadata:
    name: my-cronjob
    namespace: app-prod
  spec:
    schedule: "0 2 * * *"
    jobTemplate:
      spec:
        template:
          spec:
            containers:
              - name: my-task
                image: gcr.io/my-org/my-task:latest
                env:
                  - name: ENV_VAR
                    value: "production"
            restartPolicy: Never
  EOT
```

**Job depths** (`batch/v1 Job`, common for canary traffic-shifting, restore, derive-baseline, derive-canary): same `spec.template.spec` nesting as Deployment, no `spec.selector`/`spec.replicas`. `spec.backoffLimit`, `spec.completions`, `spec.parallelism`, `spec.template` → 2; `spec.template.spec.containers` (and dash), `spec.template.spec.restartPolicy`, `spec.template.spec.serviceAccountName`, `spec.template.spec.imagePullSecrets` → 6; container fields `.name`, `.image`, `.command`, `.env`, `.envFrom` and `command`/`env` dashes → 8; env item `.name`, `.value`, `.valueFrom` → 10; `valueFrom.fieldRef` → 12; `fieldRef.fieldPath` → 14.

**MANDATORY JOB COLUMN CHECK**: for `kind: Job`, `spec.template.spec.containers:` must be at exactly 6 spaces (0, 2 or 4 is wrong).
```hcl
"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml" = <<-EOT
  apiVersion: batch/v1
  kind: Job
  metadata:
    name: shift-traffic
    namespace: app-prod
  spec:
    backoffLimit: 0
    template:
      spec:
        containers:
          - command:
              - shift-traffic
              - -n
              - $(NAMESPACE)
            env:
              - name: NAMESPACE
                value: app-prod
              - name: CJ_NAMESPACE
                valueFrom:
                  fieldRef:
                    fieldPath: metadata.namespace
            image: registry.example.invalid/image-0434
            name: shift-traffic
        imagePullSecrets:
          - name: my-pull-secret
        restartPolicy: Never
        serviceAccountName: spinnaker-custom-job
  EOT
```

## Octopus Variable Substitution in Kubernetes YAML Numeric and Boolean Fields

**CRITICAL:** Never quote an Octopus variable substitution in a numeric or boolean Kubernetes YAML field. `replicas: "#{Frontend.Replicas}"` renders `replicas: "2"`, which Kubernetes rejects at deploy time.
* Numeric (unquoted): `spec.replicas`, `spec.minReplicas`, `spec.maxReplicas`, `containerPort`, `port`, `targetPort`, `nodePort`, `replicas` inside a `HorizontalPodAutoscaler`, `revisionHistoryLimit`, `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, `initialDelaySeconds`, `periodSeconds`, `timeoutSeconds`, `failureThreshold`, `successThreshold`, `backoffLimit`, `completions`, `parallelism`, `runAsUser`.
* Boolean (unquoted): `readOnlyRootFilesystem`, `privileged`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation`, `runAsNonRoot`, `hostNetwork`, `automountServiceAccountToken`.
* String fields (`image`, `name`, label/annotation values) MAY be quoted, and SHOULD be when a `#{...}` substitution is followed by a colon (e.g. `image: "myrepo/myapp:#{Octopus.Action.Package[app].PackageVersion}"`).
* Literal numbers (`replicas: 2`) are also unquoted; quoting is only for string-typed fields.
Correct: `replicas: #{Frontend.Replicas}`, `- containerPort: #{Frontend.Port}`, `image: "octopussamples/octopub-frontend:#{Octopus.Action.Package[octopub-frontend].PackageVersion}"`.
Self-check: in any manifest in `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` or YAML in `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody`, search for `: "#{` and unquote every numeric/boolean hit.

## Step Name Character Rules

* Step names may only contain letters, numbers, spaces, periods, commas, dashes, underscores and hashes. Replace anything else with a dash — including `()` and `[]`. Square brackets hard-fail at the API (`'Zeta Bracket [Y]' contains invalid characters. Names can only contain letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores or hashes.`) — never relax this. Parentheses/`&` are accepted by the API (e.g. `Drain (Blue)`) but still replace parentheses (`Drain -Blue-`) because the Terraform provider validates step names more strictly.
* Use the prompt's dash-replaced form exactly (e.g. `Deploy -Manifest-`); never re-introduce parentheses and never convert dashes to underscores (`Manual Judgment -Canary-`, never `Manual Judgment _Canary_`).
* The API error message's character list is misleading (same text for step/project/environment names, which are validated differently) — don't infer the allowed set from it.

| Character | Step name | Project / environment / lifecycle / channel name |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `(` `)` | accepted by the API | accepted |
| `&` | accepted by the API | accepted |
| `[` `]` | **REJECTED — hard failure** | accepted |
| `/` | rejected | rejected |

**CRITICAL:** Project names: the forward slash `/` is the ONLY rejected character (e.g. `Name 'Charset Slash / Project' contains invalid characters.`). An invalid project name aborts the apply at the project resource, losing its process, steps, variables and runbooks.
* Replace `/` with a dash and change nothing else: `33. Telemetry / Ingest (EU)` → `33. Telemetry - Ingest (EU)`.
* Pass `()`, `[]`, `:`, `&`, `\` through verbatim (verified: `Charset Project (EU)`, `Charset Project [X]: Y & Z`, `Charset Backslash \ Project` all created). Never carry step-name restrictions to project names.
* The slash substitution is the ONE allowed deviation from the prompt's project name (see Project Uniqueness Instructions). Keep any dash-replaced form the prompt already uses.

**CRITICAL:** Step-name restrictions apply to STEP names ONLY — never to environments, lifecycles, channels, tenants, project groups, accounts, feeds, worker pools or any other resource. These follow the project rule: only `/` is rejected (verified: `Probe Paren (One)`, `Probe Amp & Three` created; `Probe Slash / Two` rejected); reproduce everything else character for character.
* Replace a slash with a **dash**, never an underscore. Converting punctuation to underscores is forbidden everywhere.

Self-check: compare the `name` of `octopusdeploy_project`, every `octopusdeploy_environment`, `octopusdeploy_lifecycle`, `octopusdeploy_channel`, `octopusdeploy_tenant`, `octopusdeploy_project_group`, and every lifecycle `phase` name character by character with the prompt — the only permitted difference is `/` → dash (no underscores where the prompt had a parenthesis, bracket, colon or ampersand). Check every step name against the step charset.

## Step Description Property

* `Set the step description to "..."` → set the top-level `notes` attribute of `octopusdeploy_process_step`, NEVER inside `execution_properties`. Never omit it when instructed.
* The value is the exact verbatim prompt string, including quoted sub-strings.
* `Set the step description to include: "..."`, append instructions, or multiple fragments → build ONE `notes` string with all fragments in prompt order, joined by a single space unless the prompt specifies different punctuation. Only one `notes` per resource; never drop a fragment.
* **CRITICAL:** Never add backslash-escaping or extra quotes around GCS paths/URLs (e.g. `gs://example-bucket/path/to/file.yaml`) in `notes`.
* **CRITICAL:** All `NOTE (migration): ...` text must appear verbatim (never dropped, shortened, rephrased, or replaced with a generic note), including:
  * notes about conditional branching/preconditions, e.g. `NOTE (migration): This step was originally on the SUCCESS branch after \"Manual Judgment\". In this migration, both branches now run in parallel.`
  * **CRITICAL:** on `undoRolloutManifest` steps: `NOTE (migration): This step was originally on the rollback/rejection branch of a pipeline. In this migration it runs in parallel with the deploy step — configure this step to run only when the deploy step is not needed, or disable it and trigger rollbacks manually.`
  * `stageTimeoutMs` on `manualJudgment` stages: `NOTE (migration): The original stage had a timeout of 30 minutes (stageTimeoutMs: 1800000). Configure a Manual Intervention timeout in Octopus if required.`
  * `stageTimeoutMs` on non-`manualJudgment` stages (e.g. `deployManifest`, `runJobManifest`): `NOTE (migration): The original stage had a timeout of <N> minutes (stageTimeoutMs: <value>). Configure a step timeout in Octopus if required.`
  * fork-without-reconvergence: `NOTE (migration): In the original Spinnaker pipeline, this step depended only on "<Branch A root stage name>" and did not wait for "<Branch B root stage(s)>". In this Octopus migration, this step implicitly waits for all preceding parallel steps including the Branch B stages.`
  * `stageEnabled.expression` conditions with no Octopus equivalent — including any suggestion about Octopus Variable run conditions or parameter-based expressions.
* **CRITICAL:** Multiple `NOTE (migration):` fragments (e.g. `stageEnabled.expression` + `stageTimeoutMs` + branch linearization) are all concatenated into the single `notes`; never emit only the first, never create multiple `notes`, never omit one as redundant.

Correct: `notes = "This step originally loaded its manifest from Google Cloud Storage at gs://example-bucket/path. The manifest must be inlined or reconfigured."` as a top-level attribute. WRONG: `notes = "... at \"gs://example-bucket/storage-3209\". ..."` (escaped quotes added); `"notes" = ...` inside `execution_properties`.

## Wait Steps (Script Steps with Start-Sleep)

* A `wait` stage converted to `Start-Sleep -Seconds <N>` → `octopusdeploy_process_step` of type `"Octopus.Script"` (no other type) with `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" = "Start-Sleep -Seconds <N>"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "PowerShell"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`.
* Step name = the prompt's dash-replaced form (e.g. `"Wait -15min-"`).
* **CRITICAL:** Preserve Octostache (`#{...}`) expressions verbatim — never evaluate them (runtime, not Terraform time): `Start-Sleep -Seconds (#{waitMinutes} * 60)` stays `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" = "Start-Sleep -Seconds (#{waitMinutes} * 60)"`, NOT `"Start-Sleep -Seconds 900"`.

## "Review Template-Derived Pipeline Behavior" Script Step

* When the prompt includes `Add a "Run a Script" step with the name "Review template-derived pipeline behavior"`, create an `"Octopus.Script"` `octopusdeploy_process_step` whose `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"` is the prompt's inline PowerShell comment (e.g. `# TODO: expand the pipeline template "pipeline://basic-gcs" using the templatedPipeline variables before considering this conversion complete.`), with `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "PowerShell"` and `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`.
* No `properties` block with `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` — it runs on the server.
* It MUST be the LAST entry in the `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` `steps` array.

* Also set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`; standard `condition = "Success"`, `package_requirement = "LetOctopusDecide"`, `start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"`.

## Multiple Description Fragments in `notes` (CRITICAL)

Concatenate ALL fragments in order, space-separated (dropping one is WRONG). E.g. `Set the step description to "Original stage name: Review and Approval."` + `Append to the step description: "NOTE (migration): ..."` →
`notes = "Original stage name: Review and Approval. NOTE (migration): The original stage had a timeout of 30 minutes (stageTimeoutMs: 1800000). Configure a Manual Intervention timeout in Octopus if required."`

## kubectl Script Steps (KubernetesRunScript)

* `"Octopus.KubernetesRunScript"` steps (from `deleteManifest`, `scaleManifest`, and `undoRolloutManifest` stage types) MUST use `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "Bash"`, never `"PowerShell"`.
* `execution_properties` must include: `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`, `"OctopusUseBundledTooling" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "Bash"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"` (the inline Bash kubectl command), and `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.Namespace"` only per the namespace rule below.
* `properties` must include `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"` set to the Kubernetes target tag.
* **CRITICAL:** Never add the three "Mock K8s" compatibility properties — `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait" = "NoWait"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled" = "False"` — to an `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` step; they apply to `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` steps ONLY. Read "all Kubernetes steps" in a prompt or general instruction as "all raw-YAML steps". The step still requires the "Mock K8s" target.
* **CRITICAL:** `undoRolloutManifest` steps (`Add a "Run a kubectl script" step` with e.g. `kubectl rollout undo deployment/my-deployment -n my-namespace`) use the SAME type `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` and Bash syntax as `deleteManifest`/`scaleManifest` — NOT `Octopus.Script`.
* **CRITICAL:** Set `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.Namespace"` ONLY when the prompt explicitly says `Set the step namespace to "<namespace>"` (e.g. `Set the step namespace to "my-namespace"` → `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.Namespace" = "my-namespace"`). If the namespace appears only in the kubectl `-n <namespace>` argument, or no namespace is specified, omit the property entirely (never empty string or null).
* No `container` block unless the prompt explicitly requests a specific container image.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_rollback_internal" {
  name                 = "Rollback -internal-"
  type                 = "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript"
  process_id           = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_myproject.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_myproject[0].id}"
  condition            = "Success"
  package_requirement  = "LetOctopusDecide"
  start_trigger        = "StartAfterPrevious"
  properties           = {
    "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" = "Kubernetes"
  }
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"                    = "true"
    "OctopusUseBundledTooling"                      = "False"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"            = "Inline"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax"                  = "Bash"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"              = "kubectl rollout undo deployment/dmp-market-web-internal -n app-0112-prod"
  }
}
```

## Container Block Rules

* **CRITICAL:** `container = { feed_id = "", image = "" }` is NEVER valid and MUST NEVER be generated. If a step does not use a container, omit `container` entirely. **The default is to omit it**; emit one only when the prompt explicitly asks for the step to run inside a named container image — never invent one for "a step that binds a certificate", "a deployment step", or similar.
* **CRITICAL:** `Octopus.Manual` (manual intervention) steps MUST NEVER include a `container` attribute.
* `container` is ONLY valid for: `Octopus.Script`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.TerraformPlan`, `Octopus.TerraformApply`, `Octopus.TerraformDestroy`, `Octopus.TerraformPlanDestroy`, `Octopus.AzurePowerShell`, `Octopus.AwsRunScript`, `Octopus.GoogleCloudScripting`, `Octopus.AzureWebApp`, and `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` (only when a specific image is requested).
* **CRITICAL:** A `container` block that sets `image` MUST also set a non-empty `feed_id` referencing a Docker registry feed you create or look up — never blank/null/absent, never the Octopus Server built-in feed (it cannot serve container images). Otherwise the API rejects the step (`[A step that is configured to run inside a container, must have a container feed id if there is a container image]`), the apply aborts and **no project is created at all**.

Self-check: every `container` block in every deployment process and runbook step has BOTH `image` and `feed_id` non-empty, or is absent.

## Webhook / HTTP Script Steps

* When the prompt says `Add a "Run a Script" step` with inline Bash containing a `curl` command (a Spinnaker `webhook` stage), the `Octopus.Script` step MUST use `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "Bash"`, never `"PowerShell"` when the `ScriptBody` contains `curl`.
* `execution_properties`: `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" = "Bash"`, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"` = the inline Bash `curl` command preserving all `-H`, `-d`, and `-X` flags exactly.
* Leave `worker_pool_id` and `worker_pool_variable` unset (or `null`) — server-side steps use no worker pool.

## Default Lifecycle Instructions

* Reference the Default Lifecycle as "data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id", e.g. `lifecycle_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id}"`. The resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_default_lifecycle" does not exist — never reference it.
* "quantity_to_keep" in a "release_retention_with_strategy" or "tentacle_retention_with_strategy" block of an "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" resource must be larger than 0.
* **CRITICAL:** `strategy` is REQUIRED in both `release_retention_with_strategy` and `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` blocks — always include `strategy = "Count"`.
* Never define the "phase" block on a single line; each property of the "phase" block must be on a new line.

## Optional Lifecycle Phase Instructions

**CRITICAL:** Preserve `is_optional_phase` exactly as in the example configuration: where a `phase` sets `is_optional_phase = true`, your phase MUST set `is_optional_phase = true` (never omit it — a missing value defaults to `false`/mandatory — and never set false).

**CRITICAL:** Lifecycles modelling parallel production stacks (e.g. blue/green with "Blue Production" and "Green Production") MUST mark each stack's phase `is_optional_phase = true`.

**CRITICAL:** Every lifecycle MUST contain at least one phase that is NOT optional; otherwise Octopus rejects it (`[Lifecycle 'X' must have at least one phase that is not optional.]`), the apply aborts and **no project is created at all**.
* A blue/green prompt asking only for "a lifecycle containing the production environments" still needs a mandatory pre-production phase: emit it first (e.g. `Development`, or `Development` and `Test`, with `is_optional_phase = false`), then the optional stack phases. Only if the lifecycle genuinely cannot have a pre-production phase, mark exactly ONE stack phase non-optional.
* **CRITICAL:** This applies to EVERY lifecycle; the one that usually breaks is a secondary lifecycle named without phases, typically behind a channel (e.g. `Add a channel called "Hotfix Channel" using a new lifecycle called "Gateway Hotfix"`), whose phases get copied from the optional blue/green stacks.
* **When the prompt names a lifecycle but does not describe its phases, do NOT copy another lifecycle's phase shape.** Use one `phase` per environment in the project's environment list, each `is_optional_phase = false`.
* `is_optional_phase = true` is justified ONLY by parallel production stacks (blue/green) or a phase the prompt explicitly calls optional (or the project type template/example configuration). Otherwise write `is_optional_phase = false`.

Self-check: for EACH `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` (project's, every channel's, any other named), not just the first: if the number of `phase` blocks equals the number with `is_optional_phase = true`, fix it (set one phase false or add a non-optional pre-production phase).

```hcl
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_blue_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_blue_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_blue_production[0].id}"]
  name                                  = "Blue Production"
  is_optional_phase                     = true
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
}
# "Green Production" phase identical, using environment_green_production
```

## Lifecycle Phase `minimum_environments_before_promotion` Instructions

`minimum_environments_before_promotion` counts environments *within its own phase* (entries in `automatic_deployment_targets` + `optional_deployment_targets`) that must be deployed to before promotion. `0` (the default) means "all environments in the phase" and is always valid.

* **CRITICAL:** When the prompt gives a valid threshold counting environments in the phase (≤ the phase's environment count), you MUST set it. E.g.:
  * "where a release may be promoted after 2 environments in the phase have been deployed to" on a 2-environment phase → `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 2`
  * "releases must be promoted only after 1 environment has been deployed to" → `= 1`
  * "a release may be promoted once N of this phase's environments have been deployed to" → `= N`, clamped to the phase's environment count
* **CRITICAL (authoritative, overrides the above):** A number counting *deployments*, *releases*, *successful runs*, *promotions*, or deployments to a DIFFERENT phase's environment ("after N successful deployments", "after N successful releases", "once N deployments have completed", "promote to Staging after 3 successful deployments to Test") is NOT a threshold — Octopus has no such feature. Ignore N and set `0`. Such a phrase never sets a value on the phase being promoted to.
* **CRITICAL:** The value MUST NOT exceed the phase's environment count; if it would, write `0` ("clamped" is mandatory). A one-environment phase (almost every phase) can only be `0` or `1`. Exceeding it makes the API reject the lifecycle (`The phase "X" requires a minimum of 2 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have been added`), the apply aborts and **no project is created at all**.
* E.g. `Create a lifecycle called "Insight Lifecycle" with phases "Development", "Test", and "Production", where the "Test" phase automatically promotes releases after 2 successful deployments to "Development"` → `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` on every phase (never `2` on "Test", the phase named last).
* The "omit it if unsure" guidance elsewhere applies ONLY to `release_retention_with_strategy` / `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` — never to `minimum_environments_before_promotion`, `is_optional_phase`, other phase attributes, or a certificate the prompt asked for.

Self-check, per phase, IN ORDER: (1) what does each number count? Not environments inside this phase → `0`, discard. (2) Surviving number > phase environment count → `0`.

## Retention Policy for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL — when a new project creation prompt specifies a retention policy (e.g., "with a retention policy of 30 days for releases" or "Set the retention policy to 5 items")**:

1. If the project uses the **Default Lifecycle** (data source only, cannot be modified by Terraform), ignore the retention instruction entirely — no `release_retention_with_strategy` or `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` blocks. This is the ONLY case where dropping it is correct.
2. Otherwise apply it to the project's `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource's `release_retention_with_strategy` block: "N days" → `unit = "Days"`, `quantity_to_keep = N`; "N items" or "keep N releases" → `unit = "Items"`, `quantity_to_keep = N`. Never default to 30 Days when the prompt says items ("Set the retention policy to 5 items" → `quantity_to_keep = 5`, `unit = "Items"`).
3. **NEVER add a `retention_policy` block to an `octopusdeploy_project` resource** — always forbidden; retention belongs to the LIFECYCLE.

* **CRITICAL:** Custom lifecycles include TEMPLATE-DEFAULT lifecycles (`Application`, `DevSecOps`, `Progressive`, `Blue Green`) and any lifecycle the prompt named — all are `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resources and MUST receive the retention instruction. If several projects share a lifecycle and retention is requested for one, write it once onto the shared lifecycle — no duplicate lifecycle, don't drop it.
* **CRITICAL:** "The project's lifecycle" is the one in `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id`, NOT a channel's lifecycle (e.g. with `Set the retention policy of the project's lifecycle to keep 17 releases and 34 days of files` plus `a channel called "HighRes" that uses a new lifecycle called "HighRes Lifecycle"`, the figures go on the project's `DevSecOps` lifecycle, not `HighRes Lifecycle`).
* **Do NOT invent retention values** for a lifecycle the prompt said nothing about; keep the example configuration's values (never e.g. `5 Days`).
* **CRITICAL:** Two-part prompts ("retain N releases and M days of files", "keep N releases and M days of packages", "retention of N releases and M days") set two policies INDEPENDENTLY:
  * "N releases" (or "N items") → `release_retention_with_strategy` with `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = N`, `unit = "Items"`.
  * "M days of files" (or "of packages", "of artifacts") → `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` with `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = M`, `unit = "Days"`.
  * Never apply one unit to both, even when N = M ("keep 7 releases and 7 days of files" → `7 Items` release, `7 Days` tentacle). "releases" always means `Items`; only explicit "days" means `Days`.

Self-check: prompt retention figures/units are on the lifecycle named by the project's `lifecycle_id`; other lifecycles keep example-configuration retention; every retention block has `strategy = "Count"`.

A lifecycle missing `count` is not in canonical shape: the vertex in the error above is `octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_search_lifecycle` with no `[0]` index, whereas a correctly generated lifecycle always appears as `...[0]`.

**This is the canonical `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` shape. Follow it exactly.**

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  partial_name = "My Lifecycle"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_my_lifecycle.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "My Lifecycle"

  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  tentacle_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  phase {
    name                                  = "Dev"
    optional_deployment_targets            = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}"]
    automatic_deployment_targets           = []
```
