```hcl
    is_optional_phase                      = false
    minimum_environments_before_promotion  = 0
  }

  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

**Lifecycle Selection for New Project Creation Prompts**:

* "Use the Default Lifecycle" → use ONLY `data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id` (data source with `partial_name = "Default Lifecycle"`) for `lifecycle_id`. **CRITICAL:** Do NOT create any `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, including the template's default lifecycle resource (e.g. `lifecycle_application` for Terraform templates, `lifecycle_devsecops` for Kubernetes templates).
* "Use the [Name] lifecycle" for any non-Default lifecycle → create BOTH a `data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles"` data source AND an `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource (canonical shape above, e.g. `lifecycle_security` for "Use the Security lifecycle").
* A lifecycle name other than "Default Lifecycle" gets phases corresponding to the environments mentioned in the prompt (e.g., "Development", "Test", "Production").

## Data Resources Instructions

Length-check formats for "data" resources (replace "<data name>" with the name of the data resource included in the output — it must match):
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.<data name>.project) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.<data name>.feeds) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.<data name>.worker_pools) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.<data name>.environments) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.<data name>.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.<data name>.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.<data name>.certificates) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_git_credentials.<data name>.git_credentials) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.<data name>.accounts) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_machine_policies.<data name>.machine_policies) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.<data name>.channels) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* "${length(data.octopusdeploy_deployment_targets.<data name>.deployment_targets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"

## Account Data Instructions

* "account_type" in a "octopusdeploy_accounts" data source must be one of (exact case), never empty: "AmazonWebServicesAccount", "AmazonWebServicesRoleAccount", "AmazonWebServicesOidcAccount", "AzureServicePrincipal", "AzureOIDC", "AzureSubscription", "GenericOidcAccount", "GoogleCloudAccount", "None", "SshKeyPair", "Token", "UsernamePassword".

```
data "octopusdeploy_accounts" "account_azure" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Azure"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  account_type = "AzureOIDC"
}
```

## Certificate Creation for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL:** If the prompt asks for (or merely names) a certificate (e.g. "create a certificate called 'Ledger Signing Cert'"), you MUST create the `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource. Missing real certificate data is NEVER a reason to skip it. Ignore any prompt details that set real certificate data.

**CRITICAL:** Never use real certificate data or a real password. Always `certificate_data = "placeholder"` and `password = "Change Me!"` — the exact case-sensitive literal string, never a value derived from, truncated from, or otherwise incorporating any PEM/PFX/base64 text the prompt supplied (even a fake-looking one). Octopus validates `certificate_data` and rejects anything that isn't a parseable certificate with `Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [Unable to parse certificate. Please check the certificate data and try again.]`; the literal word `placeholder` is the only value observed to be accepted.

* `resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "..." {` starts at the beginning of the line; its closing `}` is unindented on its own line.
* `certificate_data = "..."` and `password = "..."` MUST each be present as single-line plain-string attribute lines at the resource's top level. Never omit either (no `certificate_data` line always fails to parse). No nested block, heredoc, multi-line value, `variable` block, `local`, or `${var....}` reference (e.g. `certificate_data = "${var.something}"`; fails with "Unable to parse certificate").

**This is the ONLY shape an `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource may take.** Copy it exactly, changing only the resource label, the `name`, and the data source names:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_certificates" "certificate_my_cert" {
  partial_name = "My Cert"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "certificate_my_cert" {
  count            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name             = "My Cert"
  certificate_data = "placeholder"
  password         = "Change Me!"
  environments     = []
  tenant_tags      = []
  tenants          = []
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [certificate_data, password]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

**A `Certificate`-typed variable MUST point at a certificate you actually created**, with `value` in exactly this form:

```hcl
value = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates[0].id : octopusdeploy_certificate.certificate_my_cert[0].id}"
```

* NEVER emit a `Certificate`-typed `octopusdeploy_variable` with `value = ""` or no `value`.
* If you do not create the certificate, also drop the `Certificate`-typed variable and any step property referencing it. No dangling references.

Self-check (EVERY project type, incl. IIS, Tomcat, Azure Function, and Windows Service, whose examples have no certificate): each prompt-named certificate has a resource (and, if requested, a `Certificate`-typed variable using the ternary above) in the shape above, with no `${var.`.

## Binding an Octopus Certificate to an IIS HTTPS Site

**CRITICAL:** An HTTPS binding in `Octopus.Action.IISWebSite.Bindings` consumes a certificate through `certificateVariable`, NEVER through `thumbprint`.

* `certificateVariable` = NAME of a `Certificate`-typed project variable, a plain string such as `"Project.Certificate"`.
* `thumbprint` = SHA-1 thumbprint of a cert already in the machine store. Every `"thumbprint"` MUST be `null`.
* Never put a certificate ID (e.g. `"thumbprint" = "Certificates-588"`) in `thumbprint` (binding fails; certificate orphaned inside `jsonencode`, not exported). The template only shows an `http` binding with `thumbprint = null`.

Emit ALL THREE pieces:

```hcl
# 1. the certificate itself (see the canonical shape above)
resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "certificate_claims_tls" { ... }

# 2. a Certificate-typed project variable pointing at it
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "project_certificate_1" {
  count    = "..."
  owner_id = "..."
  name     = "Project.Certificate"
  type     = "Certificate"
  value    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_claims_tls.certificates) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_claims_tls.certificates[0].id : octopusdeploy_certificate.certificate_claims_tls[0].id}"
}
```
```hcl
# 3. the binding, naming that variable and leaving thumbprint null
"Octopus.Action.IISWebSite.Bindings" = jsonencode([
  {
    "certificateVariable" = "Project.Certificate"
    "requireSni"          = "False"
    "enabled"             = "True"
    "protocol"            = "https"
    "port"                = "443"
    "host"                = ""
    "thumbprint"          = null
  },
])
```

* Keep the template's existing `http` binding entry unless the prompt says to remove it; add the `https` entry alongside.
* `Octopus.Action.EnabledFeatures` needs no certificate feature; the binding is part of `Octopus.Features.IISWebSite`.
* Any step property naming a certificate: reference a `Certificate`-typed variable by name, never a `Certificates-NNN` id or a resource id interpolation inside a JSON string.
* Self-check: every certificate named in the prompt is referenced by at least one `Certificate`-typed variable; any non-null `thumbprint` (especially `Certificates-`) moves to such a variable named in `certificateVariable`.

## Data and Resource pairing instructions

When a “data” resource is referenced in a resource’s attributes, that data source must be included in the output, and the paired resource must be defined with `count = "${length(data.<type>.<label>.<collection>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`. Never reference a "octopusdeploy_environments" data source or "octopusdeploy_environment" resource (or any other) missing from the output.

* "lifecycle_id" -> data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_<data name>" (resource `octopusdeploy_lifecycle`).
* "project_group_id" -> data "octopusdeploy_project_groups" "project_group_<data name>" + resource "octopusdeploy_project_group" "project_group_<name>".
* "feed_id" (e.g. `feed_docker_hub`) -> data "octopusdeploy_feeds" with `feed_type    = "Docker"` + resource "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" with count "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* "environment_id" -> data "octopusdeploy_environments" "environment_<name>" + resource "octopusdeploy_environment" "environment_<name>".
* "project_id" -> data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_<name>" + resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_<name>" (count "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}").

Data source shape (add `feed_type` for feeds):

```
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_<data name>" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

## Resources Are Created, Never Updated

**FOUNDATIONAL:** Generated Terraform only CREATES resources; it never edits, updates, renames or reconfigures existing ones. Every resource has a `count` guard resolving to `0` when a match exists (deliberate; makes re-apply safe).

* A prompt asking to change an existing project (add a variable, change lifecycle/retention, rename/modify steps, link a library variable set) cannot be carried out; `count = 0` leaves it untouched. This is expected, not a defect.
* **NEVER remove, weaken or re-key a `count` guard to force a change** (no replacing a project-existence `count` with `count = 1`, no dropping `count`).
* **The ONE exception is runbooks.** A runbook may be added to an existing project together with its `octopusdeploy_process`, `octopusdeploy_process_step` resources and `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`, which must be guarded by a lookup for the RUNBOOK, not the project (a project guard resolves to `0`). Nothing else about the project may change in the same output (runbook + new project variable -> runbook only).
  * The runbook's `multi_tenancy_mode` must match the existing project's `tenanted_deployment_participation`, and its `environment_scope` must not narrow it to environments the prompt never named.
* Self-check: if any resource is meant to modify something existing and is not (part of) a runbook, remove the attempt, not the `count`.

## Count Properties Instructions

* `count` on every "octopusdeploy_process", "octopusdeploy_process_step", "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" (ALL, incl. Slack Start/Finish/Complete and community step templates), "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order", "octopusdeploy_process_child_step", "octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order" and "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" must be the same as the "octopusdeploy_project" resource's `count`. Never omit it.
* "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" `count` must be `"${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_<name>.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`, matching its `octopusdeploy_tenant`. It is NOT exempt and is often forgotten; check explicitly. Reference every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` as `...[0]`.
* "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" `count`: same as its `octopusdeploy_tenant_project`.
* Format: "${length(data.<data type>.<data resource>.<collection>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}". Never a ternary returning the same value for both cases, e.g. "${length(data.<data type>.<data resource>.<collection>) != 0 ? 1 : 1}".

**CRITICAL:** `octopusdeploy_process` MUST include `depends_on` listing any tag sets, tags, or other space-level resources it references (so they exist first); otherwise `depends_on` may be omitted or empty.

**CRITICAL:** Every `octopusdeploy_process` has the project `count` and a FULL ternary `project_id` (never `"${octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project.id}"`):

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_regions, octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_regions_tag_us_east]
}
```

Every `octopusdeploy_process_step` (and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`) has the project `count` and the ternary-null `process_id` `"${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_<name>[0].id}"`, never a direct reference like `"${octopusdeploy_process.process_foo.id}"` or `${octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project.id}`.

**CRITICAL:** Every `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger` MUST have: (1) project `count`, (2) ternary `project_id`, (3) ternary `channel_id`, (4) `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`, (5) `depends_on` referencing `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` (otherwise idempotent re-apply fails). Never direct references such as `"${octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_application.id}"`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "projecttrigger_my_project_external_feed_trigger" {
  # count and project_id: same as the octopusdeploy_process example above
  space_id   = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  name       = "External Feed Trigger"
  channel_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels[0].id : octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_application[0].id}"
  is_disabled = true   # when prompt says "The trigger must be disabled."
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_my_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

**CRITICAL:** A trigger `channel_id` MUST NEVER be a literal ID such as `"Channels-1"` (silently binds another space's channel).

* When the project has no named channel (only built-in `Default`), emit `data "octopusdeploy_channels" "channel_<project>_default"` (`ids          = []`, `partial_name = "Default"`, `project_id`, `skip         = 0`, `take         = 1`) and USE it (a common mistake is emitting it and still writing the literal), i.e. `data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_<project>_default.channels[0].id`: `channel_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_ledger_service_default.channels[0].id}"` (plain data-source reference, no ternary; there is no `octopusdeploy_channel` resource for `Default`).
* No literal Octopus IDs anywhere: `Channels-N`, `Projects-N`, `Environments-N`, `Feeds-N`, `Accounts-N`, `Lifecycles-N` are always resolved via a data source or resource reference.

**CRITICAL:** A "when a new release is created in project X" trigger belongs on the project that DEPLOYS, not on X. The trigger fires on new versions in a feed its own project consumes; an orchestration project consumes children via the built-in `Octopus Server Releases` feed.

* `project_id` = the orchestration project the prompt wants deployed; `channel_id` = a channel of that same project. Read it as "deploy THIS project when a release appears in THAT project"; the project named after "in" is the SOURCE and never owns the trigger (e.g. for `a project trigger that runs the deployment whenever a new release is created in "03a. Ledger Service"`, the trigger goes on the release train, not on `03a. Ledger Service`).

* Never define a "session_token" attribute on a "octopusdeploy_aws_account" resource.

## Tenant Instructions

**CRITICAL:** Every tenant-related data source needs its resource block (`octopusdeploy_tenant`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`, `octopusdeploy_tag_set`, `octopusdeploy_tag`); e.g. `data "octopusdeploy_tenants"` needs `resource "octopusdeploy_tenant"`.

* If the prompt says tenants are created/added, create "octopusdeploy_tenant" resources in addition to the "octopusdeploy_project" resource; never "octopusdeploy_tenant" without an associated "octopusdeploy_project".
* Each resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" has an associated data "octopusdeploy_tenants":

```
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_australian_office" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Australian Office"
  description = "An example tenant that represents an Australian office"
  tenant_tags = []
  depends_on  = []
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}

data "octopusdeploy_tenants" "tenant_australian_office" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Australian Office"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  project_id   = ""
  tags         = null
}
```

* When the prompt defines project tenant variable values, define the value for all environment unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When the prompt links a tenant to a project, include a "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" resource.
* Never define a "octopusdeploy_tags" data source (it does not exist).

## Tenanted Deployment Participation for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL:** Set `tenanted_deployment_participation` on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource:

- `"TenantedOrUntenanted"`: only when the prompt mentions untenanted deployments too ("TenantedOrUntenanted deployments", "both tenanted and untenanted", "tenanted and untenanted", "TenantedOrUntenanted").
- `"Tenanted"`: "tenanted deployments only", "require a tenant for deployment", "force tenants to be used", "Tenanted", "make the project tenanted", "the project is tenanted", "this is a tenanted project", "tenanted deployments". Creating/linking a tenant does NOT widen it ("make the project tenanted, create a tenant called 'X' and link it to the project" -> `"Tenanted"`).
- `"Untenanted"`: "untenanted deployments" or no mention of tenants (default). Never `tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"` when the prompt requests TenantedOrUntenanted or Tenanted.

**When the prompt names a specific tenant** (e.g., "Acme Corp tenant"), create ALL of: `octopusdeploy_tenant`; its paired `data "octopusdeploy_tenants"`; an `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` linking it to the project (with all lifecycle environments). Never a tenant without its `octopusdeploy_tenant_project`.

**Tenant tags** (e.g., "Regions/US-East tag"): create `octopusdeploy_tag_set` (e.g., "Regions") with paired `data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets"`; an `octopusdeploy_tag` per tag (e.g., "US-East") referencing the tag set; and set the tenant's `tenant_tags` as `"TagSetName/TagName"` (e.g., `"Regions/US-East"`). Never omit any of these.

**Example — prompt specifies "Acme Corp tenant with the Regions/US-East tenant tag"**:
```hcl
# plus the paired data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" "tagset_regions" (partial_name = "Regions")
resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" "tagset_regions" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "Regions"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tag" "tagset_regions_tag_us_east" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name       = "US-East"
  tag_set_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets[0].id : octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_regions[0].id}"
  color      = "#333333"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
# plus the paired data "octopusdeploy_tenants" "tenant_acme_corp" (partial_name = "Acme Corp")
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_acme_corp" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Acme Corp"
  tenant_tags = ["Regions/US-East"]
  depends_on  = [octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_regions_tag_us_east]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" "tenant_project_acme_corp_my_project" {
  count          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  tenant_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_acme_corp[0].id}"
  project_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  environment_ids = [
    "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}",
    # ... one entry per lifecycle environment (environment_production, ...)
  ]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

**CRITICAL:** Tenant names are unique in a space; a tenant mentioned more than once is ONE tenant.

* Same tenant named twice with different tags -> ONE `octopusdeploy_tenant` carrying ALL tags (union `tenant_tags` and `depends_on`). E.g. "Contoso" tagged Gold and later "Contoso" tagged Bronze gives one resource (not `tenant_contoso_gold` + `tenant_contoso_bronze`):

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_contoso" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_contoso.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Contoso"
  tenant_tags = ["Tier/Gold", "Tier/Bronze"]
  depends_on  = [octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_tier_tag_gold, octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_tier_tag_bronze]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

* Never rename one to avoid the collision (e.g. `Contoso` vs `Contoso Bronze`). One `data "octopusdeploy_tenants"` per distinct tenant name. Every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` and `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` for the merged tenant points at the single resource.

## Tenant Project Variable Instructions

**CRITICAL:** `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` REQUIRES a `template` block on the `octopusdeploy_project` (its `template_id` references it by index, 0 for the first). If the project has none (e.g. "Add tenant variables scoped to Production" on a project with no steps/templates), add one, e.g.:
```hcl
template {
  name              = "Tenant.Variable"
  label             = "A tenant variable"
  help_text         = "Define a tenant-specific value for this project."
  default_value     = null
  display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
 }
```

* Each `template` block defines ONE variable template (0-indexed). N requested tenant variables -> N `template` blocks (indexed 0 through N-1), or fewer if variables share the same template type. Never omit them.

**CRITICAL:** Every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` MUST set `environment_id` via the usual data-source/resource ternary (else plan fails: "Must specify either 'environment_id' or 'scope' block."). Don't invent a `scope` block unless certain of its schema.

* One value **per tenant per environment**: a single value without an environment (e.g. "set Wayne Enterprises to us-east-1") -> one resource per environment in that tenant's `octopusdeploy_tenant_project.environment_ids`, same value, names suffixed by environment (`tenantprojectvariable_wayne_enterprises_region_dev`, `..._qa`, `..._prod`).
* Each MUST have `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.<the tenant/project link>]`.

**CRITICAL:** `value` MUST be a non-empty literal from the prompt. With no value, do NOT create the resource (blank values fail at apply and abandon everything after).

* A template-only request ("Add a project tenant variable template called 'Bank.Sort.Code' of type single line text", "Add a tenant variable called 'X' of type single line text") is satisfied by the project `template` block alone; no `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`.
* Create one only when a value is stated for a named tenant ("set 'Bank.Sort.Code' to '20-00-00' for the tenant 'Contoso Bank'"); `value` is that literal, never `""` or an invented placeholder.
* **CRITICAL:** "Template only" means NO VALUE is given, regardless of the words "variable template". Read the whole sentence: `Add a project tenant variable template called "Tenant.Namespace" and set it to "contoso-ns" for Contoso and "fabrikam-ns" for Fabrikam` needs the template AND values. Expect (tenants x environments) resources, e.g. two tenants linked to three environments = SIX.
* This applies equally to `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`.
* Self-check: every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` has non-null `environment_id` and non-empty prompt-sourced `value` (else delete it, keep the `template`); every requested (tenant, template) pair has one resource per linked environment.

**Example** (template_id uses the ternary-null form):
```hcl
# octopusdeploy_project "project_my_project" holds the template blocks (template[0], template[1], ...)
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" "tenantprojectvariable_acme_corp_my_project" {
  count          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"
  # project_id, tenant_id: usual ternaries (as in tenant_project_acme_corp_my_project)
  template_id    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].template[0].id}"
  value          = "acme-production"
  depends_on     = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.tenant_project_acme_corp_my_project]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

For the second template use `...[0].template[1].id`, and so on. Templates may use other control types (e.g. `{ "Octopus.ControlType" = "Boolean" }`).

**Distinction**: `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` = per-project, per-tenant values for the `octopusdeploy_project` `template` blocks; `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable` = per-tenant values for a library variable set's `template` blocks (shared across projects).

**CRITICAL:** A "project tenant variable" ("Create a project tenant variable called 'X'", "Define the 'X' project tenant variable for each tenant", or otherwise attached to a project) MUST be a `template` block on the `octopusdeploy_project` plus `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` values. `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable` can never satisfy it (the project's `Templates` stay empty though apply succeeds).

* Use `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable` only when the prompt explicitly puts the template in a library variable set AND that `octopusdeploy_library_variable_set` with the matching `template` block is in the same output.
* **The project NAME is not an instruction.** "10. Script App with Library Variable Set and Project Tenant Variables" doesn't request a library variable set; only the bullets do.
* `library_variable_set_id`, `template_id` and `included_library_variable_sets` MUST NEVER be hard-coded literals (e.g. `"LibraryVariableSets-5"` or a bare GUID like `"0ad93005-637e-4dfa-…"`); always the data-source/resource ternary, with `template_id` from `...[0].template[N].id`.

## Library Variable Set Instructions

* Each resource "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" must have an associated data "octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets" (see example below).
* A library variable set "Variable Template" is defined in the "template" block of a "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" resource.
* **CRITICAL:** Variable template NAMES must be unique within each `octopusdeploy_library_variable_set` and each `octopusdeploy_project`. If the prompt asks for the same template twice (e.g. `Add a second tenant variable template to that library variable set, also called "Registry.Endpoint"`), emit ONE `template` block, keeping the last definition. Never add a numeric suffix.
* A tenant "Common Variable" is a "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" resource.
* When the variable template defines an AWS account, the "template" block must set "display_settings" to `{ "Octopus.ControlType" = "AmazonWebServicesAccount" }`.
* Every "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" must have a "depends_on" array including the associated "octopusdeploy_tenant_project".
* Omit the "label" attribute in a "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" "template" block if it would be an empty string.
* Every "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" must define "template_id" and "library_variable_set_id", never empty strings or null:

```
# plus data "octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" (ids = null, partial_name = "Example Variable Set", skip = 0, take = 1)
resource "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Example Variable Set"
  description = ""

  template {
    name             = "Common.Variable"
    label            = "A common variable that must be defined for each tenant"
    help_text        = "The help text associated with the variable is defined here."
    default_value    = ""
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "MultiLineText" }
  }
}

# plus octopusdeploy_tenant "tenant_australian_office" ("Australian Office") and its data source

resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" "tenantcommonvariable5_australian_office" {
  count                   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  library_variable_set_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets[0].id : octopusdeploy_library_variable_set.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set[0].id}"
  template_id             = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets[0].template[0].id : octopusdeploy_library_variable_set.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set[0].template[0].id}"
  tenant_id               = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_australian_office[0].id}"
  value                   = "Option1"
}
```

* **CRITICAL:** `template_id` MUST be a Terraform reference into the library variable set's own `template` block (ternary above), NEVER a literal GUID (Octopus generates ids; fails with "Template not found"). Index by position: `...library_variable_set.<label>[0].template[0].id` for the first, `template[1].id` for the second (not `template[0].id`), etc.

## Tenant Tag Instructions

**CRITICAL — a tag set name that is a SUBSTRING of an existing tag set kills the apply.** `data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets"` matches on `partial_name`, so "Geo" also returns "Geography": `count` becomes 0, "Geo" is never created, its `octopusdeploy_tag` resources attach to "Geography", and every tenant referencing `Geo/EU` fails with "Unable to find matching tag from canonical tag name or id" (fatal: tenants, project links and everything after are abandoned). Use the exact-name-match `locals` form for EVERY tag set, as in `## Environment Uniqueness and Naming Instructions`:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" "tagset_geo" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Geo"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 100
}

locals {
  # partial_name = "Geo" also returns "Geography", "Geo Region", etc.
  tagset_geo_matches = [for ts in data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_geo.tag_sets : ts if ts.name == "Geo"]
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" "tagset_geo" {
  count = "${length(local.tagset_geo_matches) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "Geo"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tag" "tagset_geo_tag_eu" {
  count      = "${length(local.tagset_geo_matches) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name       = "EU"
  tag_set_id = "${length(local.tagset_geo_matches) != 0 ? local.tagset_geo_matches[0].id : octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_geo[0].id}"
  color      = "#333333"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

* The same substring hazard applies to every `partial_name` lookup for a prompt-named resource (tag sets, project groups, feeds, accounts, lifecycles, tenants): use the exact-match `locals` form when the name could be contained in another.
* **CRITICAL — the most common cause of this error is an INVENTED tag. If the prompt did not ask for tenant tags, `tenant_tags` MUST be `[]`.** Observed: a prompt with a tenant and a tenant variable template `Inventory.Tenant.Warehouse` (no tags) produced `tenant_tags = ["Default/Warehouse"]`, killing the apply.
  * A tenant variable TEMPLATE is not a tenant TAG; its name is not a source of tag names. Target tags, tenant tags and tenant variable templates MUST NOT be converted into one another.
  * There is no default tag set called `Default`; a canonical tag with tag-set half `Default` is invented — delete it.
  * An untagged tenant linked via `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` is completely correct.

**Self-check — tenant tags**: for every `tenant_tags` string, the output must contain an `octopusdeploy_tag_set` named exactly the part before `/` and an `octopusdeploy_tag` named exactly the part after, both using the exact-match `locals` count, and the tenant must `depends_on` the tag. If any is absent, do NOT add them — DELETE the tag string (`tenant_tags = []`). Before writing non-empty `tenant_tags`, point at the prompt words that asked for that tag; if you cannot, leave it empty.

* "tenant_tags" is an array of "<tag set name>/<tag name>" strings, e.g. "Cities/Sydney" or "BU/Sales" — never "Sydney" or "region:us-east". "<tag set name>" must match a resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" name; "<tag name>" must match a resource "octopusdeploy_tag" name.
* The "tenant_tags" attribute must only be defined in an "octopusdeploy_process_step" block.
* Never create "octopusdeploy_project_tenant_tag".
* E.g. "Cities/Sydney" requires data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" "tagset_cities", resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" "tagset_cities" and resource "octopusdeploy_tag" "tagset_cities_tag_sydney" (`name = "Sydney"`, random `color`, `description = ""`), one tag resource per tag.
* "count" on "octopusdeploy_tag" must be `length(try([for item in data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.<tagset data name>.tag_sets[0].tags : item if item.name == "<tag name>"], [])) != 0 ? 0 : 1` (written out in full, never with underscores replacing brackets, `?` and `!`).
* **CRITICAL — do NOT backslash-escape the quotes around `<tag name>` (or any other native Terraform string literal) inside a `${...}` interpolation.** `item.name == "Prod"` is correct; `item.name == \"Prod\"` is invalid HCL and OpenTofu fails the whole file with `Invalid character` / `Invalid multi-line string` errors that cascade through every resource declared after it (observed: a `count` on `octopusdeploy_tag` written as `item.name == \"Prod\"` aborted the entire apply). Backslash-escaping is only for a string that is itself a literal value being reproduced verbatim inside another string (SpEL/JSON snippets in `notes`, `ssn_Message`, script bodies — see "Take EXTREME CARE escaping quotes" below); it must NEVER be applied to native Terraform expression syntax such as `for` filters, `if` conditions, or `==` comparisons inside `${...}`.
* **CRITICAL — any resource referencing a canonical `<tag set>/<tag>` (tenant `tenant_tags`, variable `scope { tenant_tags = [...] }`, step `tenant_tags`) MUST `depends_on` BOTH the `octopusdeploy_tag_set` AND each referenced `octopusdeploy_tag`**, even without a name collision, else `"There was a problem with your request. [Unable to find matching tag from canonical tag name or id '<tag set>/<tag>']"`. E.g. for `"region/emea"`: `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_region, octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_region_tag_emea]`.

## Lifecycle Instructions

* A lifecycle other than the "Default Lifecycle" needs a data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" and a resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle":

```
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_application" {
  partial_name = "Application"
  # ...
}

resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_application" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_application.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Application"
  description = "This is an example lifecycle that automatically deploys to the first environment"
  phase {
    # one or more phase blocks
  }
}
```

* "phase" blocks list environments in "optional_deployment_targets" (default) or "automatic_deployment_targets" (only when told the environment deploys automatically). No phase may be empty.
* Custom lifecycles include "Development", "Test", and "Production" unless otherwise instructed.
* **CRITICAL — when the prompt names the phases (e.g. "with phases for Development, Test, and Production environments"), create ONLY those `phase` blocks** — no template phases (e.g. no "Security" for DevSecOps) and no environments needed only for unmentioned phases. The description may still reference the broader lifecycle purpose.
* Never set `unit` to `Releases` in `release_retention_with_strategy` or `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` of `octopusdeploy_lifecycle`.
* **CRITICAL — inside these retention blocks use 2-space indentation; closing `}` aligns with the block keyword** (1-space or column-0 `}` FORBIDDEN):

```hcl
  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Items"
  }
```

## Variable Template Instructions

* "default_value" in a "template" block is "null" (never an empty string) when there is no default:

```
  template {
    name             = "ConnectionString"
    label            = "Database Connection String"
    help_text        = "Provide the connection string for the API Gateway database."
    default_value    = null
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
  }
```

* Always include:

```
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "system_lifecycle_firstlifecycle" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = ""
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

## GitHub Connection Instructions

* Never define data source "octopusdeploy_github_connections".

## Resource and Data Instructions

* Lifecycle reference: "resource" "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" + "data" "octopusdeploy_lifecycles". Project group: "resource" "octopusdeploy_project_group" + "data" "octopusdeploy_project_groups".
* Feed: "data" "octopusdeploy_feeds" + one "resource" of "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry", "octopusdeploy_aws_elastic_container_registry", "octopusdeploy_maven_feed", "octopusdeploy_github_repository_feed", "octopusdeploy_helm_feed", "octopusdeploy_nuget_feed", "octopusdeploy_artifactory_generic_feed", "octopusdeploy_s3_feed".
* data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_type": "AwsElasticContainerRegistry", "BuiltIn", "Docker", "GitHub", "Helm", "Maven", "NuGet", "S3", "OciRegistry", "OctopusProject" or "ArtifactoryGeneric" — never "Git".

## Feed Instructions

* Always include exactly this built-in feed data source ("BuiltIn" needs empty "partial_name"); the built-in feed always exists — never create it as a resource:

```
data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_octopus_server__built_in_" {
  feed_type    = "BuiltIn"
  ids          = null
  partial_name = ""
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

* Define data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_docker_hub" exactly once.
* Unlisted "feed_type" in the prompt → "NuGet". Never "Npm".
* Never set "password" on "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" to an empty string.
* Never create "octopusdeploy_feed", "octopusdeploy_generic_artifact_feed" or "octopusdeploy_builtin_feed".
* Maven/Docker feed with anonymous authentication: "username" and "password" = null, not empty strings.
* "is_enhanced_mode" only on "octopusdeploy_nuget_feed" (never "octopusdeploy_maven_feed").

**CRITICAL — step scoping**: for "Set the step to scope to [Environment A] and [Environment B] environments only" (e.g. "Set the step to scope to Development and Production environments only") list those environment IDs (`octopusdeploy_environments` data/resource ternary, e.g. `environment_development`, `environment_production`) in the `environments` allowlist (the ONLY environments the step runs in) with `excluded_environments = null`. `excluded_environments` is a denylist (step SKIPPED), only for "exclude from X environment" or excluding Security in the DevSecOps lifecycle — never a workaround.

**Feed URIs**: `feed_uri` is only the registry base URL; `api_version` on `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry` is always separate. Never `"https://gcr.io/v2/"` or `"https://gcr.io/v2."`.
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" "feed_google_container_registry" {
  name         = "Google Container Registry"
  feed_uri     = "https://gcr.io"
  api_version  = "v2"
  username     = null
  password     = null
  # ... lifecycle { ignore_changes = [password], prevent_destroy = true }
}
```
* **CRITICAL** — GitHub Container Registry: `feed_uri = "https://ghcr.io"`, `api_version = "v2"`. Docker Hub: `feed_uri = "https://index.docker.io"`, `api_version = ""`.
* Data/resource brackets must balance: no stray extra `}`, and a `lifecycle` block must close before the resource's `}`.

## Project Group Instructions

* Only the "Default Project Group" data "octopusdeploy_project_groups" has a "postcondition" attribute.

## Account Instructions

* "account_type": "AzureOIDC", never "AzureOidc"; never "Certificate".
* Account resource types: "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account", "octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect", "octopusdeploy_gcp_account", "octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal", "octopusdeploy_ssh_key_account", "octopusdeploy_token_account", "octopusdeploy_username_password_account".
* "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account" "role_arn" is mandatory: "arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/MyRole".
* "octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect" "azure_environment": "AzureCloud" unless the prompt says otherwise; one of "AzureCloud", "AzureChinaCloud", "AzureGermanCloud", "AzureUSGovernment".
* "octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal": "application_id", "subscription_id", "tenant_id" = "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000".
* **CRITICAL — Azure account type**: "OIDC", "OpenID Connect", "Workload Identity Federation" → `octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect`; "Service Principal", "SP", "client secret" → `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal`; unspecified → `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal`.
* Never create data source "octopusdeploy_token_account".
* `octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect` requires `name` (verbatim), `azure_environment` (`"AzureCloud"` default), and placeholders `subscription_id`, `client_id`, `tenant_id` = `"00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"` (configured post-deploy).

## Octopus Runbook Instructions

* "retention_policy" only on resource "octopusdeploy_runbook". Never define "octopusdeploy_project_runbook".
* **CRITICAL — `environment_scope = "All"` (or `"Unscoped"`) with `environments = []` unless the prompt explicitly names the environments the runbook may run in.** `environment_scope = "Specified"` silently makes it unrunnable elsewhere.
  * "Add a runbook called 'Restart Server'", "Create a runbook called 'Purge Queue' in the project 'X'" and any wording without environments = unscoped. Don't infer scope from the lifecycle, its first environment or the script. Errors here are intermittent — always check.
  * `"Specified"` ONLY for e.g. "the runbook may only be run in Production" / "scope the runbook to the Dev and Test environments", listing exactly those.
  * Leave `retention_policy_with_strategy` as in the example configuration unless the prompt asks for a runbook retention policy; no `"Forever"` or invented figures.
  * Self-check: every `"Specified"` runbook must have prompt-named environments; else `"All"` + `environments = []`.
* **CRITICAL — `multi_tenancy_mode` MUST match the project's `tenanted_deployment_participation`** (an `"Untenanted"` runbook in a `"Tenanted"` project can never run):
  * `tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"` → `multi_tenancy_mode = "Untenanted"`
  * `tenanted_deployment_participation = "Tenanted"` → `multi_tenancy_mode = "Tenanted"` (or `"TenantedOrUntenanted"`)
  * `tenanted_deployment_participation = "TenantedOrUntenanted"` → `multi_tenancy_mode = "TenantedOrUntenanted"`
  * For `Create a runbook called "X" in the project "Y"` the project isn't in your output: look up the EXISTING project's mode and state it to yourself first. Errors here are intermittent — verify every time.
  * Undeterminable or in doubt → `"TenantedOrUntenanted"`; never `"Untenanted"` for a tenanted project.
  * Sibling runbooks stay consistent unless the prompt asks otherwise.
* Never define "unit" in a "retention_policy" block of "octopusdeploy_runbook".
* If "quantity_to_keep" is set, omit "should_keep_forever" (never both).
* Runbook for an existing project: omit "count" on "octopusdeploy_runbook", omit "octopusdeploy_project", set "project_id" to the existing project's ID.

## Environment, Lifecycle and Other Resource Name Characters

* "name" of "octopusdeploy_environment", "octopusdeploy_lifecycle", "octopusdeploy_channel", "octopusdeploy_tenant", "octopusdeploy_project_group", "octopusdeploy_runbook", "octopusdeploy_worker_pool" and every lifecycle "phase" MUST be copied from the prompt character for character. `()`, `[]`, `:` and `&` are accepted (verified: `Probe Paren (One)`, `Probe Amp & Three`).
* Only `/` is rejected: replace it with a DASH (never underscore), nothing else — `Staging (EU-West-1) / Primary` → `Staging (EU-West-1) - Primary`. Never alter a name without `/`.
* Stricter step-name rules (no parentheses/square brackets) apply ONLY to "octopusdeploy_process_step" and "octopusdeploy_process_child_step"; replacement is still a DASH: `Reconcile (Ledger)` → `Reconcile -Ledger-`.
* The Terraform LABEL (e.g. `environment_staging_eu_west_1_primary`: lowercased, punctuation → underscores) is not the `name`.

**Self-check**: compare each such `name` to the prompt character by character; restore anything other than `/` → dash.

## Octopus Project Instructions

* "name" of "octopusdeploy_project" may contain letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores, brackets, square brackets, colons, ampersands or hashes. Only `/` is rejected: replace it with a dash, change nothing else; never replace brackets with underscores.
* "slug" of "octopusdeploy_project": only lower case letters, numbers, dashes; strip leading/trailing dashes.
* When the prompt defines the slug, set it in "slug" (e.g. `slug = "my-project"`).
* If the prompt specifies a project name, use it for "name".
* Retention policies do not apply to "octopusdeploy_project": ignore any mention of them and never add a "retention_policy" block.
* Never create "octopusdeploy_project_deployment_settings" or "octopusdeploy_project_retention_policy" resources.
* "skip_machine_behavior": "None" or "SkipUnavailableMachines".
* Every "octopusdeploy_project" must include "lifecycle_id".
* **CRITICAL — always `is_version_controlled = false`, even if the prompt asks for a version-controlled, Config-as-Code, GitOps, or Git-stored project.** VCS conversion tries to authenticate to the real repo and fails at `terraform apply` (`Error converting project to VCS: The username or token used to authenticate with the Git Repository is invalid`), aborting the ENTIRE apply so no project is created.
  * Emit no `git_library_persistence_settings`, `git_anonymous_persistence_settings`, `git_username_password_persistence_settings` or other VCS/Git persistence block on `octopusdeploy_project`.
  * Don't reference an `octopusdeploy_git_credential` from project persistence settings or create one *in order to* version control the project — except a Platform Hub prompt that explicitly requests it (see the platform hub instructions).
  * A git credential requested in its own right (e.g. `Create a Git Connection called "Config Git" with … the allowed repository "…"`) MUST still be created, just not attached to the project. Git credentials consumed by STEPS (`Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests`, `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize`, `Octopus.CommitToGit`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` sourced from `GitRepository`, via `git_dependencies` or execution properties) are correct and required.
  * Note the declined request in the description, e.g. "This project remains a standard database-backed project rather than a version-controlled (Config-as-Code) project." Create everything else as normal.
* Never set "auto_create_release" to "true".
* Data source names must match the name used in "project_id" references; never reference a data source that does not exist in the output. E.g. data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_aws_lambda" (`partial_name = "${var.project_aws_lambda_name}"`) is referenced as:

```
"${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_aws_lambda.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_aws_lambda.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_aws_lambda[0].id}"
```

* Never define "tenant_tags" on "octopusdeploy_project".
* "tenanted_deployment_participation" must be "Untenanted", "Tenanted" or "TenantedOrUntenanted" (never "TenantedOnly"). If the project is associated with a tenant and no mode is specified, use "TenantedOrUntenanted", and link the tenant to all environments in the project's lifecycle.
* Include a variable for the project name:
```
variable "project_my_project_name" {
  type        = string
  nullable    = false
  sensitive   = false
  description = "The name of the project"
  default     = "My Project"
}

resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_project" {
  name                                 = "${var.project_my_project_name}"
  # Define other properties here
}
```

* "Set the prompt to disabled" refers to the option to automatically create a new project; ignore it — it never disables the project or affects the Terraform.
* Projects are enabled by default unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* **CRITICAL — set `is_disabled = true` on `octopusdeploy_project` if and ONLY if the prompt contains the EXACT line `* The project must be disabled.`** (a common miss). NOT justified by: steps with `The step must be disabled.`, all steps disabled (e.g. TODO YAML placeholders), a disabled trigger (`is_disabled` on the trigger resource), or parameters/variables named `suspend`, `disabled`, etc. Self-check: remove `is_disabled = true` if that exact line is absent.

**CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_project_versioning_strategy` MUST use `count` matching the project and a ternary `project_id`** (without `count` it fails on idempotent re-apply), and MUST NOT include `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }` (project-scoped, unlike feeds/channels):

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_project_versioning_strategy" "project_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  template   = "#{Octopus.Version.LastMajor}.#{Octopus.Version.LastMinor}.#{Octopus.Version.NextPatch}"
  # NO lifecycle block
}
```

**CRITICAL — package-based release versioning uses a `donor_package` block (omit `template`); never silently drop it.** Triggers: "version releases using the package version", "set the release versioning template to use the package version of the package in the 'Warm Cache' step", "base the release number on the package in the deploy step", "use the package version as the release version".

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_project_versioning_strategy" "project_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"

  donor_package {
    deployment_action = "Warm Cache"
    package_reference = ""
  }
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_warm_cache]
}
```

* `donor_package` REQUIRES `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_<project>_<step>]`: the step is referenced by literal name, so Terraform can't infer ordering, and without it the apply aborts with `Error updating associated project` / `The resource 'Warm Cache' was not found.` A `template` strategy needs no `depends_on`.
* Match by NAME: `deployment_action` = the step's "name" exactly (also its first action's name); `package_reference = ""` = the step's primary_package, else the "packages" map key (e.g. "octopub-frontend"). **Never set `donor_package_step_id`** — the API expects an action ID there, but Terraform can only produce `octopusdeploy_process_step.<name>.id`, a step ID (same trap as `actions` in an `octopusdeploy_variable` `scope` block).
* `donor_package` and `template` are mutually exclusive: package-based → `donor_package`; date-based or incrementing → `template`.
* The `deployment_action` step MUST have a package (`primary_package` or a `packages` map entry), else no release can be created.

* Every project tenant variable needs a "template" block in "octopusdeploy_project"; add them BEFORE creating `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resources. **Number of `template` blocks ≥ number of `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resources.** Each block defines ONE variable (`name`, `label`, `help_text`, `default_value`, `display_settings`); each tenant variable's `template_id` references `template[N].id` (0-indexed).
* No trailing whitespace/line breaks in "octopusdeploy_project" "description" (e.g. not `"This is a description with line breaks\n\n"`).
* **CRITICAL — multiple migration notes set/appended to the project description (e.g. "NOTE (migration): keepWaitingPipelines=false...", "NOTE (migration): limitConcurrent=true...", "NOTE (migration): roles=[app-0299-prod]...") are concatenated into ONE `description` value** — never duplicate `description` attributes or separate resource blocks:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_deploy_to_production" {
  description = "NOTE (migration): The original Spinnaker pipeline had keepWaitingPipelines=false, ... NOTE (migration): The original Spinnaker pipeline had limitConcurrent=true, ... NOTE (migration): The original Spinnaker pipeline required the following roles for execution: app-0299-prod."
}
```
* Never split "release_notes_template" over two lines (no line break after `=`); keep the value on the same line.

## Octopus Project Trigger Instructions

**CRITICAL — cron expressions:** Octopus requires 5, 6 or 7 whitespace-separated fields; an unparseable cron aborts the apply (`It has 3 fields and we are expecting 5, 6 or 7 fields.`).
* When the prompt supplies a cron expression, count its fields. If not 5, 6 or 7 fields, or plainly not a cron (e.g. `Add a scheduled trigger called "Nightly Settlement" that runs on the cron expression "not a cron"`), do NOT pass it through: emit a `once_daily_schedule` and record the prompt's original text in the trigger `description`. (Same principle as deployment freeze date ordering: normalise impossible schedules to the nearest valid form.)

**CRITICAL — every `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger` needs BOTH a schedule block AND an action block** (missing action: `The Action field is required.`). Canonical shape: `once_daily_schedule` + `deploy_latest_release_action`. When rewriting an invalid schedule, keep the action block.
* If the prompt gives no environment for the action, use the environment the trigger's purpose implies (Security for a scan, Production for a nightly job) for both `source_environment_id` and `destination_environment_id`.

**CRITICAL — `once_daily_schedule` is the ONLY schedule block allowed.** Invented blocks (`monthly_schedule`, `weekly_schedule`, `cron_schedule`, `days_per_month_schedule`) fail at PLAN time (`Blocks of type "monthly_schedule" are not expected here.`), so NOTHING is created.
* **Daily** ("every day at 2am", "nightly") → `once_daily_schedule` with `start_time` and all seven `days_of_week`.
* **Weekly** ("every Monday at 6am", "every Friday at 5pm") → `once_daily_schedule` with ONLY the named day(s) in `days_of_week`.
* **Monthly, quarterly, cron, "the first day of each month", "every 15 minutes"** → cannot be expressed; emit `once_daily_schedule` on the closest workable cadence (typically daily) and say so in `description` (e.g. `"Approximates the requested monthly schedule; Octopus scheduled triggers created here support a daily schedule only."`).
* NEVER write a block name not seen in this file or the example configurations. The trigger must never be the reason a project fails.
* `start_time`: ISO 8601 string. `timezone`: a valid "Windows Time Zone Identifier" string.
* `project_id` on a trigger must never be empty.

Example (09:00 daily, Pacific Standard Time):
```
resource "octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger" "projecttrigger_aws_lambda_daily_security_scan" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_aws_lambda.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  space_id    = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  name        = "Daily Security Scan"
  description = "This trigger reruns the deployment in the Security environment. This means any new vulnerabilities detected after a production deployment will be identified."
  timezone    = "Pacific Standard Time"
  is_disabled = false
  project_id  = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_aws_lambda.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_aws_lambda.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_aws_lambda[0].id}"
  tenant_ids  = []

  once_daily_schedule {
    start_time   = "2025-05-08T09:00:00"
    days_of_week = ["Sunday", "Monday", "Tuesday", "Wednesday", "Thursday", "Friday", "Saturday"]
  }

  deploy_latest_release_action {
    source_environment_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_security[0].id}"
    destination_environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_security[0].id}"
    should_redeploy            = true
  }
}
```

**External feed triggers (`octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger`):**

* Never create `octopusdeploy_project_automatic_release_trigger` resources.
* Every trigger MUST have `count`, ternary `project_id` and `channel_id`, `depends_on` the process steps order, and `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`. **CRITICAL:** this applies to placeholder triggers (no `package` block) too.
* If the prompt requests an external feed trigger but no channel, create a channel called "Application" and link it to the trigger.
* **CRITICAL:** `channel_id` on ALL triggers MUST use the ternary lookup, even if the channel is created in the same file. Never a literal like `"Channels-1"`, never a direct reference like `"${octopusdeploy_channel.channel_application.id}"`. Likewise `project_id` must never be a direct reference like `octopusdeploy_project.project_foo.id`.
* **CRITICAL:** when the prompt says the trigger must be disabled (e.g. `"The trigger must be disabled."`, used for migrated pipelines with originally-disabled Docker triggers), set `is_disabled = true`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_channel" "channel_application" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_foo.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name       = "Application"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_foo.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_foo.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_foo[0].id}"
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "projecttrigger_every_step_project_external_feed_trigger" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  space_id   = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_every_step_project[0].id}"
  name       = "External Feed Trigger"
  channel_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_frontend_application.channels) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_frontend_application.channels[0].id : octopusdeploy_channel.channel_frontend_application[0].id}"

  package {
    deployment_action_slug = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_every_step_project_run_a_script[0].slug}"
    package_reference      = "mypackage"
  }
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_every_step_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

**Regex channel rules:**
* If the prompt says `Add a channel called "Application" to the project and configure a version rule that matches the regex "<regex>" for every step that deploys a Docker image.`, create an `octopusdeploy_channel` "Application" with a `rule` block whose `tag` is the exact regex, and one `action_package` block per matching deployment action that exposes package metadata.
* **CRITICAL:** if the matching steps are `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` steps without `primary_package` or `packages` blocks, create the channel WITHOUT any `rule` block (API error: "Version rules must specify a package step"). Do not drop the channel.
* If the prompt has both a regex-based `Application` channel and an external feed trigger, the trigger references that channel; do not create a second bare `Application` channel.

**CRITICAL — no channel named in the prompt:** look up the project's implicit `Default` channel; never fall back to `"Channels-1"` (copied from example configurations; belongs to another space; fails with `Trigger 'Automatic Release Creation' references a channel with identifier 'Channels-1' which does not exist.` after other resources were created, e.g. for `Enable automatic release creation on "79. Billing API" whenever a new package is pushed`). Filter by BOTH `partial_name = "Default"` and `project_id` (REQUIRED; name alone matches every project's Default channel), and reference `.channels[0].id` directly (no ternary; no resource to fall back to). Same applies to `octopusdeploy_built_in_trigger` `channel_id`.

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_channels" "channel_project_foo_default" {
  ids          = []
  partial_name = "Default"
  project_id   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_foo.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_foo.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_foo[0].id}"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
# trigger: channel_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_project_foo_default.channels[0].id}"
```

**`package` block rules:**
* `deployment_action_slug` must reference the slug of a deployment action in the associated project; never empty string or null when the referenced step has no primary package.
* `package_reference`: `""` when referencing the step's `primary_package`; otherwise the exact key from the step's `packages` map. Never `""` when the step has no `primary_package`, or is `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`.
* If none of the project's steps define `primary_package` or `packages` (e.g. `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` without them), omit the `package` block (placeholder trigger). Do not drop the trigger.

```
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step" {
  name          = "Run a script"
  packages      = {
    # webapp is a named package
    webapp = {
        # ...
        package_id = "MyPackage",
    }
  }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "project_trigger" {
  # ...
  package {
    deployment_action_slug = "run-a-script"
    # The package_reference must match the key "webapp" from the "packages" block in the octopusdeploy_process_step resource because there is no primary package
    package_reference      = "webapp"
  }
}
```

**Self-check — trigger `package` vs. the step it names:** for each trigger with a `package` block, read the emitted step: either it has `primary_package` and `package_reference = ""`, or it has a `packages` map and `package_reference` is the exact key. Otherwise DELETE the `package` block (placeholder); never guess. Decide by the step TYPE actually emitted, not the prompt's wording ("deploys the Azure Web App" / "web app deployment" does not imply a package) — e.g. an Azure Web App deployment emitted as `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` inline `az` script has no package (error: `The specified package reference '' for trigger 'Automatic Release Creation' does not exist on the action 'Deploy Azure Web App'.`).

**CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_git_trigger`:** each `sources` entry's `deployment_action_slug` must name an action that declares a git dependency (else: `The specified Git dependency '' for trigger 'Git Trigger' does not exist on the action 'Deploy to IIS'.`).
* A step has a git dependency only if sourced from Git: `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository"` in `execution_properties`, or a Git-manifest step type (`Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests`, `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize`, `Octopus.CommitToGit`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` sourced from Git). Package steps (`Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.AzureWebApp`, `Octopus.TentaclePackage`, `Octopus.WindowsService`) never have one; e.g. never point at `deploy-to-iis` (observed for `Create a git trigger on this project that creates a release whenever the "main" branch of "https://github.com/example/portal.git" changes`).
* `git_dependency_name = ""` means the step's UNNAMED git dependency.
* The template's `Scan for Vulnerabilities` step (runs `octopus/DirectorySbomScan.ps1` from Git) is usually the only git-dependent action; if so, point `deployment_action_slug` at it.
* If NO step has a git dependency, do NOT emit the trigger; note it in the project `description` as not implemented.
* If git-dependent steps read from a DIFFERENT repository than requested (e.g. `Scan for Vulnerabilities` reads `https://github.com/OctopusSolutionsEngineering/Octopub.git` but the prompt names `https://github.com/example/portal.git`), drop the trigger and state accurately in `description` that no step reads from the REQUESTED repository (not that no step reads from Git).
* Self-check: for every git trigger, verify each slug's step has a git dependency; else repoint or delete and note it.

## Tenant Variable Instructions

* If the prompt specified that "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" tenant variables are to be assigned to tenants without specifying the environment, define the tenant variable for all environments the tenant is connected to.
* `environment_id` and `template_id` on "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" must never be "null" or an empty string.
* `template_id` must be like "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.<project name>.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_project.<project name>[0].template[0].id}"

## Environment Instructions

* Never define the "use_guided_infrastructure" property on a resource "octopusdeploy_environment".

## Ephemeral Environment Instructions

* Ephemeral environments require an `octopusdeploy_channel` with `type` = `EphemeralEnvironment` and `parent_environment_id` set to the ID of an `octopusdeploy_parent_environment` resource — never an empty string, null, or an `octopusdeploy_environment` ID. Create at least one `octopusdeploy_parent_environment` when creating such a channel.
* An `EphemeralEnvironment` channel MUST have `ephemeral_environment_name_template`.
* `custom_field_definitions` values are accessed via `Octopus.Release.CustomFields[<field_name>]`. If `Octopus.Release.CustomFields` is used anywhere, a matching `custom_field_definitions` entry must exist on the `EphemeralEnvironment` channel. Objects in `custom_field_definitions` have ONLY `field_name` and `description`.
* Never add `custom_field_definitions` or `ephemeral_environment_name_template` to a channel of `type` `Lifecycle` or with no `type`.
* `octopusdeploy_parent_environment`: MUST have `space_id`; never `allow_dynamic_infrastructure` or `base_environment_id`. Look it up with an `octopusdeploy_parent_environments` data source, never an `octopusdeploy_environments` data source.

CORRECT EXAMPLE (WRONG would be `parent_environment_id` pointing at `environment_development`, or missing the name template):
```
data "octopusdeploy_parent_environments" "parent_environment_features" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Features"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_parent_environment" "parent_environment_features" {
  count                         = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_features.parent_environments) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  space_id                      = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  name                          = "Features"
  use_guided_failure            = false
  automatic_deprovisioning_rule = { days = 7, hours = 0 }
  depends_on                    = []
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_channel" "channel_k8s_web_app_with_ephemeral_environments_ephemeral_environment" {
  name       = "Ephemeral Environment"
  type       = "EphemeralEnvironment"
  parent_environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_features.parent_environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_features.parent_environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_parent_environment.parent_environment_features[0].id}"
  ephemeral_environment_name_template = "#{Octopus.Release.CustomFields[FeatureBranch]}"
  custom_field_definitions            = [{ description = "The name of the feature branch", field_name = "FeatureBranch" }]
}
```

**CRITICAL — parent and regular environments SHARE ONE NAME SPACE.** Never emit an `octopusdeploy_environment` with the same `name` as an `octopusdeploy_parent_environment` (apply aborts: `An environment with this name already exists in this space.`). E.g. `Add support for ephemeral environments, with the Parent Environment and the Ephemeral Environment channel both called "Previews"` plus a runbook `runnable in the "Previews" environment only` must NOT produce a regular "Previews" environment.
* When a runbook, step, target or trigger is scoped to the parent environment, reference the PARENT environment's ternary id:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_my_project_provision_preview" {
  environment_scope = "Specified"
  environments      = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_previews.parent_environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_previews.parent_environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_parent_environment.parent_environment_previews[0].id}"]
  ...
}
```
* Do NOT rename either resource or drop the parent environment; emit exactly one resource, the `octopusdeploy_parent_environment`.
* Self-check: if any name appears as both a parent and a regular environment, delete the `octopusdeploy_environment` and repoint its references to the parent environment's ternary.
* When a target is scoped to a parent environment, create both the `octopusdeploy_parent_environment` resource and the `octopusdeploy_parent_environments` data source. Example target referencing a regular (Development) and a parent (Features) environment:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target" "target_kubernetes_with_token_auth" {
  count                             = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_deployment_targets.target_kubernetes_with_token_auth.deployment_targets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  cluster_url                       = "http://clusterurl"

  # This is how the target references a parent environment (Features) and a regular environment (Development)
  environments                      = [
    "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}",
    "${length(data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_features.parent_environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_parent_environments.parent_environment_features.parent_environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_parent_environment.parent_environment_features[0].id}"]
  name                              = "Kubernetes with Token Auth"
  roles                             = ["Kubernetes"]
  # ...

  endpoint {
    communication_style = "Kubernetes"
  }

  # NEVER leave these empty. The health check container cannot be pulled from the built-in feed,
  # so the target's container block MUST point at the anonymous "Docker Hub" feed.
  container {
    feed_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_docker_hub[0].id}"
    image   = "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04"
  }

  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

## Octopus Project Channel Instructions

**CRITICAL — scoping a step to a channel MUST NOT create a dependency cycle** (e.g. "this step only runs on the 'Hotfix' channel"; the step references the channel).
* A step-scoped channel's `depends_on` may reference the project and lifecycle ONLY — never `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`, `octopusdeploy_process`, or any `octopusdeploy_process_step` (cycle `process_step → channel → process_steps_order → process_step`).
* If scoping can't be expressed without a cycle, drop the step's `channels` attribute and keep everything else.

**CRITICAL — output MUST be valid HCL and NOTHING ELSE.** No prose, explanations, apologies or diagnostics outside `#` comments (observed: a cycle explanation written as line 1 gave `Blocks of type "The" are not expected here.`).
* If you notice an unresolvable problem, FIX IT SILENTLY by dropping the offending attribute or resource.
* Never use backticks in the Terraform. The output must begin with a valid HCL construct (`data`, `resource`, `variable`, `#`, or `terraform`).

* resource "octopusdeploy_channel" required: "name", "project_id". Optional: "description", "is_default" (set "false" unless otherwise specified), "lifecycle_id", "rule" (nested block for per-step/package versioning rules), "space_id", "tenant_tags" (list of strings).
* A project can have multiple "octopusdeploy_channel" resources. Never create "octopusdeploy_project_channel" resources.
* "count" must be "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.<name>.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}" where "<name>" is the project's "octopusdeploy_projects" data source — never an "octopusdeploy_channels" data source. Never reference an "octopusdeploy_projects" data source that does not exist in the output.
* Every "octopusdeploy_channel" resource must have an associated "octopusdeploy_channels" data source.
* **CRITICAL:** every `octopusdeploy_channel` MUST have `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`, including bare channels without `rule` blocks (e.g. the "Application" channel for external feed triggers).
* `project_id` must use the ternary lookup, never `"${octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project.id}"`.
* **CRITICAL:** a channel referenced by an `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger`, or having `rule` blocks (the `action_package` slugs must exist first), MUST have `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_<project>]`.

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_channels" "channel_my_project_application" {
  ids          = []
  partial_name = "Application"
  project_id   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_channel" "channel_my_project_application" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"   # REQUIRED
  name       = "Application"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"   # REQUIRED: ternary
  is_default = false
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_my_project]   # REQUIRED
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true   # REQUIRED
  }
}
```

Channel with a "rule" block matching tag hotfix (regex ^hotfix$) targeting specific deployment actions:
```
resource "octopusdeploy_channel" "channel_lambda_hotfixing_hotfix" {
  count         = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_lambda_hotfixing.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name          = "Hotfix"
  # ...
  lifecycle_id  = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_lambda_hotfix.lifecycles) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_lambda_hotfix.lifecycles[0].id : octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_lambda_hotfix[0].id}"
  rule {
        action_package {
          deployment_action = "deploy-database-changes"
        }
        action_package {
          deployment_action = "deploy-rate-service"
        }
        action_package {
          deployment_action = "deploy-trading-site"
        }
        tag = "^hotfix$"
        # NOTE: there is deliberately no `version_range` line here. A rule that filters on a tag
        # only must OMIT `version_range` entirely — writing `version_range = ""` aborts the apply.
    }
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```
Version-range example: `rule { action_package { deployment_action = "Deploy a Helm Chart" } tag = "^featurebranch$" version_range = "[1.0,)" }`.

* **ABSOLUTE RULE:** every `rule` block MUST have at least one `action_package` block ("Version rules must specify a package step"). If no package-based steps exist (e.g. all `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` without package blocks), add NO `rule` block; a bare channel is valid for triggers.
