* **CRITICAL:** a `rule` block MUST NEVER set `version_range = ""` or `tag = ""` — OMIT the attribute (Octopus stores empty as null; `Provider produced inconsistent result after apply` / `.rule[0].version_range: was cty.StringVal(""), but now null.`; fatal and late, after project/steps/variables are created). Tag-only rules have no `version_range` line (e.g. never `tag = "^master.*"` with `version_range = ""`); version-only rules have no `tag` line. Omitting `version_range` means "any version". Same for every optional string attribute on a `rule` (same failure mode as empty `parameters` on `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`).
* Self-check: search every channel `rule` block for `= ""` and delete those lines.

## Octopus Global Deployment Freeze Instructions

**CRITICAL — a requested deployment freeze MUST be created** as `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze` ("add a deployment freeze called X", "block deployments to <environment> between <dates>", "freeze deployments over <period>"; observed dropped for `Add a deployment freeze called "Change Freeze" that blocks deployments to "Prod" for the whole of December 2026`). Self-check: for each "freeze", "blackout", "change window" or "no deployments between" in the prompt, confirm a matching `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze`.
* **CRITICAL — there is NO `project_scope` block.** `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze` accepts only `name`, `start`, `end` (optional `recurring_schedule`); never `description` or `space_id`. Scope with one `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_project` per named project; an unscoped freeze applies to everything.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze" "deploymentfreeze_data_freeze" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "Data Freeze"
  start = "2027-07-01T00:00:00.000+00:00"
  end   = "2027-07-08T00:00:00.000+00:00"
}

resource "octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_project" "deploymentfreeze_data_freeze_cafe_ingestor" {
  count               = "..."
  deploymentfreeze_id = "${octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze.deploymentfreeze_data_freeze[0].id}"
  project_id          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_cafe_ingestor.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_cafe_ingestor.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_cafe_ingestor[0].id}"
  environment_ids     = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"]
}
```
  * `deploymentfreeze_id` and `project_id` are BOTH required. `environment_ids` is optional but never `environment_ids = []`.
  * E.g. `Add a deployment freeze called "Data Freeze" from 2027-07-01 to 2027-07-08 covering only the "15. Cafe Ingestor" project` needs the scope resource.
  * Also available: `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_tenant` (tenant-scoped) and `octopusdeploy_project_deployment_freeze` (project-OWNED: `owner_id`, `name`, `start`, `end`, `environment_ids`). Prefer the `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze` + `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_project` pair for "global freeze scoped to some projects".
  * Self-check: each freeze whose prompt names projects/environments has an `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_project` per named project.
* Deployment freezes are GLOBAL (not space-scoped). Create exactly the freezes the prompt names and no others.
* **CRITICAL — a named freeze with NO DATES must STILL be created** (e.g. `Add a deployment freeze called "Freeze" covering every environment and every project`): use a plausible future window (default: a week from the start of the next calendar year) and note in the project description that dates were chosen because the prompt gave none:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze" "deploymentfreeze_freeze" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "Freeze"
  start = "2027-01-01T00:00:00.000+00:00"
  end   = "2027-01-08T00:00:00.000+00:00"
}
```
* "every environment and every project" is the one case where NO `octopusdeploy_deployment_freeze_project` is correct; don't invent per-project scopes or drop the freeze.
* Generally, for any resource with unspecified mandatory attributes: choose a defensible value, create the resource, document the choice.
* The "end" property must be in the future AND strictly AFTER "start" (`The end date must be after the start date`; `2026-12-01` to `2026-11-01` is rejected).
* **When the prompt gives dates the wrong way round, SWAP them** and note the correction in the project description; don't pass through or drop (observed: `Create a deployment freeze called "Change Window" that runs from "2026-12-01" to "2026-11-01"`).

## Octopus Deployment Process Instructions

* An execution container image like `octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.6.4-ubuntu.22.04` implies the "Hosted Ubuntu" worker pool (image is Linux-only); one like `octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.6.4-windows.ltsc2025` implies the "Hosted Windows" worker pool (image is Windows-only).
* The `dockerfile` property in a `container` block of a `octopusdeploy_process_step` must be an inline string, never a heredoc. Invalid HCL:

```
container = { dockerfile = <<-EOT
    FROM python:3.11-slim
    CMD ["/bin/bash"]
  EOT
  , feed_id = "", git_url = null, image = "" }
```

* **CRITICAL:** In inline script bodies (`Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` or kubectl commands) from `deleteManifest`, `scaleManifest`, or `runJob` stage types, convert SpEL pipeline parameter references (which don't evaluate in Octopus) to Octopus syntax: `${ parameters.<name> }` → `#{<name>}` (e.g. `${ parameters.model_version }` → `#{model_version}`, `${ parameters.namespace }` → `#{namespace}`). If any were converted, add at the top of the script or in the step notes: `# NOTE: Source system parameter references have been converted to Octopus variable syntax (#{variable_name}).`
* **CRITICAL:** When a Terraform string value (e.g. in `parameters`, `notes`, or `execution_properties`) must contain an unconvertible SpEL expression verbatim, escape `$` as `$$`: `${execution.name} has started.` → `"$${execution.name} has started."`, never `"${execution.name} has started."`. Already-converted Octopus syntax (e.g. `#{Octopus.Release.Number}`) needs no escaping — only `${}` patterns.
* Treat attributes in the "execution_properties" and "properties" blocks of the example steps as mandatory unless otherwise specified (exception: properties on script steps defining inline scripts or scripts sourced from packages).
* Every "octopusdeploy_project" must have an "octopusdeploy_process"; every "octopusdeploy_process" an "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order"; every "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" an "octopusdeploy_process_step". Always define steps for a project or runbook.
* Steps are defined in "octopusdeploy_process_step" and "octopusdeploy_process_child_step"; step order in "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order"; child step order in "octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order".

**CRITICAL: canonical shape for rolling deployments and child steps (no example configuration contains a child step; copy this exactly).** `octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order` requires BOTH `process_id` AND `parent_id`, and lists children in `children` (never `steps`). A missing `process_id` fails at PLAN time (`The argument "process_id" is required, but no definition was found.`), so nothing at all is created. The rolling window size is `Octopus.Action.MaxParallelism` in the PARENT step's `properties` (not `execution_properties`, not on children).

```hcl
# The parent step carries the target roles and the rolling window
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_deploy_to_iis" {
  count      = "..."
  name       = "Deploy to IIS"
  type       = "Octopus.IIS"
  process_id = "..."
  properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.MaxParallelism"           = "2"
    "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"              = "iis-server"
    "Octopus.Action.ExecutionTimeout.Enabled" = "False"
  }
  execution_properties = { ... }
}

# Each child step names BOTH the process and its parent step.
# Child steps have NO start_trigger, NO package_requirement and NO notes attribute.
resource "octopusdeploy_process_child_step" "process_child_step_my_project_drain_connections" {
  count                 = "..."
  name                  = "Drain Connections"
  type                  = "Octopus.Script"
  process_id            = "..."
  parent_id             = "${octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_deploy_to_iis[0].id}"
  channels              = null
  condition             = "Success"
  environments          = null
  excluded_environments = null
  slug                  = "drain-connections"
  tenant_tags           = null
  execution_properties  = {
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"         = "true"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax"       = "PowerShell"
    "OctopusUseBundledTooling"           = "False"
  }
}

# The child order names the process, the parent, and the children in order
resource "octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order" "process_child_step_order_my_project_deploy_to_iis" {
  count      = "..."
  process_id = "..."
  parent_id  = "${octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_my_project_deploy_to_iis[0].id}"
  children   = ["${octopusdeploy_process_child_step.process_child_step_my_project_drain_connections[0].id}"]
}
```

* The parent step appears in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.steps` as normal; child steps appear only in `children`.

**CRITICAL: action names must be unique across the WHOLE process, parent and child steps together; a child may NOT reuse its parent's name**.
* The numeric-suffix rule (` 2`) applies across the parent/child boundary.
* When the prompt gives a rolling step and one of its children the same name, keep the name on the PARENT and rename the child. E.g. `Make "Deploy to IIS" a rolling deployment … give that rolling step three child steps: "Drain Node", "Deploy to IIS" and "Rejoin Node"`: emit the child as `Deploy to IIS 2`, or better, fold it into the parent and emit only the other two children.

**CRITICAL: child steps nest exactly ONE level deep.** `parent_id` must reference a top-level `octopusdeploy_process_step`, never an `octopusdeploy_process_child_step`.
* Child of a child requested: attach it to the top-level ancestor and note the flattening in `notes`. E.g. `Make "Rejoin Node" a child of "Drain Node"` (where `Drain Node` is a child of the rolling step) → `Rejoin Node` gets the ROLLING step's id as `parent_id`.
* Named parent doesn't exist: don't invent a `parent_id` or emit a dangling reference; create the child as an ordinary top-level `octopusdeploy_process_step` and record in its `notes` that the parent was not found. E.g. `Add a child step called "Verify Node" to a parent step called "Nonexistent Parent"` → normal step `Verify Node`.
* Every `parent_id` must textually match an `octopusdeploy_process_step.<label>.id` in the same output.

**Self-check (child steps):** every `octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order` sets `process_id`, `parent_id`, `children`; every `octopusdeploy_process_child_step` sets `process_id` and a `parent_id` resolving to an `octopusdeploy_process_step`. Collect the `name` of every `octopusdeploy_process_step` AND `octopusdeploy_process_child_step` into one list; append ` 2`, ` 3` … to any repeat.

* If the prompt says `The step must be disabled.` (hard-disabled stage), still create the step, set `is_disabled = true`, and include it in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`.
* When a skipped/ignored stage is removed from the dependency graph, the step order must reflect the prompt's rewritten dependency chain, not assume the skipped stage exists.
* Never use asterisks (e.g. "*****") as placeholders in step properties.
* "Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" may only be defined in the "properties" block, never "execution_properties".

**When the prompt scopes a STEP to a named channel, never silently drop it.** Step `channels` takes channel IDs; any channel is accepted, including an `EphemeralEnvironment` channel (e.g. `Add a script step called "Seed Test Data" that only runs in the ephemeral "PR Builds" channel` must not yield `channels = null`).
* Existing channel (data source): `channels = ["${data.octopusdeploy_channels.<name>.channels[0].id}"]`.
* Channel CREATED IN THE SAME OUTPUT: a channel with a `rule` block must `depends_on` the `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` (its `action_package` entries name actions), so referencing it from a step creates a cycle. Either:
  * give the channel NO `rule` block, drop its `depends_on`, and reference `octopusdeploy_channel.<name>.id` from the step; or
  * if it needs rules, leave `channels = null` on the step and record the limitation in the step's `notes`.
* Never scope a step to the built-in "Default" channel (same as variables; it's a no-op).
* Any dropped prompt-specified channel scope must be stated in the step's `notes`.

* The "condition" attribute on "octopusdeploy_process_step" and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` must be one of "Always", "Failure", "Success", "Variable". Any other prompt condition → "Success".
* **CRITICAL:** condition mapping (both resource types):

| Prompt phrase | `condition` value |
|---|---|
| `Only run the step when the previous step has failed.` | `"Failure"` |
| `Always run the step.` | `"Always"` |
| *(no condition phrase)* | `"Success"` ← default |
| `Set condition to "Variable"` with expression | `"Variable"` |

Self-check: after generating all steps, verify each step whose prompt contains `Always run the step.` has `condition = "Always"` (common mistake: left as `"Success"`) and each with `Only run the step when the previous step has failed.` has `condition = "Failure"`.

**Self-check (steps order completeness):** every `octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` ID must appear in the `steps` array of its `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` — including disabled, parallel, notification, and **"Review template-derived pipeline behavior" script steps** (a step not in the order never executes). The `steps` length MUST equal N(process_step) + N(templated_step) for that project: add missing IDs if fewer; if more, remove duplicate IDs. Every `steps` reference must point to an existing resource.

**CRITICAL:** In a `templatedPipeline` project, the "Review template-derived pipeline behavior" step reference MUST be the LAST element of `steps`. Order: all Slack Notification - Start, then all deployment stages, then all Slack Notification - Finish, then all Slack Notification - Complete, then the Review step. Never place it between Start and Finish/Complete.
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" "process_step_order_my_project" {
  count      = ...
  process_id = ...
  steps      = [
    octopusdeploy_process_templated_step.process_step_slack_notification_start[0].id,
    octopusdeploy_process_templated_step.process_step_slack_notification_finish[0].id,
    octopusdeploy_process_templated_step.process_step_slack_notification_complete[0].id,
    octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_review_template[0].id,          # ← CORRECT: last
  ]
}
```

* `condition = "Variable"` requires "Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" in "properties", and it must be NON-EMPTY (`"Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" = ""` is rejected by Octopus).
* If the prompt asks for a variable run condition with no expression, or an explicitly empty one, do NOT emit `condition = "Variable"`: set `condition = "Success"`, omit the property, and note the shortfall in `notes`. (Observed: `Add a Bash step called "Empty Guard" whose run condition is a variable expression, with an empty expression`.)
* Octopus validates only non-emptiness, not Octostache well-formedness (e.g. `#{if Payout.Enabled}true` with no closing `#{/if}` is accepted and fails silently at deploy time). Copy the prompt's expression verbatim anyway.

**CRITICAL: copy a prompt-supplied run-condition expression VERBATIM into `Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression`; never replace it with a constant.** E.g. `Add a script step called "Purge CDN" that only runs when the variable expression "#{unless Octopus.Deployment.Error}True#{/unless}" evaluates to true`:
```hcl
properties = {
  "Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" = "#{unless Octopus.Deployment.Error}True#{/unless}"
}
```
* Applies to any quoted Octostache: `#{if ...}`, `#{unless ...}`, `#{Variable == "value"}`, bare `#{MyVariable}`. Reproduce the characters between the quotes.
* Only invent an expression when the condition is described in prose without a quote (e.g. "run this step only when the deployment has failed" → `#{if Octopus.Deployment.Error}True#{/if}`).
* `"True"` (or `"False"`) is never a valid expression — `"True"` means always run, even after failure. Either write the prompt's expression or use `condition = "Success"` without the property.
* Self-check: literally search output for `Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression`; any value not containing `#{` is wrong — replace with the prompt's expression, or if none quoted, remove the property and set `condition` to `"Success"` (or `"Failure"` / `"Always"` per prompt). Don't assume template-derived values are correct.

* Adding a step needs a new "octopusdeploy_process_step" resource plus its ID added to the "steps" array of "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order". E.g. adding "Deploy an Azure Web App (Web Deploy)" after "Validate setup":

```
resource "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" "process_step_order_azure_web_app" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_web_app.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  process_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_web_app.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_azure_web_app[0].id}"
  # The new step is added to the steps array
  steps      = [
  "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_web_app.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_azure_web_app_validate_setup[0].id}",
  "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_web_app.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_azure_web_app_deploy_web_app[0].id}"
  ]
}

# This is the new step being added
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_azure_web_app_deploy_web_app" {
  count                 = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_web_app.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                  = "Deploy Web App (Web Deploy)"
  # ...
}
```

* For "octopusdeploy_process_step" / "octopusdeploy_process_child_step":
  * "name": only letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores, or hashes; unique across the project.
  * "slug": only lower case letters, numbers, or dashes; unique across the project; derived from "name" as "step-name" (lowercase, spaces → dashes), collapse consecutive dashes, and strip leading/trailing dashes (never e.g. "start-" or "-deploy"). `"Start (main)"` → `"start--main-"` → `"start-main"`; `"Deploy (Manifest)"` → `"deploy-manifest"`.
  * No asterisk placeholders (e.g. "*****") in "slug" or "name".
* **CRITICAL:** A numeric deduplication suffix (` 2`, ` 3`) in a step name must appear in both the Terraform resource name (`_2`) and the `slug` (`-2`): "Rollback -internal- 2" → resource suffix `rollback_internal_2`, slug `rollback-internal-2`. Omitting it causes duplicate resource names/slugs.

E.g. `process_step_my_project_rollback_internal` (`"Rollback -internal-"`, `"rollback-internal"`) and `process_step_my_project_rollback_internal_2` (`"Rollback -internal- 2"`, `"rollback-internal-2"`).

* A "octopusdeploy_process_step" or "octopusdeploy_process_child_step" "name" must never contain characters other than letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores, or hashes (in particular never "/" or "\").
* Target tags must only be defined in the "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" property of a "octopusdeploy_process_step" step-level `properties` block, e.g. `properties = { "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" = "AzureWebApp" }`.

**CRITICAL: an `Octopus.AzureWebApp` (and `Octopus.AzureAppService`) step MUST set `"Octopus.Action.Azure.WebAppName"` in `execution_properties`**, or the API rejects it — unless your output also creates an Azure Web App deployment target resolving the app.
* Prefer a variable expression; if the prompt creates a variable holding the app name (e.g. `Portal.AppServiceName`), the value MUST be `"#{Portal.AppServiceName}"`.
* If the prompt names no web app variable, create a `String` project variable `Azure.WebAppName` and reference it, or use the project name slug as a literal.
* Also set `"Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupName"`, e.g. `"#{Azure.ResourceGroupName}"`, creating that variable if the prompt supplies none.
* Never add `"Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId"` to an `Octopus.AzureWebApp` step, even if the prompt names an Azure account; create the account as a top-level resource and reference it from a project variable such as `Project.Azure.Account`.

**CRITICAL: an `Octopus.AzureWebApp` (and `Octopus.AzureAppService`) step MUST ALSO set a non-empty `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` in its STEP-LEVEL `properties`, EVEN WHEN THE PROMPT NEVER MENTIONS A TARGET OR TAG.** Independent of `WebAppName` (different block); set both in the same edit.
* If the prompt names no target tag, invent one — `"AzureWebApp"` is conventional. "The prompt did not mention a target" is never a reason to omit it.

**Self-check:** check each `type = "Octopus.AzureWebApp"`/`type = "Octopus.AzureAppService"` step SEPARATELY for non-empty `WebAppName` (execution_properties) and `TargetRoles` (properties); `properties = {}` is always wrong.

* Never use target tags in "name=value" format in the "target_roles" attribute.
* Never add the "target_roles" attribute on an "action".
* Never add the "excluded_environments" attribute on a "step".
* Never add target tags to the "tenant_tags" attribute.
* **A target tag is NOT a tenant tag and MUST NOT get an `octopusdeploy_tag_set` or `octopusdeploy_tag` resource.** Target tags (roles) are free-form strings, declared nowhere, existing only in a step's `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"` and a target's `roles` list. `octopusdeploy_tag_set`/`octopusdeploy_tag` are TENANT tag sets, created ONLY to support a `tenant_tags` value such as `"Regions/US-East"`. A tag set such as `Target Tag Set` containing a target tag (e.g. `gpu-pool`, `insight-cluster`, `tomcat-server`) is always wrong; an untenanted project must contain no tag sets. If a target tag is the only "tag" mentioned, output ZERO `octopusdeploy_tag_set` resources.

* When "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" is "Package", define a `primary_package` block and "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName" (e.g. `"MyScript.ps1"`), and do not define "Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax" or "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody".
* When "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" is "Inline", do not define "primary_package" on the "octopusdeploy_process_step".

* If the prompt specifies both an inline script and a script from a package: ignore the package, define no "primary_package", define only the inline script in "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" in "execution_properties", set "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" to "Inline", and exclude "Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax".
* Never define a script step with no script.

**CRITICAL: a "run a script from a package" step MUST set `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Package"`.** (`ScriptSource` defaults to `Inline`; package + no body is rejected.) E.g. `Add a script step called "Warm Cache" that runs a script from the package "com.octopus:octopub-frontend" retrieved from the "Octopub Maven" feed`.
* The phrases "runs a script from the package", "run a script from a package", "execute the script in the package", or "script from a package" ALWAYS mean this shape, requiring ALL THREE:
  1. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Package"` in `execution_properties`
  2. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName"` naming the script file in the package — mandatory, never empty; invent a plausible name (e.g. `deploy.sh` for Bash, `Deploy.ps1` for PowerShell) if not given.
  3. a `primary_package` block — NOT a named `packages` map — giving `feed_id` and `package_id`
  and NO `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"` or `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax"`.
* An **inline** script that merely *references* a package ("add a reference package to the script step") is the mirror image: named `packages` map, `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`, and a non-empty `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"`.
* **CRITICAL:** `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName"` is ONLY valid for `Octopus.Script` steps (`type = "Octopus.Script"`) — see the `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` rule below.

**CRITICAL: third shape `ScriptSource = "GitRepository"` requires `ScriptFileName` and FORBIDS `Script.Syntax`**. When a step's script "comes from a Git repository", set:
  1. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository"`,
  2. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName"` to the prompt's path (e.g. `scripts/read.sh`), inventing one only if none given,
  3. **NO `"Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax"`** (the extension determines syntax — opposite of inline, where `Syntax` is required). A word like "Bash" in the prompt (e.g. `Add a Bash script step called "Read Config" whose script comes from the Git repository "…" on branch "main" at the path "scripts/read.sh"`) describes the file; don't set `Syntax = "Bash"`.
  4. no `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"`.
* If a Git-sourced script is requested but no repository is named, emit an ordinary inline `Octopus.Script` step with a placeholder body and record the missing repository in `notes`; never `ScriptSource = "GitRepository"` with an empty repository.

**Self-check (script steps):** for every `Octopus.Script`, `Octopus.AwsRunScript`, `Octopus.AzurePowerShell`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` or `Octopus.GcpRunScript` step with a `primary_package` or `packages` map, `execution_properties` must match exactly one shape:
1. `ScriptSource = "Package"` **and** `ScriptFileName` set **and** no `ScriptBody`;
2. `ScriptSource = "Inline"` **and** non-empty `ScriptBody`;
3. `ScriptSource = "GitRepository"` **and** `ScriptFileName` set **and** no `ScriptBody` **and** no `Syntax`.

* Steps of type "Octopus.AzureAppService" must have at least one item in the "target_roles" attribute.

**CRITICAL: TARGET-BASED step types MUST carry a non-empty `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"` in their STEP-LEVEL `properties` block**. They run ON a deployment target (every occurrence in the `everystep.tf` example configuration has one):
`Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.WindowsService`, `Octopus.JavaArchive`, `Octopus.TentaclePackage`, `Octopus.AzureWebApp`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize`, `Octopus.TomcatDeploy`, `Octopus.TransferPackage`.
* The tag goes in step-level `properties`, NOT `execution_properties`, NOT a `target_roles` attribute on the step resource. A worker pool is NOT a substitute.
* **CRITICAL: do NOT put a worker pool (`worker_pool_id` / `worker_pool_variable`) on a target-based step** (Octopus flips `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer`, aborting the apply). If the prompt asks a target-based step to run on a named worker pool (e.g. `Add a "Deploy Java Archive" step called "Stage Archive" … * Create a worker pool called "Reporting Pool" and run the "Stage Archive" step on it`), IGNORE the pool for that step, keep the target tag, and note it in the step's `notes`. Still create the `octopusdeploy_static_worker_pool` if requested, and apply it to steps that legitimately run on a worker (`Octopus.Script` with `RunOnServer = "true"`, `Octopus.Aws*`, `Octopus.Terraform*`, `Octopus.AzurePowerShell`, Kubernetes steps using `worker_pool_variable`).
* If a target-based step must nevertheless carry a worker pool, state `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"` explicitly in its `execution_properties`.
* When the prompt names one target tag for the project (e.g. "deploy to targets tagged `reporting-tomcat`"), apply it to EVERY target-based step unless a step is given a different tag.
* Server/worker step types — `Octopus.Manual`, `Octopus.Email`, `Octopus.DeployRelease`, `Octopus.Terraform*`, `Octopus.Aws*`, `Octopus.AzurePowerShell`, `Octopus.ArgoCD*`, `Octopus.Claude` — get no target tag unless the prompt asks. `Octopus.Script` takes one only when the prompt says it runs against targets.

* Steps of type "Octopus.AwsUploadS3" must have a "Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable" property in "execution_properties".
* Steps of these types must have a "primary_package" block and must NOT have a "packages" block: "Octopus.IIS", "Octopus.AzureWebApp", "Octopus.TentaclePackage", "Octopus.WindowsService", "Octopus.AwsUploadS3", "Octopus.JavaArchive", "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade", "Octopus.AzureAppService".

**CRITICAL: a prompt naming no package is NOT permission to omit `primary_package` from these types**, e.g. `Add a step to each project that deploys the Azure Web App using the "Billing Azure" account`. Supply one: prefer the package the project type's example configuration uses; otherwise use the built-in feed with a package id derived from the project name. (correct behaviour, not fabrication):

```hcl
primary_package = {
  acquisition_location = "Server"
  feed_id              = "${data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_server__built_in_.feeds[0].id}"
  id                   = null
  package_id           = "BillingApi"
  properties           = { SelectionMode = "immediate" }
}
```

* "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade" steps must define "Octopus.Action.Helm.ClientVersion" = "V3".
* **CRITICAL:** Never include `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName` in an `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` step's `execution_properties` unless the prompt explicitly requests a specific script file — even if it has `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Package"`. Canonical Helm execution properties are limited to `"Octopus.Action.Helm.ClientVersion"`, `"Octopus.Action.Helm.ResetValues"`, `"Octopus.Action.Helm.Namespace"`, `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"`, `"OctopusUseBundledTooling"`, and `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"` (plus e.g. `"Octopus.Action.Helm.ReleaseName"`). The "Mock K8s" compatibility properties `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck"`, `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled"` belong to "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" steps ONLY, never Helm.
* Never define a "version" attribute on the "primary_package" block.

**CRITICAL: `primary_package.acquisition_location` MUST be `"Server"`.** Octopus normalises it to `Server`, so `"NotAcquired"` (or `"ExecutionTarget"`) aborts the apply.
* `"NotAcquired"` is correct ONLY for a **container image reference** in a named `packages` map (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.AzureWebApp` container deployments, `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags`), whose entry also has `properties = { Extract = "False", Purpose = "DockerImageReference", SelectionMode = "immediate" }`.
* When converting a named container-image package (e.g. `packages = { octopub-selfcontained = { acquisition_location = "NotAcquired", … } }`) into a `primary_package`, change it to `"Server"` — or better, leave the template's `packages` map as is. Don't "fix" it by removing the package; the listed types still require a `primary_package`.
* Self-check: every `primary_package` has `acquisition_location = "Server"`.

**CRITICAL: a package-deployment step accepts EXACTLY ONE package, supplied as `primary_package`, and REJECTS the named `packages` map outright (apply dies).** Verified against the Octopus API for `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` and `Octopus.IIS`:

Error: `This deployment step does not support named package references`.

* Applies to the single-package-slot package-deployment family: `Octopus.TomcatDeploy`, `Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.WindowsService`, `Octopus.AzureWebApp` (non-container) and similar single-artefact steps; a named reference has nowhere to go.
* **If the prompt asks for several packages on ONE such step:** keep the FIRST listed package as `primary_package`; give each remaining package its OWN step of the same type; record in `notes` that packages were split because the step type supports only one. Do NOT convert to `Octopus.Script` (discards deployment behaviour). Do NOT silently drop packages.
* Observed failure: `Add a single step called "Deploy Billing" that deploys ALL THREE of these packages together on that one step` → one `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` step with three `packages` entries; apply aborted.
* Only script/container types (`Octopus.Script`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags` and the like) support the named `packages` map / more than one package.
* The restriction is on the reference NAME, not the package id: a Maven coordinate like `com.octopus:billing-portal` is a valid `package_id` on a primary package.
* Define "start_trigger" only once. Never define an empty "properties" block ("properties = {}"), a "properties_additional" block, or multiple "features" attributes.
* "container" = the step's "Container Image". Include it on any "octopusdeploy_process_step" that has one in the sample Terraform configurations. Prompts about a "Container Image" define/edit "container", never the "packages" block. Required properties: "feed_id", "image". Write it with "=" (`container = { ... }`). E.g. prompt sets "Container Image" to "octopuslabs/k8s-workertools" on a step whose package is "nginx" → set `container` `image` (with a `feed_id` ternary on a feed such as `feed_docker_hub`), leave `packages` unmodified.
* Never set "container" when `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "false"`.
* Steps of type "Octopus.AzureWebApp" and "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" must have at least one role in the "target_roles" array.
* "Octopus.DeployRelease": "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId" must reference a data "octopusdeploy_projects" project ID (e.g. `"${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_project.projects[0].id}"`), never a string like "Projects-#".
* Steps of type "Octopus.AwsRunScript" must define `"Octopus.Action.Aws.Region"` (e.g. "us-east-1").

* **CRITICAL — "Octopus.Claude" steps MUST define BOTH `Octopus.Action.Claude.ApiKey` and `Octopus.Action.Claude.SandboxMode`** in `execution_properties`; each is API-enforced and omission aborts the apply (`"There was a problem with your request. [An API Key is required.]"` / `"Unable to create process step"`; `[A sandboxing option must be selected.]`). Applies regardless of other accounts (e.g. AWS/Bedrock) requested, and to any step described as "uses AI to …", "assess … with AI", or "run the Claude agent", however named.
  * API key: create sensitive project variable `Project.Claude.ApiKey` (default `"Change Me!"`), set `"Octopus.Action.Claude.ApiKey" = "#{Project.Claude.ApiKey}"` (e.g. `octopusdeploy_variable` "claude_project_claude_apikey_1": `type = "Sensitive"`, `is_sensitive = true`, `sensitive_value = "Change Me!"`). Example step also sets `"Octopus.Action.Claude.Model" = "claude-sonnet-5"`, `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`.
  * SandboxMode: only `"None"` or `"SandboxRuntime"`. Use `"None"` unless the prompt explicitly asks for a sandboxed agent. `"SandboxRuntime"` also needs `Octopus.Action.Claude.SandboxSettings` (a `jsonencode({...})` of `network` and `filesystem` rules); if unsure, use `"None"`.

* **CRITICAL — RunOnServer: any step with no `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` property MUST set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`**, in every project type (not just blue/green/progressive swap, cutover and "health check gate" steps; also LLM/Claude/orchestration steps like `Perform Deployment`, `List Commits`, `Extract JSON`, whatever the name). Octopus forces server execution, so `"false"` causes `Provider produced inconsistent result after apply` and no project is created. Steps that DO set `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` may use `"false"`; don't blanket-set `"true"` on them. Self-check: check every deployment-process and runbook step before responding.

* **CRITICAL — "Scan for Vulnerabilities" community step template steps MUST set `parameters` entry `"Sbom.Package" = jsonencode({ "FeedId" = "<feed id>", "PackageId" = "<package id>" })`**, else `"There was a problem with your request. [Package parameter 'Sbom.Package' is missing]"` / `"Unable to create process step"`. If the prompt names no package, reuse the project's deployment package (or the template default, e.g. the Maven feed with `com.octopus:octopub-sbom`).

* **CRITICAL — Argo CD "Wait for Argo CD Applications" steps (e.g. "wait for Argo CD to report Healthy") MUST set the expected-number-of-apps property `>= 1`** (default `1`); `0` fails with `"There was a problem with your request. [Please provide an expected number of apps greater than 0.]"` / `"Unable to create process step"`.

**CRITICAL — Terraform steps using an AWS account MUST set `Octopus.Action.Aws.Region`** (`Octopus.TerraformPlan`, `Octopus.TerraformApply`, `Octopus.TerraformPlanAndApply`, `Octopus.TerraformDestroy`, `Octopus.TerraformPlanDestroy`). Setting `Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable` or `"Octopus.Action.Terraform.ManagedAccount" = "AWS"` enables the AWS feature, which fails without a region (`[Please specify the AWS region.]`); the Terraform example has no AWS properties to copy. When the prompt says "create an AWS account … and reference it from the Terraform steps" or names an AWS region, set on EVERY Terraform step (runbooks included): `"Octopus.Action.Terraform.ManagedAccount" = "AWS"`, `"Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable" = "AWS.Terraform.Account"`, `"Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.UseInstanceRole" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Aws.Region" = "us-east-1"`.

* Use the prompt's region; if an AWS account is named but no region, default to `"us-east-1"`.
* `Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable` takes the NAME of a project variable (type `AmazonWebServicesAccount`), not an account ID or `#{...}` reference.
* Azure equivalent: `Octopus.Action.AzureAccount.Variable`; Google Cloud: `Octopus.Action.GoogleCloudAccount.Variable`.
* Self-check: every `Octopus.Terraform*` step with `Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable` has a non-empty `Octopus.Action.Aws.Region`.

* **CRITICAL — never define a "primary_package" block when `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`** (incl. "Octopus.AwsRunScript"); the API fails with "Providing a package is not valid for inline scripts." Correct inline AWS Lambda deploy:
```hcl
  type  = "Octopus.AwsRunScript"
  # NO primary_package when using an inline script
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"        = "Inline"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"          = "aws lambda update-function-code --function-name my-function --zip-file fileb://function.zip"
    "Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable"        = "Project.AWS.Account"
    "Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.UseInstanceRole" = "False"
    "Octopus.Action.Aws.Region"                 = "us-east-1"
    "Octopus.Action.Aws.AssumeRole"             = "False"
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"                = "true"
    "OctopusUseBundledTooling"                  = "False"
  }
```
* "deploy an AWS Lambda function" with a named package → TWO steps: (1) "Upload Lambda", type `"Octopus.AwsUploadS3"` with `primary_package` uploading the zip to S3; (2) "Deploy Lambda", type `"Octopus.AwsRunScript"` (inline, NO `primary_package`) calling `aws lambda update-function-code` with the S3 bucket/key.

* "Octopus.AzureResourceGroup" and "Octopus.AzurePowerShell" steps must define "Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId", never an empty string:
```
"Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId" = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_azure.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_azure.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect.account_azure[0].id}"
```
* If the prompt specifies no Azure account, create an "octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect" account with default settings and assign it.
* **CRITICAL — if the prompt names an Azure account, ALL Azure steps must reference it, not the template's `account_octopussamples_azure_account`.** E.g. 'Add an Azure OIDC account named "Azure Production Account"':
```hcl
"Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId" = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_azure_production_account.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_azure_production_account.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect.account_azure_production_account[0].id}"
```
(WRONG: still using `data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_octopussamples_azure_account` / `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal.account_octopussamples_azure_account`.)

* "Octopus.Script" steps must define `"OctopusUseBundledTooling" = "False"`.
* Orchestration/orcestrator projects: "Octopus.DeployRelease" steps deploy child projects.
* **CRITICAL — every child project you create for an orchestration project MUST have a deployment process with at least one step.** When the prompt names child projects that don't exist (e.g. "Add a step that deploys a release of the child project 'Retail Storefront'"), create each child project *and* its process. If the prompt doesn't say what it does, give it one `Octopus.Script` step named "Run a Script" with a short inline script, like the example orchestration configuration.
* Each child project needs a `lifecycle_id`, a `project_group_id`, and its own `octopusdeploy_process` and `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` (not shared with the parent).
* Each "Octopus.DeployRelease" step needs "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId" and a "primary_package" whose "package_id" is the child project ID, both from a data "octopusdeploy_projects" data source only. Example (`project_every_step_project` = parent lookup):

```
data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_octopus_server_releases__built_in_" {
  feed_type    = "OctopusProject"
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Octopus Server Releases"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_every_step_project_deploy_a_release" {
  count                 = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  type                  = "Octopus.DeployRelease"
  process_id            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_every_step_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_every_step_project[0].id}"
  primary_package       = {
    acquisition_location = "NotAcquired",
    feed_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_server_releases__built_in_.feeds[0].id}",
    id = null,
    package_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_project.projects[0].id}",
    properties = null
  }
  execution_properties  = {
        "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"
        "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.DeploymentCondition" = "Always"
        "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId" = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_project.projects[0].id}"
      }
  # ...
}
```

* `Set the "Wait for deployment to complete" property to true` → `"Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.WaitForDeployment" = "True"`; `Set the "Wait for deployment to complete" property to false` → `"Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.WaitForDeployment" = "False"`; unspecified → default `"True"`.

**CRITICAL — `Octopus.DeployRelease` step name fallback**: when the prompt says "Add a 'Deploy a Release' step to the deployment process" WITHOUT "with the name 'X'", use the child project name X from "Set the 'Project' property to X" as `name` (e.g. "Set the 'Project' property to 'Deploy Development Pipeline'." → `name = "Deploy Development Pipeline"`, `slug = "deploy-development-pipeline"`), NOT `"Deploy a Release"`. Never emit generic names like "Deploy a Release", "Deploy a Release 2".

**CRITICAL — parallel**: `Set the start trigger to "Run in parallel with the previous step"` → `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`. For several parallel "Deploy a Release" steps, the FIRST gets `start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"`, all subsequent ones `"StartWithPrevious"`.

**CRITICAL — one `data "octopusdeploy_projects"` lookup per child project** (N steps → N data sources, `partial_name` = child project name); never share one lookup across children.

* Never set "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId" or "package_id" to a data/resource ternary like `length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_reporting_microservice.projects) != 0 ? ... : octopusdeploy_project.project_reporting_microservice[0].id`, or ProjectId to fixed values like "Projects-8916", "Projects-8915", "Projects-8917". Never set `primary_package` "feed_id" to fixed values like "Feeds-5936", "Feeds-5678", "Feeds-91011".
* Do not recreate the demo-only data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_child_project" or resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_child_project".
* Always include `data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_octopus_server_releases__built_in_"`; every "Octopus.DeployRelease" `primary_package` "feed_id" must use it.

WRONG patterns: one shared `data "octopusdeploy_projects"` for several children; omitting `count`; `process_id = "${octopusdeploy_process.process_orchestrator[0].id}"` without the ternary count guard; omitting `execution_properties` `Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId`.

The **CORRECT** pattern for N child deployments requires N separate data source lookups, one per child project:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_child_a" {
  ids = null; partial_name = "Child A"; skip = 0; take = 1
}
data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_child_b" {
  ids = null; partial_name = "Child B"; skip = 0; take = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_deploy_child_a" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_orchestrator.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name       = "Deploy Child A"
  type       = "Octopus.DeployRelease"
  process_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_orchestrator.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_orchestrator[0].id}"
  primary_package = {
    acquisition_location = "NotAcquired",
    feed_id    = "${data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_server_releases__built_in_.feeds[0].id}",
    id         = null,
    package_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_a.projects[0].id}",
    properties = null
  }
  start_trigger        = "StartAfterPrevious"
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId"            = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_a.projects[0].id}"
    "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.DeploymentCondition"  = "Always"
    "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.WaitForDeployment"    = "True"
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"                        = "true"
  }
  # ... other required attributes
}
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_deploy_child_b" {
  name       = "Deploy Child B"
  # ... same as above, but:
  primary_package = {
    # ...
    package_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_b.projects[0].id}",   # separate data source
  }
  start_trigger        = "StartAfterPrevious"   # or "StartWithPrevious" if parallel
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId"            = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_child_b.projects[0].id}"
    # ...
  }
}
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — orchestrator project completeness** (fix every NO):
1. Exactly ONE `data "octopusdeploy_projects"` per child project?
2. `data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_octopus_server_releases__built_in_"` included?
3. Each step's `"Octopus.Action.DeployRelease.ProjectId"` uses the CHILD project's data source (NOT the orchestrator's)?
4. Each step's `primary_package.package_id` uses the correct, unique per-child data source?
5. Each step is in the `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` `steps` list?
6. Each step has a UNIQUE, DESCRIPTIVE `name` derived from the child project name (e.g. `name = "Deploy Development Pipeline"`), not "Deploy a Release", "Deploy a Release 2", etc.?
7. `slug` = kebab-case of `name` (e.g. `slug = "deploy-development-pipeline"`)?

* Only define "execution_properties" and "properties" attributes that appear in the supplied examples. In particular, never define properties starting with "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation" that are not in the examples, and never define "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation.Template", "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation.TemplateFile", "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation.TagsEnabled", "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation.StackName", "Octopus.Action.Manual.ResponsibleUserIds", "Octopus.Action.Manual.Roles", or "Octopus.Step.Manual.Instructions".
* Never set "Octopus.Action.Manual.ResponsibleTeamIds" to an empty string.
* "execution_properties" and "properties" must each be a single map with an equals sign: `execution_properties = { ... }`. Never define multiple "execution_properties" or "properties" blocks in one resource, never use block syntax without "=" (`execution_properties {` / `properties {`), and never use a `name`/`value` block form:
```
execution_properties {
  name  = "<some name>"
  value = "<some value>"
}
```
* Each "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" resource must be guarded by its corresponding "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" data source:
```
data "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" "my_community_step_template" {
  website = "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/<guid goes here>"
}

resource "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" "my_community_step_template" {
  community_action_template_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.my_community_step_template.steps) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.my_community_step_template.steps[0].id : null}"
  count                        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.my_community_step_template.steps) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
}
```
* Never reference an "octopusdeploy_step_template" data source "actions" property (e.g. `count = "${length(try(data.octopusdeploy_step_template.my_step_template.step_template.actions, [])) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`).
* Never define both "environments" and "excluded_environments" on an "octopusdeploy_process_step" or "octopusdeploy_process_child_step"; if the prompt gives both includes and excludes, define only "environments".
* Escape `${` as `$${` in "Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupTemplate" and "Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormationTemplate" values, e.g.:
```
"Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupTemplate" = "resource storageAccount 'Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts@2021-04-01' = {\n  name: 'analytics$${uniqueString(resourceGroup().id)}'
```
  (CloudFormation: `!Sub '$${AWS::StackName}-function'`, `!Sub '$${Project.AWS.Lambda.S3.BucketName}'`, `DB_HOST: $${DB_HOST}`.)
* "Octopus.Script" steps with no target roles must set "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" to "true".
* Never set the "container" "image" to an empty string. If the prompt wants a container image but names none, or "container" has a "feed_id" but the prompt specifies no image, use "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04".
* Additional packages ("referenced packages") go in the "packages" block of an "octopusdeploy_process_step"; always emit the complete block (never just its tail, e.g. `", id = null, package_id = "mypackage", properties = { SelectionMode = "immediate" } }`):
```
packages = {
    package-id = {
        acquisition_location = "NotAcquired",
        feed_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_ghcr_anonymous.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_ghcr_anonymous.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_ghcr_anonymous[0].id}",
        id = null,
        package_id = "${var.project_kubernetes_web_app_step_deploy_a_kubernetes_web_app_via_yaml_package_octopub_selfcontained_packageid}",
        properties = {
            Extract = "False",
            Purpose = "DockerImageReference",
            SelectionMode = "immediate"
        }
    }
}
```
* Likewise never emit an incomplete "primary_package" block (e.g. a truncated line `", id = null, package_id = "${var.project_serverless_api_9231_step_deploy_lambda_packageid}", properties = { SelectionMode = "immediate" } }` in place of `primary_package = { ... }`).
* Never define empty-string values in "execution_properties".
* Never define "target_roles" on an "octopusdeploy_process_step" or "octopusdeploy_process_child_step".
* Custom scripts not provided by the examples or the prompt: write only comments describing the intended functionality plus an `echo`/`Write-Host` placeholder statement, or a single comment in "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" for runbooks/steps not in the template (e.g. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" = "# This is a placeholder script that would swap load balancers"`). Never generate complex or example scripts. When the prompt provides script code, recreate it exactly.
* Client side apply = disabling server side apply: set "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled" = "False", only on "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" steps (never on "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript" or "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade").
* "Octopus.Manual" steps must set:
  * "Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions", NON-EMPTY (empty fails: `Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [Please provide instructions to display during the step.]`). If the prompt gives empty/blank/unspecified instructions (e.g. `Add a manual intervention step called "Approve Settlement" with completely empty instructions`), write a sensible default such as `"Do you approve the deployment?"` and note in `notes` that the prompt supplied none. Never emit `"Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions" = ""` or omit it.
  * `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`.
  * `"Octopus.Action.Manual.BlockConcurrentDeployments" = "True"` (always; omission lets concurrent deployments skip the gate).

**CRITICAL — `Octopus.Action.Manual.ResponsibleTeamIds` may only be `"teams-everyone"` (Everyone), `"teams-administrators"` (Octopus Administrators) or `"teams-managers"` (Octopus Managers).** Never slugify a prompt team name (e.g. `teams-release-managers` for "Release Managers"): other teams have unknowable `Teams-123` IDs, and a fabricated value silently leaves nobody able to approve. For any other team, omit the property and name the team in the instructions. Don't create an `octopusdeploy_team` unless the prompt explicitly asks.
```hcl
execution_properties = {
  "Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions"              = "Approval required from the Release Managers team. Do you approve the release to production?"
  "Octopus.Action.Manual.BlockConcurrentDeployments" = "True"
  "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"                      = "true"
}
```
* Preserve prompt-defined manual-intervention instructions exactly: don't rewrite `${ ... }` SpEL into `#{...}`, normalize punctuation, or strip/replace Unicode. If the prompt appends a NOTE about SpEL expressions, include it verbatim at the end of the same string; don't "fix" embedded expressions.
* **CRITICAL — escape `${...}` as `$${...}` in `Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions`** (e.g. `release: *${trigger['tag']}* (NOTE: ...)` → `"Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions" = "release: *$${trigger['tag']}* (NOTE: ...)"`). Octostache `#{...}` needs no escaping.
* `jsonencode({})` values must not be followed by a quote or newline: `"Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupTemplateParameters" = jsonencode({})`, not `"Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupTemplateParameters" = jsonencode({})"`.
* Never define "Octopus.Action.Aws.S3.BucketName" on an "Octopus.AwsRunCloudFormation" step.
* Every "tenant_tags" item must reference a tag in a resource "octopusdeploy_tag" and its tag set in a resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set".
* Step template and community step template steps must use "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" (never "octopusdeploy_process_step"), with no "packages" block.
* "Octopus.Script" steps use "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptFileName", never "Octopus.Action.Script.FileName".
* If the prompt says to retain the original sample steps, emit them in full, never comments like "# ... include all original Azure Web App steps here, in order".
* Every step in "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" must be defined as a resource ("octopusdeploy_process_step" or "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step").
* Recreate prompt container/docker image names exactly (e.g. "octopusdeploylabs/azure-workertools" must not become "octopuslabs/azure-workertools" or "octopusdeploylabs/azure-workertools:latest").
* Step names must be unique within a process.
* "notes": keep quotes balanced and emit the whole value, never half. Recreate long sample "notes" exactly, including line breaks and special characters. Notes may contain markdown, e.g. `notes = "Here is a code block\n\n```\n# This is some code in a code block\n```"`.
* "start_trigger" must be "StartWithPrevious" ("Run in parallel with the previous step" in the UI) or "StartAfterPrevious" ("Wait for all previous steps to complete, then start").
* **CRITICAL — fork-without-reconvergence** (parallel group of branch roots, then Branch A continuation steps, then Branch B continuation steps with a migration NOTE): `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` `steps` must list ALL Branch A continuation steps BEFORE the first Branch B continuation step, never interleaved, exactly following the prompt's order, even though both carry `start_trigger = "StartAfterPrevious"`.
* "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" with "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository" must define "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYamlFileName" (default "custom-resource.yaml").
* **CRITICAL — `Set the File Paths to "<path>"`** on a "Deploy Kubernetes YAML" step with YAML source "Files from a Git repository": set `file_path_filters = ["<path>"]` in `git_dependencies` AND `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYamlFileName = "<path>"`, same exact value. `file_path_filters = null` is forbidden when a path is given. E.g. `Set the File Paths to "resource-0172".`:
```
git_dependencies = { "" = { default_branch = "master", file_path_filters = ["resource-0172"], git_credential_type = "Anonymous", repository_uri = "https://example.invalid/url-0234" } }
execution_properties = {
  "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYamlFileName" = "resource-0172"
  "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository"
  ...
}
```
* `Set the step namespace to "<namespace>".` on "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" → `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.Namespace" = "<namespace>"`. If no namespace is given, omit the property (never an empty string).
* **CRITICAL — inline `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` steps MUST include** (unless the prompt explicitly says client-side apply): `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"`, `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml"` (inline YAML or a `# TODO:` placeholder), `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`, `"OctopusUseBundledTooling" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.ForceConflicts" = "True"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck"`, `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.DeploymentTimeout" = "180"`.
  * **CRITICAL:** with the "Mock K8s" target (every new project prompt without a real cluster URL; use it if unsure) set `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait" = "NoWait"` and `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled" = "False"`, as the CRITICAL rule at the top of this file requires. The examples' `"True"` values are for real clusters only.
* **CRITICAL — every `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` step (enabled or disabled/TODO, Inline or Git-sourced) MUST have a non-empty `container` block** — never `container = { feed_id = "", image = "" }` (most common mistake; fails at runtime):
```hcl
container = {
  feed_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_github_container_registry.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_github_container_registry.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_github_container_registry[0].id}"
  image = "ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/k8s-workertools"
}
```
* **CRITICAL — any project with a `KubernetesDeployRawYaml` step MUST define both `data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_github_container_registry"` and `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry "feed_github_container_registry"`** (even if no artifact uses the feed), and the resource MUST have `count` (never unguarded with a direct `.id` reference; the ternary's `[0]` index requires count), `username = null`, `password = null`, and `lifecycle { ignore_changes = [password]; prevent_destroy = true }`:
```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_github_container_registry" {
  feed_type    = "Docker"
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "GitHub Container Registry"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" "feed_github_container_registry" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_github_container_registry.feeds) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "GitHub Container Registry"
  feed_uri    = "https://ghcr.io"
  api_version = "v2"
  username    = null
  password    = null
  package_acquisition_location_options = ["ExecutionTarget", "NotAcquired"]
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [password]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

**CRITICAL — "Add a 'Deploy Kubernetes YAML' step" MUST produce `type = "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml"`**, never `"Octopus.Script"`, `"Octopus.KubernetesRunScript"` or another type, with YAML in `"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml"` (never `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"`) plus `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"` for inline, or `git_dependencies` for GitRepository. E.g. "Add a 'Deploy Kubernetes YAML' step named 'Restore Prod'.":
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_restore_prod" {
  type = "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml"
  # container = { ... } as above
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml" = <<-EOT
      apiVersion: batch/v1
      kind: Job
      ...
    EOT
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Inline"
  }
}
```


## Cloud Step Container Image Instructions

* **CRITICAL:** these step types MUST include a `container` block referencing the GitHub Container Registry feed and the worker-tools image for the type:
  * `Octopus.TerraformPlan`, `Octopus.TerraformApply`, `Octopus.TerraformDestroy`, `Octopus.TerraformPlanDestroy` — `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/terraform-workertools`
  * `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` — `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/azure-workertools`
  * `Octopus.AwsRunScript` and `Octopus.AwsCreateS3` — `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/aws-workertools`
  * `Octopus.GoogleCloudScripting` — `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/gcp-workertools`
  * `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` — `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/k8s-workertools`

```hcl
container = {
  feed_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_github_container_registry.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_github_container_registry.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_github_container_registry[0].id}"
  image   = "ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/terraform-workertools"
}
```

**CRITICAL:** `container` is the WORKER TOOLING image, NEVER the application image being deployed. E.g. for "use it for the image 'customer/portal'", `customer/portal` goes in the step's `packages` block (or package properties) with `feed_id` = the prompt's feed, not in `container`.
* `container.image` is ALWAYS a `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/*-workertools` image above (or `octopusdeploy/worker-tools:...` for a deployment target's health check container); `container.feed_id` is ALWAYS the GitHub Container Registry feed (or the anonymous "Docker Hub" feed for a target's container). Never the prompt's image/feed.
* Self-check: any `container` `image` that is not `*-workertools` (or the target's `octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04`) is an app image: move it to `packages`.
* When any Terraform, Azure PowerShell, AWS CLI, or GCP step is present, include both a `data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "feed_github_container_registry"` data source AND an `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry "feed_github_container_registry"` resource with the full idempotency pattern (as for Kubernetes steps).

* A step of type "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" with YAML source "Inline YAML" must have properly indented YAML in "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml". Copy prompt inline YAML verbatim (structure, list items, indentation are authoritative). Do not flatten nested keys, regenerate it, or remove `image`, `namespace`, `initContainers` or other fields. Never left-shift nested keys such as `labels`, `annotations`, `containers`, `env`, `ports`, `metrics`, or `scaleTargetRef` to their parent's column.
* Multi-document YAML (standalone `---` lines, e.g. a Deployment followed by a HorizontalPodAutoscaler): keep the ENTIRE payload in the single `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` value (never split into multiple resources/steps). Keep document order, each `---` on its own line, each document's top-level keys at column 0 and its own nested (2-space) indentation.
* When the prompt says `The step must be disabled.`, set `is_disabled = true` on the `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`. Do not omit the resource or reorder steps.
* **CRITICAL:** if `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` begins with `# TODO:`, set `is_disabled = true` on that step even if the prompt does not say "The step must be disabled." (`false` or omitted is wrong).
* **CRITICAL:** a step with `Set the YAML Source to "Files from a Git repository"` has a real git source, not a TODO: it MUST NOT have `is_disabled = true` unless the prompt explicitly says `The step must be disabled.`. Its `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource` MUST be `"GitRepository"`, NEVER `"Inline"`, even if `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` is also defined. E.g. step "Deploy -canary-" (no `is_disabled`):
```hcl
  git_dependencies = { "" = { default_branch = "main", file_path_filters = ["deploy-to-prod-canary.yaml"], git_credential_type = "Anonymous", repository_uri = "https://example.invalid/url-0034" } }
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"                          = "GitRepository"
    "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYamlFileName" = "deploy-to-prod-canary.yaml"
  }
```

Disabled TODO step (all required execution_properties):
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_run_job_manifest" {
  name                  = "Run Job -Manifest-"
  type                  = "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml"
  is_disabled           = true   # Required because the YAML content is a TODO placeholder
  container             = { feed_id = "..." /* GHCR feed as above */, image = "ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/k8s-workertools" }
  properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" = "Kubernetes"
  }
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml"   = "# TODO: replace with manifest downloaded from gs://example-bucket/path"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource"                       = "Inline"
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"                               = "true"
    "OctopusUseBundledTooling"                                 = "False"
    # "False"/"NoWait"/"False" because this project uses the "Mock K8s" target. Only a project
    # deployed against a real cluster may use "True" here.
    "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled"        = "False"
    "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.ForceConflicts" = "True"
    "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck"            = "False"
    "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait"       = "NoWait"
    "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.DeploymentTimeout"              = "180"
  }
}
```

* Never define "Octopus.Action.GitRepository.ExternalRepositoryUrl" or "Octopus.Action.GitRepository.FilePathFilters" on a step of type "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml".
* Step names must be unique; on a clash append a number ("Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML 2").
* Any "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" based on the community step template "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/99e6f203-3061-4018-9e34-4a3a9c3c3179" ("Slack - Send Simple Notification") must set "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true" in "execution_properties".
* **CRITICAL:** include `"ssn_Message"` only when the prompt provides it (`Set the "ssn_Message" property to "..."`); otherwise omit the key (not an empty string).
* **CRITICAL:** a Slack `parameters` block contains ONLY parameters the prompt mentions (e.g. `ssn_HookUrl`, `ssn_Channel`, `ssn_Message`). If the prompt only says `Set the "ssn_HookUrl" property to "..."` and `Set the "ssn_Channel" property to "..."`, the block holds ONLY `"ssn_HookUrl" = "#{Project.Slack.WebhookUrl}"` and `"ssn_Channel" = "my-channel"`. This also applies to PROSE prompts naming no `ssn_` key, e.g.

```
Add a community step template step called "Slack - Detailed Notification - Bash" as the final step, with the channel "#storefront-deploys"
```
  → `parameters` holds `ssn_Channel` and `ssn_HookUrl` and NOTHING else.
* Don't copy `everystep.tf`'s five Slack parameters wholesale (shape/spelling only). `ssn_IconUrl`, `ssn_Username` and `ssn_Color` are cosmetic and optional — omit them unless the prompt names an icon, a sender name, or a colour; never carry over the example's `https://octopus.com/content/resources/favicon.png`, `Octopus Deploy` or `good`.
* `ssn_HookUrl` is the ONLY exception: always include it (even if the prompt doesn't mention it), as an Octostache reference such as `"#{Project.Slack.WebhookUrl}"` with a matching sensitive project variable. Never write a literal webhook URL.
* For every community step template: a parameter whose VALUE the prompt gives (even in prose) is specified; one the prompt is silent about stays out of the map unless the template requires it (as `Sbom.Package` does for "Scan for Vulnerabilities").

**Substituting a community step template the prompt named but that does not exist**: do NOT drop the step — substitute the closest available template, record the substitution in `notes`, and keep `name` exactly as the prompt wrote it. E.g. `Slack - Detailed Notification - Bash` keeps that name with `template_id` pointing at `communitysteptemplate_slack___send_simple_notification`, plus a note.
* **CRITICAL:** maintain the prompt's step order in the "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" resource: add each step one by one, top to bottom and left to right (e.g. "Slack Notification - Start", then "Deploy user-profile worker -dev-", then "Deploy user-track worker -dev-", even when later steps "Run in parallel with the previous step").
* **CRITICAL:** correctly escape dollar signs and curly braces: e.g. `"ssn_Message" = "*Triggered by:* $${ trigger.user }\n*Container image:* $${ #triggerResolvedArtifactByType(\"docker/image\")[\"reference\"] }"`. Escape `${...}` SpEL as `$${...}` in ALL Terraform string values that aren't Terraform variables — `notes`, `"Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions"`, any `execution_properties` value.
* SpEL already converted to Octopus syntax (e.g. `#{Octopus.Release.Number}`) must NOT be re-escaped; `#{...}` needs no escaping. Only escape what remains in `${...}` form.
* **CRITICAL:** when `notes` derives from prompt text with paths/URLs like `at gs://...`, do not add quotation marks around the URL/path:
`notes = "... Google Cloud Storage at gs://my-bucket/path. The manifest must be inlined."`, not `at \"gs://my-bucket/path\"`.

## Email Step Instructions

* Steps of type "Octopus.Email" must set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` and must define `"Octopus.Action.Email.To"` (address or variable expression), `"Octopus.Action.Email.Subject"` and `"Octopus.Action.Email.Body"` in `execution_properties`. Optional: `"Octopus.Action.Email.Priority"` = `"Normal"` or `"High"`.
* Preserve Octopus variable syntax in prompt subject/body verbatim (e.g. `#{Octopus.Project.Name}`, `#{Octopus.Release.Number}`, `#{Octopus.Environment.Name}`); never replace with plain text.
* Email steps do NOT require `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"`; use `properties = {}`. E.g. `"Octopus.Action.Email.Subject"  = "Deployment #{Octopus.Project.Name} #{Octopus.Release.Number} complete"`, `"Octopus.Action.Email.Priority" = "Normal"`.


## ArgoCD Step Instructions

**CRITICAL:** a deployment process may contain AT MOST ONE ENABLED Argo CD update action (`Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests` or `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags`), else the apply aborts.
* `Octopus.VerifyArgoCDApplicationHealthy` ("Wait for Argo CD Applications") is NOT counted.
* When the prompt asks for both a manifest-update and an image-tag-update step, create BOTH but set `is_disabled = true` on all but one. Keep enabled the step the project type is named after: "Argo CD Modify/Update Manifest" keeps `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests`; "Argo CD Image Tag Update" keeps `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags` (as in `everystep.tf`). Never drop the requested step. This `is_disabled = true` is NOT covered by the rule forbidding disabling steps the prompt did not ask to disable.
* The limit counts same-type steps too: NEVER duplicate an Argo CD step (not even disabled) for a second branch, repository, application or environment ("one step per environment" included). Use ONE step with the value the prompt lists FIRST; record the others in `notes` as not implemented. Per-environment values: use an Octopus variable.
* Self-check: count `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests` + `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags` steps per process; if more than one lacks `is_disabled = true`, disable all but one.
* `Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.InputPath` holds ONE manifest path. When several are named, put the FIRST in `Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.InputPath` and list the rest in `notes`, e.g. `Also requested: k8s/quote-api/service.yaml. This step type takes a single input path, so only the first file is configured.` Never drop them silently, never create a second `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests` step, never concatenate (`"a.yaml,b.yaml"` or `"a.yaml b.yaml"`).
* **General rule: when a prompt supplies a list and the target property accepts one value, the shortfall goes in `notes`** (manifest paths, branches, repositories, Argo CD applications).

**CRITICAL:** `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.CommitMethod" = "PullRequest"` REQUIRES a non-empty `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.PullRequest.Environments"` in the same `execution_properties`, else the apply aborts.
* When the prompt asks to commit "via a pull request", "by raising a PR", or "with a pull request for review", set both: `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.CommitMethod" = "PullRequest"` and `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.PullRequest.Environments"` = comma-separated environment IDs resolved with the usual data-source/resource ternary (e.g. `"Environments-4661,Environments-4662"`); use every lifecycle environment unless the prompt names a subset.
* Otherwise use `"DirectCommit"` (no environment list needed) — also when you cannot confidently build the ID list.

**CRITICAL:** an `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests` step MUST NOT have a `packages`, `package`, or `primary_package` block; it reads manifests via `git_dependencies` (attaching an image aborts the apply). When the prompt says a manifest-update step handles "the container image `org/name`", that belongs in manifest content/Argo configuration, not a package.
* By contrast `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags` DOES take the container image as its package; a Docker-style ID with `/` is valid when `feed_id` points at an `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry`.
* A package ID with `/` is only valid on a Docker container registry feed — never the built-in or a NuGet feed, or a step type taking no package.

* Both "Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags" (updates image tags in an ArgoCD application's values file) and "Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests" (updates manifest files in a Git repository) must define: `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`, `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.CommitMethod" = "DirectCommit"` (unless `"PullRequest"` per above), `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.Sync.Mode" = "AllEnvironments"`, `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.StepVerification.Method" = "CommitCreated"`, `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.StepVerification.Timeout" = "180"`, plus:
  * ImageTags: `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.CommitMessageSummary" = "Octopus Deploy updated image versions"`; when specified by the prompt, `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.NewImageTag"` (e.g. `"#{Docker.Image.Tag}"`), `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.ApplicationName"` (e.g. `"#{ArgoCD.AppName}"`), `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.ServerUrl"`; and a `packages` block with a Docker image reference from the GHCR Anonymous feed.
  * Manifests: `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository"`, `"Octopus.Action.GitRepository.Source" = "External"`, a descriptive `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.CommitMessageSummary"` (e.g. `"Updated Manifests with Release: #{Octopus.Release.Number}"`), `"Octopus.Action.ArgoCD.InputPath"` (manifest path), and a `git_dependencies` block with the repository URL.
## Tomcat Deploy Step Instructions

* Steps of type "Octopus.TomcatDeploy" (deploy WAR/JAR via the Tomcat Manager API) must define a `primary_package` block referencing a Maven feed package, `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"` in `properties` targeting the Tomcat server role, and these execution properties:
  * `"Octopus.Action.EnabledFeatures" = ",Octopus.Features.TomcatDeployManager"`
  * `"Tomcat.Deploy.Enabled" = "True"`
  * `"Tomcat.Deploy.Controller"` (manager URL, e.g. `"http://localhost:8080/manager"`), `"Tomcat.Deploy.Name"` (application name), `"Tomcat.Deploy.User"` (manager admin user), `"Tomcat.Deploy.Password"` (sensitive variable reference)

**CRITICAL:** `Tomcat.Deploy.User` and `Tomcat.Deploy.Password` are API-enforced: ALWAYS non-empty, even if the prompt gives no credentials — then default to `"Tomcat.Deploy.User" = "#{Tomcat.User}"` and `"Tomcat.Deploy.Password" = "#{Tomcat.Password}"` and create project variables `Tomcat.User` (`String`, value `"tomcat"`) and `Tomcat.Password` (`Sensitive`).

**CRITICAL:** `Tomcat.Deploy.Password` MUST be a VARIABLE REFERENCE (e.g. `"#{Tomcat.Password}"` + sensitive variable), never a literal, invented password or placeholder like `"changeme"`/`"Change Me!"` (else `inconsistent values for sensitive attribute`).
* Same for every sensitive `execution_properties` value (IIS/service account passwords, API keys, secret webhook URLs): secret in an `octopusdeploy_variable` with `is_sensitive = true`, referenced via Octostache.
* Self-check: every `execution_properties` key containing `Password`, `Secret`, `ApiKey`, `Token` or `Credential` must have a value starting with `#{`.

**CRITICAL:** hand-setting a RESERVED provider-managed package property causes the same error.
* NEVER put `Octopus.Action.Package.DownloadOnTentacle`, `Octopus.Action.Package.PackageId` or `Octopus.Action.Package.FeedId` in `execution_properties` on a step with a `primary_package` block; the provider derives them. Delete any `Octopus.Action.Package.` key from such steps. Treat the provider's `... is reserved` warning as fatal.
* They remain CORRECT on a step without `primary_package` that uses the named `packages` map (e.g. `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "Package"`).

**CRITICAL:** an `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` step has no account field; an `octopusdeploy_username_password_account` cannot be attached. When a prompt says "create a username/password account called 'X' with the username 'U' and use it in the deploy step":

1. Still create the `octopusdeploy_username_password_account` resource named `X`.
2. Still set `Tomcat.Deploy.User` and `Tomcat.Deploy.Password` — the account does NOT satisfy them.
