3. Set `Tomcat.Deploy.User` to the prompt's username (`"U"`) or a variable holding it.
4. **When the prompt names the account but gives NO username** (e.g. `Create a username/password account called "Tomcat Manager Account" and the Tomcat step must reference it`), do NOT invent a literal like `"Tomcat.Deploy.User" = "CHANGEME"`. Point a project variable of type `UsernamePasswordAccount` at the account and read both credentials from it:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "project_tomcat_manager_account_1" {
  count        = "..."
  owner_id     = "..."
  name         = "Project.Tomcat.Manager.Account"
  type         = "UsernamePasswordAccount"
  value        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_tomcat_manager_account.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_tomcat_manager_account.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_username_password_account.account_tomcat_manager_account[0].id}"
  is_sensitive = false
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [sensitive_value]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
# step: "Tomcat.Deploy.User" = "#{Project.Tomcat.Manager.Account.Username}"
#       "Tomcat.Deploy.Password" = "#{Project.Tomcat.Manager.Account.Password}"
```

* (Account variables expose `#{<variable name>.Username}`/`#{<variable name>.Password}`.) This binding is the ONLY way a username/password account reaches a Tomcat step; with NO account, keep `#{Tomcat.User}` / `#{Tomcat.Password}`.
* Never write `CHANGEME`, `changeme`, `TODO`, or similar into `Tomcat.Deploy.User`.

E.g. account "Tomcat Manager" with username "octopus" → account `username = "octopus"`, `password = "#{Tomcat.Password}"`; step `"Tomcat.Deploy.User" = "octopus"`, `"Tomcat.Deploy.Password" = "#{Tomcat.Password}"`.

Self-check: every `octopusdeploy_process_step` with `type = "Octopus.TomcatDeploy"` has non-empty `Tomcat.Deploy.User` and `Tomcat.Deploy.Password` in `execution_properties` (a placeholder such as `Change Me!` counts for this presence check).

## Kustomize Step Instructions

**CRITICAL:** an `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize` step MUST set a non-empty `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.Kustomize.OverlayPath"`. If the prompt names no overlay directory, use `"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.Kustomize.OverlayPath" = "overlays/#{Octopus.Environment.Name}"`.
* It reads files from Git: also set `"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource" = "GitRepository"`, `"Octopus.Action.GitRepository.Source" = "External"` and a `git_dependencies` block naming the repository, branch and Git credential.
* It needs a target (see the "Kubernetes target" pattern); target tag in STEP-LEVEL `properties` as `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"`.

## Transfer Package Step Instructions

* Steps of type "Octopus.TransferPackage" must define a `primary_package` block, execution property `"Octopus.Action.Package.TransferPath"` (destination path), and `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"` in `properties` targeting the deployment target.

## AWS Create S3 Step Instructions

* Steps of type "Octopus.AwsCreateS3" (create an Amazon S3 bucket) need a `container` block with the GitHub Container Registry feed and `ghcr.io/octopusdeploylabs/aws-workertools`, and execution properties `"Octopus.Action.AwsAccount.Variable"` (AWS account variable), `"Octopus.Action.Aws.AssumeRole" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Aws.Region"` (e.g. `"us-east-1"`), `"Octopus.Action.Aws.S3.BucketName"` (name or variable reference), `"Octopus.Action.Aws.S3.PublicAccess" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Aws.S3.ObjectWriterOwnership" = "False"`, `"Octopus.Action.Aws.CloudFormation.Tags" = jsonencode([])`.

## Octopus step template instructions

**CRITICAL:** the `parameters` map of an `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` may contain ONLY the template's OWN declared parameter names (undeclared keys "vanish" and the apply fails).
* Template parameter names are template-prefixed, e.g. `ssn_Color`, `Sbom.Package`, `CheckTargets.Octopus.Role`, `Block.Octopus.Reason`, `Run.Runbook.Project.Name`, `OctopusAI.Prompt`, `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name`. Keys starting `Octopus.Action.` or `Octopus.Step.` are NOT template parameters.
* Move `Octopus.Action.*` properties to `execution_properties` (delete script ones); normally just `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` (plus `OctopusUseBundledTooling` where the example uses it).
* A templated step must NOT define a script (`Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource`/`Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` in neither map); if parameters can't express it, use an `octopusdeploy_process_step` of type `Octopus.Script`.
* Unknown parameter names: use `parameters = {}`, don't invent keys (as the "Calculate Deployment Mode" example step does).
* This holds even when the PROMPT asks for an undeclared parameter (e.g. `Add a step template parameter called "Fabric.Region" to the "Scan for Vulnerabilities" community step template and set it to "eu-west-1"`): do NOT add it to `parameters`; record the request in `notes` and, if the value is needed at deployment time, create an ordinary project variable. A plausible-looking name is no evidence it is declared.

**CRITICAL:** the "Scan for Vulnerabilities" community step template REQUIRES its `Sbom.Package` package parameter:

```hcl
  parameters = {
    "Sbom.Package" = jsonencode({
      "PackageId" = "com.octopus:octopub-frontend-sbom"
      "FeedId"    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_maven_feed.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_maven_feed.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_maven_feed.feed_octopus_maven_feed[0].id}"
    })
  }
```

* The feed MUST exist in the output (`feed_octopus_maven_feed` or a prompt feed); the package id may be the prompt's own SBOM package.
* Applies whenever the step is added, even renamed (e.g. "Add a 'Scan for Vulnerabilities' step called 'Scan Evidence Bundle'") or in a project with no other packages (still needs a feed).
* General rule: for ANY community step template, supply every required `parameters` entry shown in the example configurations; optional parameters MUST be omitted, not set to `""`.
* **CRITICAL — for a community step template with NO documented example in this file** (e.g. "AWS - Copy S3 Object"), do NOT guess by setting an unfamiliar required parameter to `""`. Octopus wraps many fields in "tiny type" value objects that reject blank strings at apply time: `Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [value (Parameter 'Tiny types should never be empty or whitespace. If the value you want to communicate is null/empty/whitespace then just pass a null rather than a tiny type wrapping it.')]` (observed on `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` for an undocumented template). Either provide every parameter a concrete, sensible placeholder value derived from the prompt (never `""`), or omit the key entirely so the template's own default applies — never pass an empty string for any `parameters` value.
* **CRITICAL — NEVER fabricate a `website` GUID for a community step template that is not one of the ones with a documented `https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/<guid>` URL in this file** (the verified set includes "Slack - Send Simple Notification" `99e6f203-3061-4018-9e34-4a3a9c3c3179`, "Run Octopus Deploy Runbook" `0444b0b3-088e-4689-b755-112d1360ffe3`, "Block Release Progression" `78a182b3-5369-4e13-9292-b7f991295ad1`, "Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment" `72db001f-ae7f-4a0f-b952-5f80e2fc4cd2`, "Calculate Deployment Mode" `d166457a-1421-4731-b143-dd6766fb95d5`). An invented/incorrect GUID does not fail cleanly — it crashes the Octopus server apply with `Octopus API error: An exception was thrown while attempting to evaluate a LINQ query parameter expression ... Object reference not set to an instance of an object.` and `unable to install community step template` (observed for a fabricated "AWS - Copy S3 Object" GUID). If the prompt names a community step template outside this verified set, do NOT create `octopusdeploy_community_step_template`/its data source — implement the same intent as an ordinary `Octopus.Script` step instead, and record in that step's `notes` that the requested community step template could not be verified and was replaced with an equivalent script.

* A prompt creating a community step template with a url to https://library.octopus.com/step-templates needs both a resource "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" and a data "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" (existence check), never a "octopusdeploy_process_step" instead. Copy the data source's "website" attribute literally.
* The "properties" attribute must be added to "octopusdeploy_step_template" resources.
* The "id" property of objects in the "parameters" array must be a GUID, never plain text like "array-input".
* Never separate "parameters" array items with two commas (invalid: `default_sensitive_value = null,, display_settings = ...`).
* **CRITICAL:** a step template parameter with `"Octopus.ControlType" = "Select"` MUST also carry `"Octopus.SelectOptions"` in the same `display_settings` map (else the dropdown is empty). Format: `"<value>|<display name>"` per option, separated by literal `\n`:

```hcl
parameters = [{ default_sensitive_value = null, default_value = "info", display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "Select", "Octopus.SelectOptions" = "info|info\nwarn|warn\nerror|error" }, help_text = "The severity of the notification", id = "3f4a5b6c-7d8e-49f0-a1b2-c3d4e5f6a7b8", label = "Ops Severity", name = "Ops.Severity" }]
```

  * `default_value` must be one of the options; if the prompt's default isn't listed, add it to the list.
  * Applies to EVERY `Select` control: `octopusdeploy_step_template` parameters, `octopusdeploy_library_variable_set` templates, and `octopusdeploy_project` templates (same shape as `"Octopus.SelectOptions" = "A|A\nB|B"`). Only an `octopusdeploy_variable` `prompt` block uses the different `select_option { display_name = ... value = ... }` sub-block syntax.


## Octopus Variable Syntax Instructions

* Octopus variable syntax is "#{<variable name>}", never "${<variable name>}" or "$<variable name>".

**CRITICAL:** an Octostache expression QUOTED IN THE PROMPT must be copied verbatim, NEVER resolved to a literal value. If the prompt put `#{` and `}` around something, so does the output.
* **`#{Octopus.Environment.Name}` is the most frequently broken (replaced with `Production`)**; e.g. `the subject "Ledger Service deployed to #{Octopus.Environment.Name}"` became `"Octopus.Action.Email.Subject" = "Ledger Service deployed to Production"`; `the Terraform variable "region" with the value "#{Octopus.Environment.Name}"` became `"region" = "Production"`; `"#{unless Octopus.Deployment.Error}True#{/unless}"` became `"Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" = "True"` — all WRONG.
* Applies everywhere (step properties, Terraform template parameters, script bodies, variable values, package IDs, image tags, YAML, email subjects, channel and step names) and to helpers (`#{if ...}`, `#{unless ...}`, `#{each ...}`, `#{ ... | Contains "x"}`) and output-variable references such as `#{Octopus.Action[My Step].Output.Foo}`.
* Do NOT "simplify" an expression you judge redundant, or evaluate one because only one environment exists. Self-check: every prompt-quoted `#{...}` appears in the output.

## Octopus Variable Instructions

* A variable scoped to a runbook sets the `processes` scope to the runbook ID:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_create_new_terraform_project" {

}

resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "easy_mode_mcp_projectname_1" {
  owner_id     = "${data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_easy_mode_mcp.projects[0].id}"
  name         = "ProjectName"
  type         = "String"
  description = "The new project name"
  is_sensitive = false
  scope {
      # The process references the runbook ID to scope the variable to the runbook
      processes = ["${octopusdeploy_runbook.runbook_create_new_terraform_project.id}"]
  }
}
```

* resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "type" must be one of "AmazonWebServicesAccount", "AzureAccount", "GoogleCloudAccount", "UsernamePasswordAccount", "Certificate", "Sensitive", "String", "WorkerPool"; never "Token".
* "name" + "scope" must be unique across "octopusdeploy_variable" resources; never two with the same name.
* "is_sensitive" "true" requires "type" "Sensitive". Sensitive variables set "sensitive_value" to "CHANGEME" (nothing else) and never define "value"; never set both "value" and "sensitive_value".
* **CRITICAL:** every `octopusdeploy_variable` MUST include (both entries required):
```hcl
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [sensitive_value]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
```
* **CRITICAL:** variables from `templatedPipeline` `variables` (`Add a project variable called "<name>" with the value "<value>"`) always use `type = "String"`, even for values like `"true"`, `"false"`, `"15"`, `"900"` or URLs; never `type = "Boolean"`, `type = "Integer"` etc. Exception: `Add a sensitive project variable` → `type = "Sensitive"`, `is_sensitive = true`.
* **CRITICAL:** migration variables (`Add a project variable called`) need a non-empty `description`: if none given (`Add a project variable called "<name>" with the value "<value>".`) use `description = "Migrated Spinnaker pipeline variable: <name>"`; otherwise use the given one verbatim.
* `Add a sensitive project variable called "<name>" with the description "<description>".` → project-scoped `octopusdeploy_variable`: `name = "<name>"`, `description = "<description>"`, `type = "Sensitive"`, `is_sensitive = true`, `sensitive_value = "CHANGEME"`, no `value`.
* `Add a sensitive prompted project variable called "<name>" with the description "<description>" and the label "<label>". The variable must be prompted for when creating a release.` → as above plus `prompt` block: `label = "<label>"`, `is_required = true` (`false` if "must not be required"), `display_settings { control_type = "Sensitive" }`.

```
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "a_sensitive_variable" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  owner_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) == 0 ?octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id : data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_azure_my_project[0].id}"
  name         = "A.Sensitive.Variable"
  type         = "Sensitive"
  is_sensitive = true
  sensitive_value = "CHANGEME"
}
```
(Regular variable: `value = "canadacentral"`, `type = "String"`, `is_sensitive = false`.)

* **CRITICAL:** an unscoped variable has NO `scope` block: never "scope {}" and never a "scope" block whose attributes are all `null` (aborts the whole apply: `.scope: block count changed from 1 to 0`).
* Valid "scope" attributes: "actions", "channels", "environments", "machines", "roles", "tenant_tags", "processes". Never "tenants".
* "processes" identifies the process OWNER: a project ID (deployment process, e.g. `processes = ["${octopusdeploy_project.project_example[0].id}"]`) or a runbook ID only. Never an "octopusdeploy_process_step" or "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" id (stored as `"ProcessOwner": ["<step guid>"]`, silently resolves to nothing). A scope to a named STEP is not expressible here: omit "processes" (same mistake as the CRITICAL `actions` scope rule at the top of this file).
* "tenant_tags" entries are "Tag Set Name/Tag Name", e.g. `tenant_tags = ["Region/West"]`; never a single word like "West" or "Enterprise-Client". The tag set must match a resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" name and the tag a resource "octopusdeploy_tag". Every tag belongs to a tag set.
* If the prompt gives tags but no tag set, create tag set "Default" and assign all tags to it:

```
data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" "default_tag_set" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Default"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" "default_tag_set" {
  name        = "Default"
  description = "The default tag set"
  count       = length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.default_tag_set.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1
}
```

* "WorkerPool" variables set "value" to hosted pool first, default pool second (the `worker_pool_id` expression below); never fall back to `null` ("${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools[0].id : null}"). Example: `resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "worker_pool_variable"` with `name = "Octopus.Project.WorkerPool"`, `type = "WorkerPool"`, `is_sensitive = false`, `description = "Using a variable to define the worker pool used by steps allows the worker pool to be easily changed in a central location."`.
* If the prompt creates a variable in a library variable set or as a tenant variable, create no project variable of the same name (those take precedence).
* Never define "tenant_id" on "octopusdeploy_variable".
* "octopusdeploy_variable" resource names start with a letter or underscore, never a number or special character.
* A "prompt" block always includes "label" (empty string if no label should display).
* Never create data "octopusdeploy_variables".

## Runbook Instructions

* A runbook without an associated project: "octopusdeploy_runbook" "count" is "1". Otherwise "count" is based on the parent project:
```
resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_my_runbook" {
  count                       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
}
```
* "runbook_id" of "octopusdeploy_runbook_process" is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.<project>.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_runbook.<runbook>[0].id}"
* "octopusdeploy_runbook" / "octopusdeploy_runbooks" data sources do not exist; never create them.

## Worker Pool Instructions

* Always include:

```
data "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" "workerpool_default_worker_pool" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Default Worker Pool"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

* "worker_pool_id" tests the hosted pool data source's "worker_pools" length, falling back to the default pool:

```
worker_pool_id = ${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools[0].id : data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools[0].id}
```

* **CRITICAL:** when the prompt names a pool for a step (e.g. "Set the step to run on the Hosted Ubuntu worker pool"), set `worker_pool_id` on that step. Do NOT create a `Project.WorkerPool` variable of type "WorkerPool" or use `worker_pool_variable` unless explicitly asked (e.g. "create a variable for the worker pool", "define the worker pool as a project variable").
* **CRITICAL:** that forbids CREATING an unrequested pool variable, not IGNORING an existing one. If the template already has `Project.WorkerPool` (or `Project.Octopus.WorkerPool`) of type `WorkerPool`, prompt-ADDED steps MUST use `worker_pool_variable = "Project.WorkerPool"` (the template's exact name), not `worker_pool_id`. `worker_pool_id` is correct only when the prompt names a pool for that step or no pool variable exists. (Observed intermittently on a `Check Canary Metrics` step.) Self-check: with a `WorkerPool` variable, every `octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` uses `worker_pool_variable` unless the prompt named its pool.
* **CRITICAL:** never hardcode IDs: no `worker_pool_id` like `"WorkerPools-1234"`, no project `lifecycle_id` like `"Lifecycles-7411"`; no `WorkerPools-*` / `Lifecycles-*` anywhere. Default lifecycle: `"${data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id}"`.

## Octopus Target Instructions

* **CRITICAL:** every deployment target needs at least one item in `environments`, `roles` and the `endpoint` block (`MinItems: 1`; else PLAN fails with `diagnostic_summary="Not enough list items"`, `tf_rpc=ValidateResourceConfig`, and nothing is created). Applies to `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_cloud_region_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_listening_tentacle_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_polling_tentacle_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_azure_web_app_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_azure_cloud_service_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_azure_service_fabric_cluster_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_offline_package_drop_deployment_target`, `octopusdeploy_ssh_connection_deployment_target`.
  * `environments = []` is always wrong; if the prompt names none, list all environments in the project's lifecycle / defined or looked up in the output.
  * `roles = []` is always wrong. Kubernetes targets: every target tag referenced by the project's Kubernetes steps plus `"Kubernetes"`; if none, `roles = ["Kubernetes"]`.
  * `endpoint` present with at least one attribute (Kubernetes: `endpoint { communication_style = "Kubernetes" }`); not omitted, not `endpoint {}`.
  * `tenant_tags = []` and `tenants = []` have no minimum; keep empty on untenanted targets.
* **CRITICAL:** every `environments` entry is a real ID via this ternary, with the `data "octopusdeploy_environments"` and `resource "octopusdeploy_environment"` it reads present in the output:
```
environments = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}"]
```
  * NEVER `environments = [""]` (passes plan, fails apply with `The EnvironmentIds field is required.`, orphaning earlier resources); NEVER literals like `environments = ["Environments-1"]` (`Environments-n`); NEVER IDs from `octopusdeploy_machine_policies`, `octopusdeploy_worker_pools`, `octopusdeploy_feeds` or `octopusdeploy_accounts`; a ternary with identical branches is always wrong, e.g. observed `"${data.octopusdeploy_machine_policies.default_machine_policy.machine_policies[0].id != "" ? "" : ""}"`.
  * If the output has no environments at all (e.g. a prompt asking only for an account, feed and target), emit data/resource pairs for "Development", "Test" and "Production" and list all three.
* Each "octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target" needs a data "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets" of the same name:

```
data "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets" "kubernetes_cluster_target_example" {
  partial_name         = "Kubernetes Target"
  skip                 = 0
  take                 = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target" "kubernetes_cluster_target_example" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_deployment_targets.kubernetes_cluster_target_example.deployment_targets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Kubernetes Target"
}
```

* No "description" on "octopusdeploy_ssh_connection_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_listening_tentacle_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_polling_tentacle_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_cloud_region_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_offline_package_drop_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_azure_cloud_service_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_azure_service_fabric_cluster_deployment_target", "octopusdeploy_azure_web_app_deployment_target". No "parent_environment_ids" on any target.
* **CRITICAL:** `octopusdeploy_listening_tentacle_deployment_target` and `octopusdeploy_polling_tentacle_deployment_target` need a non-empty 40-character uppercase hex `thumbprint` (empty fails at PLAN: `Error: expected "thumbprint" to not be an empty string`, zero resources). `thumbprint = ""` is valid ONLY on `octopusdeploy_cloud_region_deployment_target` (don't copy it). If none given use `0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01234567`; never empty, `null`, `CHANGEME` or a certificate ID. No example configuration exists; copy this canonical shape (for `Create a listening tentacle deployment target called "Dispatch VM" with the tag "dispatch" and the URI "https://dispatchvm.example.com:10933"`):

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets" "target_dispatch_vm" {
  partial_name = "Dispatch VM"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_listening_tentacle_deployment_target" "target_dispatch_vm" {
  count                             = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_deployment_targets.target_dispatch_vm.deployment_targets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                              = "Dispatch VM"
  roles                             = ["dispatch"]
  environments                      = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}"]
  machine_policy_id                 = "${data.octopusdeploy_machine_policies.default_machine_policy.machine_policies[0].id}"
  tentacle_url                      = "https://dispatchvm.example.com:10933"
  thumbprint                        = "0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01234567"
  is_disabled                       = false
  shell_name                        = "Unknown"
  shell_version                     = "Unknown"
  tenant_tags                       = []
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
  tenants                           = []
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

* "machine_policy_id" is never an empty string. If the prompt gives no machine policy, use `machine_policy_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_machine_policies.default_machine_policy.machine_policies[0].id}"` (never "MachinePolicies-1" or any fixed value) with:

```
data "octopusdeploy_machine_policies" "default_machine_policy" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Default Machine Policy"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```
* "octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target" uses a "container" block (never "health_check_container"; never `container = {` with an equals sign):
```
  container {
    feed_id = "Feeds-5455"
    image   = "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.4.0-ubuntu.22.04"
  }
```

## Octopus Machine Policy Instructions
* Never add "tenant_ids", "environment_ids" or "project_ids" to a "octopusdeploy_machine_policies" data source.
* "tentacle_update_behavior" on "octopusdeploy_machine_policy" is "NeverUpdate" or "Update"; never "UpdateAutomatically".

## Octopus Account Instructions

* "octopusdeploy_token_account" "token" is always "CHANGEME"; ignore any instruction to set it otherwise.

* **CRITICAL:** `username` is NOT a secret; NEVER write `CHANGEME` into `username`. `CHANGEME` rules apply to `token`, `password`, `secret_key` and `sensitive_value` ONLY. If the prompt gives a username (`Create a username/password account called "Service Runner" with the username "svc_ledger"`), use exactly `username = "svc_ledger"`. Only if the prompt names an account but gives NO username may you use a sensible default, preferring a real-looking value over a placeholder.

* **CRITICAL:** a step property naming an account holder (e.g. `Octopus.Action.WindowsService.CustomAccountName`, which takes a WINDOWS USER NAME) MUST NOT contain an `Accounts-NNN` id or a Terraform reference to an account resource.. Mirror the Tomcat account rule: create the account, bind it to a `UsernamePasswordAccount`-typed project variable, and read BOTH credentials from it; with `ServiceAccount = "_CUSTOM"`, `CustomAccountName` and `CustomAccountPassword` must BOTH be present:

```hcl
"Octopus.Action.WindowsService.ServiceAccount"        = "_CUSTOM"
"Octopus.Action.WindowsService.CustomAccountName"     = "#{Project.Service.Runner.Account.Username}"
"Octopus.Action.WindowsService.CustomAccountPassword" = "#{Project.Service.Runner.Account.Password}"
```

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "project_service_runner_account_1" {
  # ... count, owner_id
  name     = "Project.Service.Runner.Account"
  type     = "UsernamePasswordAccount"
  value    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_service_runner.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_service_runner.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_username_password_account.account_service_runner[0].id}"
  is_sensitive = false
  # ... lifecycle, depends_on
}
```

* General rule (also e.g. no `Certificates-NNN` id in an IIS binding's `thumbprint`): an account or certificate reaches a step property only through an account-/certificate-typed project VARIABLE referenced by Octostache, never a raw id or a Terraform resource interpolation inside a string property. Self-check: search `execution_properties` for `Accounts-`, `Certificates-`, `${octopusdeploy_..._account.`, `${octopusdeploy_certificate.` and replace each hit; confirm every `username` holds the prompt's value, not `CHANGEME`.
* If an account is associated with a tenant: include the tenants in its "tenants" attribute, and if the prompt specifies no tenanted mode set "tenanted_deployment_participation" to "TenantedOrUntenanted". If associated with tenant tags, include them in "tenant_tags".
* **CRITICAL:** an account's `tenanted_deployment_participation` is INDEPENDENT of the project's and MUST be `"Untenanted"` unless the account itself is tied to tenants or tenant tags. (a tenanted account with empty `tenants` AND `tenant_tags` aborts the apply).
  * "The project must allow tenanted deployments only", "make the project tenanted", or tenants in the prompt affect the `octopusdeploy_project` ONLY. Do NOT copy the mode to `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal`, `octopusdeploy_aws_account`, `octopusdeploy_token_account`, `octopusdeploy_username_password_account`, `octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect_account`, `octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account`, or any account.
  * Use `Tenanted`/`TenantedOrUntenanted` only when the prompt ties that account to tenants (e.g. "create a token account called 'K8s' for the 'Contoso' tenant"), and then `tenants` or `tenant_tags` MUST be non-empty in the same resource.
  * Same for `octopusdeploy_certificate`: `"Untenanted"` with empty `tenants`/`tenant_tags` unless the prompt scopes it to tenants.
  * Self-check: every account and `octopusdeploy_certificate` not `"Untenanted"` has non-empty `tenants` or `tenant_tags`; otherwise change to `"Untenanted"`.
* Create one distinct "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account" per AWS OIDC account in the prompt, each with its own "octopusdeploy_accounts" data source with "account_type" "AmazonWebServicesOidcAccount" ("role_arn" mandatory).
* **CRITICAL:** explicitly requested accounts ("Create an [account type] account called [name]" / "Add an [account type] account called [name]") MUST be created (resource + data source) even if no step references them.
* **CRITICAL:** "Create an AWS account" without OIDC/access key specified → `octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account` with a placeholder role ARN and `account_test_subject_keys`, `execution_subject_keys`, `health_subject_keys` = `["space"]`. Example ("Create an AWS account called 'AWS Production'"):
```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_accounts" "account_aws_production" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "AWS Production"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  account_type = "AmazonWebServicesOidcAccount"
}
resource "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account" "account_aws_production" {
  count                             = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_aws_production.accounts) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                              = "AWS Production"
  description                       = "AWS Production account"
  role_arn                          = "arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/OctopusDeployRole"
  account_test_subject_keys         = ["space"]
  environments                      = []
  execution_subject_keys            = ["space"]
  health_subject_keys               = ["space"]
  session_duration                  = 3600
  tenant_tags                       = []
  tenants                           = []
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
  depends_on                        = []
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = []
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```
* Never use a `locals` block for account names.
* When a step requires an account, create its data source and resource.
* **CRITICAL:** prompt-named accounts replace template defaults for that cloud provider (e.g. "Add an AWS OIDC account named 'AWS Production Account'", "Add an Azure OIDC account named 'Azure Production Account'"). Create ONLY the named accounts:
  - Azure: do NOT create the default `account_octopussamples_azure_account` (`octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal`).
  - AWS: do NOT create the default `account_aws_oidc` (`octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account`).
  - ALL steps must reference the prompt-named account's resource/data source: `"Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId"` in `Octopus.AzureWebApp`, `Octopus.AzurePowerShell`, `Octopus.AzureResourceGroup` steps; `"Octopus.Action.AwsAccount"` in `Octopus.AwsRunScript`, `Octopus.AwsUploadS3` steps. E.g. for "AWS Production Account":
```hcl
execution_properties = {
  "Octopus.Action.AwsAccount" = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_aws_production_account.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_aws_production_account.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account.account_aws_production_account[0].id}"
}
```
  - Self-check: if the prompt names an Azure/AWS account, `account_octopussamples_azure_account` / `account_aws_oidc` must not appear anywhere (delete it), and every `Octopus.Action.Azure.AccountId` / `Octopus.Action.AwsAccount` references the named account.

## Worker Instructions

* "octopusdeploy_ssh_connection_worker" must define non-empty "account_id", "fingerprint" and "host".
* If no machine policy is specified for a worker, look up "machine_policy_id" from the existing "Default Machine Policy".
* If no worker pool is specified for a worker, look up an existing worker pool with a suitable name via a data source and assign it; if it doesn't exist, create it and assign the worker.

## Platform Hub Instructions

* Platform Hub version control settings use a "octopusdeploy_platform_hub_version_control_username_password_settings" resource. Do not also create a "octopusdeploy_git_credential" unless the prompt says to.

## Terraform Instructions
* All resource and data names start with a character or underscore, never a number (e.g. not "14_kubernetes_with_mock_target_kubernetes_deployment_name_1", "123_my_resource", "05_script_project"). For names from strings starting with a number (e.g. "123 My Resource"), prefix an underscore: "_123_my_resource", "_08_script_project", "_14_kubernetes_with_mock_target_octopus_project_workerpool_1".
* Only these built-in functions: "jsondecode", "jsonencode", "keys", "length", "list", "trim", "trimprefix", "trimspace", "trimsuffix", "try". Never `replace()` or `lower()`, no pipes (`${var.step_name | lower | replace(" ", "-")}`).
* Resource names and data names must be unique per type (e.g. never two `resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "step1"` or two `data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project1"`).
* Never define resources or attributes not in the sample Terraform configurations.
* Every resource has a "count" attribute in the format "${length(data.<data type>.<data resource>.<collection>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"; never a "count" of "1".
* Heredoc syntax only (and preferred) for `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` and `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml`; quoted strings otherwise.
* Blocks write each child property on a new line; never a single-line block like "lifecycle { ignore_changes = [sensitive_value] prevent_destroy = true }".
* Use password "CHANGEME" for all passwords and secret keys. Use username "CHANGEME" for all usernames.
* Only create these step types: "Octopus.Script" (Run a Script); "Octopus.AzurePowerShell" (Run an Azure Script); "Octopus.AwsRunScript" (Run an AWS CLI Script); "Octopus.GoogleCloudScripting" (Run gcloud in a Script); "Octopus.AzureResourceGroup" (Deploy an Azure Resource Manager template); "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript" (Run a kubectl script); "Octopus.IIS"; "Octopus.TentaclePackage" (Deploy a Package); "Octopus.WindowsService"; "Octopus.AzureWebApp" (Deploy an Azure Web App (Web Deploy)); "Octopus.AwsUploadS3" (Upload a package to an AWS S3 bucket); "Octopus.JavaArchive"; "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade"; "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" (Deploy Kubernetes YAML); "Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize"; "Octopus.TerraformApply"; "Octopus.TerraformDestroy"; "Octopus.TerraformPlan"; "Octopus.TerraformPlanDestroy"; "Octopus.AwsRunCloudFormation" (Deploy an AWS CloudFormation template); "Octopus.AwsApplyCloudFormationChangeSet"; "Octopus.AwsDeleteCloudFormation"; "Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags"; "Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests" (Update ArgoCD Application Manifests); "Octopus.TomcatDeploy"; "Octopus.TransferPackage"; "Octopus.AwsCreateS3"; "Octopus.DeployRelease"; "Octopus.Manual" (Manual Intervention); "Octopus.Email".
* The "octopusdeploy" provider sets "space_id" to "trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)" (never a value like "Spaces-1"), and this variable must be included:
```
variable "octopus_space_id" {
    type        = string
    nullable    = false
    sensitive   = false
    description = "The ID of the Octopus space to populate."
}
```
* String interpolation is "${}", never "${{}}".
* In script steps escape dollar signs as "$$" (e.g. "echo $$variable"; "${VARIABLE}" → "$${VARIABLE}") and percent signs as "%%" (e.g. `"curl -w \"%%{http_code}\" http://example.org"`). Multi-line bodies may use heredoc:
```
"Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody" = <<-EOT
  curl -w \"%%{http_code}\" http://example.org
EOT
```
* Define "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" in "properties" for steps that deploy to targets, including "Octopus.TentaclePackage", "Octopus.TomcatDeploy", "Octopus.AzureAppService", "Octopus.IIS", "Octopus.WindowsService", "Octopus.AzureWebApp", "Octopus.JavaArchive", "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade", "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml", "Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize", "Octopus.TransferPackage".
* Take EXTREME CARE escaping quotes; escape every inner quote **when that quote belongs to a literal value being reproduced verbatim** (SpEL expressions, JSON snippets, script text) inside a larger string such as `ssn_Message`, `notes`, or a script body:
 ```
 "ssn_Message" = "Image: $${trigger[\"artifacts\"].?[name == _\"registry.example.invalid_image-0095_\"][0][_\"version_\"]}"
 ```
  * **CRITICAL — this does NOT apply to native Terraform expression syntax.** A string literal that is part of the Terraform language itself (a `for` filter, an `if` condition, a `==` comparison, a function argument) inside `${...}` uses a plain unescaped quote, e.g. `item.name == "Prod"` in an `octopusdeploy_tag` `count` expression — never `item.name == \"Prod\"`. Escaping native Terraform quotes produces invalid HCL that aborts the entire apply (see the `octopusdeploy_tag` `count` instruction above).


## Terraform Variable Instructions

* All terraform variables have default values; `type = string` without quotes (never `type = "string"`); always an equals sign between property and value (never `nullable     false`); never a "scope" block:

```
variable "project_group_name" {
  type        = string
  nullable    = false
  sensitive   = false
  description = "The name of the project group to lookup"
  default     = "My Project Group"
}
```

## Penalties and General Instructions

* Respond only with the Terraform configuration; never invalid Terraform. No markdown, code blocks, instructions, comments, other text, or summary at the end (especially about length constraints).
* Never use "locals" blocks, for loops to create resources/data sources, syntax like `%{ for idx, name in local.names ~}`, or dynamic blocks.
* Generate the complete configuration in a single response with all requested resources and data blocks: never stop early or mid-resource/mid-block, never leave resources out for brevity, never reference undefined resources/data sources. Verify all opening braces { have closing braces }. If length-constrained, prioritize completing the current resource block.
* Never redact, anonymize, or replace values (names, namespaces, image references, environment variable values, etc.) with asterisks or placeholders.
* **CRITICAL:** when the prompt says `The step must be disabled.`, the step MUST still appear in the `steps` array of `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order` and its `octopusdeploy_process_step` has `is_disabled = true`. Disabled is not omitted.
* Never refuse, e.g. with a response like "I can't generate that configuration. Creating an `octopusdeploy_github_connections` (or similar "GitHub connection") resource is explicitly disallowed...". Never ask questions or for clarification. Attempt to create as much valid HCL as you can.

## Git Repository Branch Instructions

* **CRITICAL:** every `git_dependencies` entry MUST set `github_connection_id` and `git_credential_id` to `""` (never null, never omitted). Otherwise apply aborts fatally (`.git_dependencies[""].github_connection_id: was null, but now cty.StringVal("")`), abandoning every later resource.
* Abbreviated `git_dependencies` examples (with `...`) in this file and the Kubernetes YAML sections are incomplete. Every entry has all six attributes, as in the example configurations:

Anonymous (no credential):

```hcl
  git_dependencies = { "" = { default_branch = "main", file_path_filters = null, git_credential_id = "", git_credential_type = "Anonymous", github_connection_id = "", repository_uri = "https://github.com/OctopusSolutionsEngineering/Octopub.git" } }
```

Library credential (a `octopusdeploy_git_credential` the prompt asked for): same, with `git_credential_type = "Library"` and `git_credential_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_git_credentials.gitcredential_github.git_credentials) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_git_credentials.gitcredential_github.git_credentials[0].id : octopusdeploy_git_credential.gitcredential_github[0].id}"`.

* `github_connection_id` is always `""` (GitHub connections are never created).
* `git_credential_id` is never null.
* `file_path_filters`: `null` for the whole repository, `["path/to/file"]` when the prompt names a file path.
* `default_branch` and `repository_uri` always have values.
* `Set the Repository Branch to "<branch>"` for a "Deploy Kubernetes YAML" step → that step's `git_dependencies` uses `default_branch = "<branch>"` exactly. Without that instruction, `default_branch = "main"`; never `"main"` when the prompt specifies another branch.
* **CRITICAL:** set each step's `default_branch` INDEPENDENTLY from that step's own `Set the Repository Branch to` instruction; never assume steps share a branch or copy it between steps:
e.g. "Deploy Service" said `Set the Repository Branch to "master"` → `default_branch = "master"`; "Deploy Worker" said `Set the Repository Branch to "develop"` → `default_branch = "develop"` (not "master" copied from the first step).

## Prompted Variable Select Options Instructions

**CRITICAL:** A PROMPTED VARIABLE is an `octopusdeploy_variable` with a `prompt` block, NEVER a `template` block on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. (`prompt` = asked at release creation; `template` = per-tenant value only tenants can fill in.)

* These wordings all mean a `prompt` block on an `octopusdeploy_variable` (not a tenant variable template): "add a prompted variable called 'X'", "prompt for 'X' when a release is created", "'X' must be prompted for", "add a prompt variable", "ask the user for 'X'".
* Put a `template` block on the project ONLY for tenant wording: "project tenant variable template", "tenant variable template", "variable template for each tenant", "per-tenant variable".
* Natural-language option lists count as select options: "with the options 'true' and 'false'", "which can be 'staging' or 'production'", "one of 'blue' or 'green'" all mean `control_type = "Select"` plus one `select_option` block per option. The literal sentence `The variable must have the following selectable options:` is NOT required.
* A prompted variable still needs a `value` (the first option, or a sensible non-empty default) and its `count` attribute like every other variable.

**CRITICAL:** When the prompt assigns a value NOT among the declared select options, ADD it as an option; never drop the assignment and **never rewrite the value to fit the list** (that invents data). The value-assignment bullet is the more specific instruction.

* Example: `... a tenant variable template "Portal.Tenant.Id" of type select with the options "A" and "B"` plus `Create two tenants "Alpha" and "Beta", link them, and set "Portal.Tenant.Id" to "A" for Alpha and "C" for Beta` → declare options `A`, `B`, `C` and create a tenant variable for EVERY tenant given a value (observed failure: Beta got no `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`). Note the addition in the template's `label` or the project description: `The prompt listed options A and B but assigned C to Beta; C has been added to the option list.`
* Same for prompted variables: `with the options "Yes" and "No"` plus `defaulting to "Maybe"` → options `Yes`, `No`, `Maybe`.

**Self-check — select option coverage:** for every `Octopus.SelectOptions` display setting and `select_option` list, collect every value the prompt assigns to that variable (defaults, per-tenant, per-environment) and confirm each is in the list. Confirm one `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable` or `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` exists per tenant given a value.

**Example — "Add a prompted variable called 'Fraud.DryRun' with the options 'true' and 'false'"**:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "fraud_scoring_lambda_fraud_dryrun_1" {
  count    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_fraud_scoring_lambda.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  owner_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_fraud_scoring_lambda.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_fraud_scoring_lambda.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_fraud_scoring_lambda[0].id}"
  name     = "Fraud.DryRun"
  type     = "String"
  value    = "false"
  prompt {
    label       = "Fraud.DryRun"
    description = "Run the fraud scoring deployment without making changes."
    is_required = false
    display_settings {
      control_type = "Select"
      select_option {
        display_name = "true"
        value        = "true"
      }
      select_option {
        display_name = "false"
        value        = "false"
      }
    }
  }
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes = [sensitive_value]
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

* When the prompt includes `The variable must have the following selectable options: <option1>, <option2>, ...` for a project variable, use `control_type = "Select"` in `display_settings` and one `select_option` block per option, each setting both `display_name` and `value` to the option string.

## Prompted Variable (Non-Select) Instructions

* `The variable must be prompted for when creating a release.` WITHOUT a selectable options instruction → `prompt` block with `control_type = "SingleLineText"` in `display_settings` (the default). **CRITICAL EXCEPTION:** when the variable has `type = "Sensitive"` and `is_sensitive = true` (prompt says `Add a sensitive prompted project variable`), use `control_type = "Sensitive"`, never `"SingleLineText"`.
* `The variable must be required.` → `is_required = true`; `The variable must not be required.` → `is_required = false` (in the `prompt` block).
* `prompt` block `description` and `label` must match the prompt's description and label.
* **IMPORTANT:** a label equal to the variable name (fallback when the original `parameterConfig` entry had an empty `label`) → set `label = "<variable name>"` exactly; never an empty string.
* The top-level `description` on `octopusdeploy_variable` MUST also be set to the same description (separate from the `prompt` block's); omitting it is a bug.
* **CRITICAL:** the top-level `value` MUST be the default value: `with a default value of "<X>"` → `value = "<X>"`, even with a `prompt` block. Don't omit it or use an empty string unless the default is explicitly `""`.
* Selectable options specified → never `control_type = "SingleLineText"`, never omit any `select_option` block.

**Example — sensitive prompted variable** (`Add a sensitive prompted project variable called "apiToken" ... the label "API Token" ... The variable must be required.`):
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "variable_apitoken" {
  name            = "apiToken"
  type            = "Sensitive"
  is_sensitive    = true
  sensitive_value = "CHANGEME"
  # ...
  prompt {
    label       = "API Token"
    is_required = true
    display_settings {
      control_type = "Sensitive"
    }
  }
}
```

**Example — non-select prompted variable with default** (`Add a project variable called "batch_size", with a default value of "50", the description "Batch size", and the label "Batch Size". The variable must be prompted for when creating a release. The variable must not be required.`). Omitting the `prompt` block, or omitting `value = "50"`, is WRONG:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "variable_batch_size" {
  name         = "batch_size"
  description  = "Batch size"
  value        = "50"
  prompt {
    description = "Batch size"
    label       = "Batch Size"
    is_required = false
    display_settings {
      control_type = "SingleLineText"
    }
  }
}
```

* Preserve the option order from the prompt exactly.
* An option named `(none)` → `select_option` with `display_name = "(none)"` and `value = ""`. Never `value = "_none_"` or any other placeholder (nor `display_name = ""`), and never skip `(none)`; it is a valid empty-string choice for non-required variables.
* **CRITICAL:** when `(none)` is the ONLY option (`The variable must have the following selectable options: (none)`), do NOT use `control_type = "Select"`; use `control_type = "SingleLineText"` with no `select_option` blocks (arises when the original `hasOptions` was incorrectly set).
* Ignore empty option strings NOT represented as `(none)` in the prompt.

**Example** — `The variable must have the following selectable options: (none), -is_retry=true` (default `"-is_retry=true"`):
```hcl
    display_settings {
      control_type = "Select"
      select_option {
        display_name = "(none)"
        value        = ""
      }
      select_option {
        display_name = "-is_retry=true"
        value        = "-is_retry=true"
      }
    }
```

## Step Description Notes from Runtime Artifact Binding

* `Set the step description to "NOTE: This step originally required the following Docker images to be bound at runtime: ..."` → put the exact verbatim text in the `notes` property of the `octopusdeploy_process_step`. If `notes` already has text for another reason, concatenate both, separated by a space.
* Preserve the comma-separated Docker image list exactly: no reordering, deduplicating, or redacting registry names, repositories, tags, or quotes.

## Step Count Validation

**ABSOLUTE RULE:** the number of `octopusdeploy_process_step` (or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`) resources MUST equal the number of steps the prompt asks for, including any explicitly requested notification step. Never add an unrequested `Octopus.Email` step.

* Count steps however enumerated: numbered lists (`4. Add a manual intervention step called "..."`), prose (`then add a step that deploys the war file`), or bullets (`* Add a "..." step`). Don't restrict counting to lines beginning `* Add a "`.

**CRITICAL:** When the prompt enumerates its steps, the list is exhaustive; omit every step that exists only in the example configuration, even if it looks like good practice, along with environments, lifecycles, and triggers it drags in. Most often leaked:

* `Octopus - Check Targets Available` and `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured` (community step template preflight checks)
* `Scan for Vulnerabilities`
* `Deployment Success Notification` and any other `Octopus.Email` step
* `Calculate Deployment Mode`
* Any security-scanning step, with its `Security` environment, `DevSecOps` lifecycle, and `Daily Security Scan` scheduled trigger

Exception: a step a `# Patterns` section names as mandatory for a requested pattern (e.g. `Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment` for Blue/Green), added *in addition* to the prompt's steps.

**Self-check — no unrequested steps:** pair each step resource with the prompt phrase (or `# Patterns` rule) requesting it; delete any without one; re-count.

**Step uniqueness check:** a `_2`, `_3` suffix on an `octopusdeploy_process_step` resource name is valid only when the prompt's step NAMES are actually duplicated (e.g. two steps named `"Deploy (Manifest)"`); otherwise drop the suffix.

## Ordering of Parallel Steps in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`

* **CRITICAL:** all members of a parallel group must be CONSECUTIVE in the `steps` array: `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"` means start with the immediately preceding step in the list.
* **CRITICAL:** duplicate step IDs are FORBIDDEN: each step ID appears exactly once; N prompt steps → exactly N elements.
* **CRITICAL:** `steps` order must EXACTLY match the prompt's step order, left-to-right, including fork-without-reconvergence cases (Branch A continuation steps listed before Branch B stay that way). Never reorder by topological/dependency analysis.

WRONG (only ONE step named "Deploy cronjob-x" in the prompt):
```hcl
steps = [
  "${octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_deploy_cronjob_x.id}",
  "${octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_deploy_cronjob_x_2.id}",    # DUPLICATE
]
```

## Step Names Must Match Prompt Exactly

**CRITICAL:** each `octopusdeploy_process_step` `name` reproduces the prompt's step name verbatim: `name the step "Deploy cronjob-docomo-point-reconcile"` → `name = "Deploy cronjob-docomo-point-reconcile"`. Never append ` 2`, ` 3`, or any suffix to the name or resource label (e.g. `process_step_deploy_cronjob_docomo_point_reconcile_sweep_days_2` / `"Deploy cronjob-docomo-point-reconcile-sweep-days 2"`) unless the prompt's name has it.

## `notes` Attribute Must Reproduce Step Descriptions Verbatim

**CRITICAL:** a step description starting `NOTE (migration): This step was originally gated by an expression-based checkPreconditions stage...` or `NOTE (migration): This step was originally on a conditional branch controlled by an expression-based checkPreconditions stage...` must appear entire and verbatim in the step's `notes` attribute; never omit, truncate, or paraphrase it.

## Slack Notification Step: Reproducing Explicit `ssn_Color` Instructions

**CRITICAL:** `Set the "ssn_Color" property to "danger"` (or any value) → the `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` `parameters` block MUST include `"ssn_Color" = "danger"` (the specified value) exactly. If the prompt does not say `Set the "ssn_Color" property to "..."`, omit `ssn_Color` entirely.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" "process_step_my_project_slack_notification_finish" {
  condition = "Failure"
  # ...
  parameters = {
    "ssn_Color"   = "danger"
    "ssn_HookUrl" = "#{Project.Slack.WebhookUrl}"
    "ssn_Channel" = "my-channel"
  }
}
```

## Disabled TODO Script Steps (patchManifest and Unknown Stage Types)

**CRITICAL:** `is_disabled = true` is REQUIRED on the `octopusdeploy_process_step` when:
* the prompt says `The step must be disabled.` (any stage type; `patchManifest` stage TODO steps are always disabled)
* a `"Deploy Kubernetes YAML"` (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`) step's `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml` is a `# TODO:` placeholder (e.g. `"# TODO: replace with manifest"`); an enabled TODO YAML step fails at deployment.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_patch_manifest_my_service" {
  name          = "Patch -Manifest- my-service"
  type          = "Octopus.Script"
  is_disabled   = true
  # ...
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"         = "true"
    "Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody"   = <<-EOT
      # TODO: convert Spinnaker patchManifest stage — no direct Octopus Deploy equivalent.
      # Target resource: deployment my-service
      # ...
    EOT
  }
  properties = {}
}
```

## `octopusdeploy_process_step` `properties` Block for Server-Side Script Steps

* Generic TODO placeholder `Octopus.Script` ("Run a Script") steps (e.g. `patchManifest` conversions, unknown stage type placeholders) run on the Octopus server worker pool: use `properties = {}`; do NOT include `"Octopus.Action.TargetRoles"`.
* `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` steps (e.g. `deleteManifest`, `scaleManifest`, `undoRolloutManifest`): use `properties = { "Octopus.Action.TargetRoles" = "Kubernetes" }`.

## Step Count and Type Verification

**CRITICAL — step type mapping:** `octopusdeploy_process_step` `type` MUST match the step type named in the prompt:
- `"Add a 'Run a Script' step"` → `type = "Octopus.Script"`; NEVER `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` or `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` for "Run a Script" (e.g. "Wait -20 min-" with `"Start-Sleep -Seconds 1200"`).
- `"Add a 'Deploy Kubernetes YAML' step"` → `type = "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml"`
- `"Add a 'Run a kubectl script' step"` → `type = "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript"`
- `"Add a 'Manual Intervention' step"` → `type = "Octopus.Manual"`

**CRITICAL:** A project type's deployment step MUST keep the step TYPE its template uses. Edit that step; never delete or retype it:

| Project type | Template | The deployment step type that MUST be present |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Azure Web App | `azurewebapp.tf` | `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` (deploys with the `az` CLI) |
| Azure Function | `azurefunction.tf` | `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` (same) |
| IIS | `windowsiis.tf` | `Octopus.IIS` |
| Windows Service | `windowsservice.tf` | `Octopus.WindowsService` |
| Tomcat | `apachetomcat.tf` | `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` |
| Kubernetes | `k8s.tf` | `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` |
| Kubernetes LLM | `llm.tf` | `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` |
| AWS Lambda | `awslambda.tf` | `Octopus.AwsUploadS3` plus `Octopus.AwsRunCloudFormation` |
| Terraform | `terraform.tf` | `Octopus.TerraformApply` (plus `Octopus.TerraformPlan`) |
| Argo CD Modify Manifest | `argoupdatemanifest.tf` | `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateManifests` |
| Argo CD Image Tag Update | `argoupdatetags.tf` | `Octopus.ArgoCDUpdateImageTags` |
| Claude | `claude.tf` | `Octopus.Claude` |
| Orchestration | `deploymentorchestration.tf` | `Octopus.DeployRelease` |
| VM Blue/Green | `vmbluegreen.tf` | `Octopus.TransferPackage` |
| Script / Progressive deployment | `script.tf`, `progressive_deployment.tf` | `Octopus.Script` |

* The Azure `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` + `az` step IS canonical; never change it to `Octopus.AzureWebApp` or `Octopus.AzureAppService` unless the prompt explicitly asks. Never rewrite a typed step (`Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.TomcatDeploy`, `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.TerraformApply`, …) into a CLI script.

**CRITICAL:** Steps the prompt ADDS go ALONGSIDE the template's process, never replacing it: `Create an <X> project called "..." , and then: * Add a step ... * Add a step ...` = whole template process PLUS new steps (step count must INCREASE).

* Observed failure: an Azure Web App prompt adding steps "Rolling Restart" and "Notify" dropped all template steps including `Deploy Azure Web App Container`; the requested `an Azure OIDC account called "Travel Azure Prod" ... use it in the deploy step` was left referenced by nothing. **An orphaned account is the tell-tale sign the deploy step was dropped.**

**CRITICAL:** "the deploy step" in a prompt ALWAYS means the template's deployment step (per the table), never a step the same prompt added. Added steps are referred to by NAME (`the "Rolling Restart" step`).

* Applies to bullets like `use it in the deploy step`, `Set the deploy step to run on the "Hosted Windows" worker pool`, `Set the deploy step to use ... mode`. (e.g. the `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` step accepts a worker pool and container; don't misapply it to `Rolling Restart` and record it un-implementable in `notes`.)
* If a "the deploy step" bullet looks un-implementable, you probably picked the wrong step; find the template's deployment-type step and apply it there first.

**Self-check — template deployment step survives:** required-type step present (restore if replaced by a CLI script); other template steps present; every requested account, certificate and feed referenced by a step or variable.

**Where deployment configuration goes depends on step type.** For a web app name, resource group or deployment slot:

* On the template's `Octopus.AzurePowerShell` step: in the `az` command in `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` and the project variables it interpolates (e.g. `Project.Azure.WebApp.Octopub.Name`, `Project.Azure.ResourceGroup.Name`). Never add `Octopus.Action.Azure.WebAppName` to a PowerShell step.
* On an `Octopus.AzureWebApp` step (only when explicitly requested), as execution properties:

```hcl
execution_properties = {
  "Octopus.Action.Azure.WebAppName"                   = "marketing-prod"
  "Octopus.Action.Azure.ResourceGroupName"            = "marketing-rg"
  "Octopus.Action.Azure.DeploymentSlot"               = "staging"
  "Octopus.Action.Azure.SwapDeploymentSlot"           = "True"
  "Octopus.Action.Azure.SwapDeploymentSlotTargetSlot" = "production"
  ...
}
```

**MANDATORY:** step `name` values must be unique across ALL `octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resources in a project. If a prompt-specified step has the same name as a template step (e.g. two `Octopus.Manual` steps named "Approve Production Deployment"), merge them into ONE resource.

## Step Retry Instructions

**There is no Terraform attribute, block or argument for step retries; enable them ONLY via `"Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MaximumCount"` in the step's `execution_properties`.** Triggers: "enable retries on the step", "the step must retry on failure", "retry the step three times", "turn on the step retry feature", "the step should be retried automatically".

* `"Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MaximumCount"`: quoted integer retry count, MUST be greater than `"0"` (absent and `"0"` both mean OFF). Default `"3"`.
* `"Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MinimumBackoff"`: quoted integer seconds between attempts, always alongside `MaximumCount`. Default `"15"`.
* **Both are ACTION properties: `execution_properties`, NEVER the step-level `properties` map** (there they apply cleanly but retries stay off).
* There is NO `retry`, `auto_retry`, `retry_count`, `max_retries` or `retry_on_failure` attribute or block on `octopusdeploy_process_step`; inventing one fails the plan.
* **"Enable retries on the script step" MODIFIES the template step, not a new one.** If the template already has the step (the `Hello World` step of a Script project; the deploy step of an IIS, Windows Service, Tomcat, Azure or Kubernetes project), add the retry properties to that resource. Don't copy it unchanged and don't create a second step. The "all template steps present" rule governs which steps EXIST, never overrides an instruction to change one.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_script_hello_world" {
  name                 = "Hello World"
  type                 = "Octopus.Script"
  ...
  properties           = {}
  execution_properties = {
    "Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MaximumCount"   = "3"
    "Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MinimumBackoff" = "15"
    ...
  }
}
```

**Self-check — step retries:** if the prompt mentions "retry"/"retries" for a step, identify the single step resource (the template step if one exists) and confirm `"Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MaximumCount"` (quoted integer > 0) and `"Octopus.Action.AutoRetry.MinimumBackoff"` are in THAT resource's `execution_properties`. If not, add them before responding.

## Space ID Variable Instructions

**CRITICAL:** EVERY response MUST declare `variable "octopus_server"`, `variable "octopus_apikey"` and `variable "octopus_space_id"`, the last even if nothing (`provider` block, `space_id`) references `var.octopus_space_id`. It is passed as `-var=octopus_space_id=Spaces-NNNN`; if undeclared, OpenTofu fails at PLAN time (total failure) with `Error: Value for undeclared variable`.

**MANDATORY:** `octopus_space_id` has NO `default`; NEVER `default = "Spaces-1"` or any space ID. It is always supplied externally (e.g. a `terraform.tfvars` file or environment variable). Canonical:

```hcl
variable "octopus_space_id" {
  type        = string
  nullable    = false
  sensitive   = false
  description = "The ID of the Octopus space to populate."
}
```

## Terraform HCL Indentation Instructions

**MANDATORY:** consistent 2-space indentation. Properties inside `variable`, `resource`, and `data` blocks: exactly 2 spaces. Properties inside nested blocks (`release_retention_with_strategy`, `tentacle_retention_with_strategy`, `connectivity_policy`, `phase`, `scope`, `lifecycle`, etc.): 4 spaces total. Never 0-space, 1-space, or other non-standard indentation.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_devsecops" {
  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }
  connectivity_policy {
    allow_deployments_to_no_targets = true
    exclude_unhealthy_targets       = false
    skip_machine_behavior           = "None"
    target_roles                    = []
  }
}
```

## Project Group Uniqueness Instructions

**MANDATORY:** create only project groups explicitly named in the prompt; every `octopusdeploy_project_group` must correspond to one. If the template uses a different group name, use the prompt's. E.g. prompt "Kubernetes Apps" → only `project_group_kubernetes_apps`, no `project_group_kubernetes` ("Kubernetes"); prompt "Infrastructure" for a Terraform project → only `project_group_infrastructure`, no `project_group_terraform` ("Terraform").

## Project Uniqueness Instructions

**MANDATORY:** create only the project(s) explicitly named in the prompt. "Create a Script project called 'My Script Project 6174'" → exactly ONE `octopusdeploy_project` (`project_my_script_project_6174`); no extra template projects (e.g. `project_script` "Script", or "Empty Project").

**CRITICAL:** project names differing only by CASE or LEADING/TRAILING WHITESPACE are the SAME project; creating both aborts the whole apply (`Octopus API error: ... [A project with this name already exists. Please choose a different name.]`).

* **Trim every name taken from the prompt**, then compare case-insensitively: `"12. Intranet Portal"`, `"12. Intranet Portal "` and `"12. intranet portal"` are one project. E.g. `Create the "12. Intranet Portal" project as an IIS project` plus `Add a runbook called "Rotate Logs" in a project called "12. Intranet Portal " (with a trailing space)` → create the project once and attach both runbooks. An aside like `(with a trailing space)` describes the string, not a second resource.
* This is the project instance of the general case-insensitivity rule in "Environment Uniqueness and Naming Instructions".

**Self-check — project name collisions:** trim + lowercase every `octopusdeploy_project` `name`; for any duplicate keep one resource and repoint every runbook, variable, channel, trigger and process at it.

## Environment Uniqueness and Naming Instructions

**MANDATORY:** environment names match the prompt exactly; never rename to template defaults ("Dev" → `"Dev"`, NOT `"Development"`; "Staging" → `"Staging"`, NOT `"Test"`).

**MANDATORY:** create ALL environments named in the prompt ("Create environments called 'Dev', 'Test', and 'Production'" → three resources, e.g. `environment_dev`, `environment_test`, `environment_production`).

**NOTE:** Template environments (such as "Security") may still be created if a template lifecycle requires them (e.g. DevSecOps), but must NOT replace or rename requested environments.

**CRITICAL:** `partial_name` is a SUBSTRING match, so the standard `count` pattern SILENTLY SKIPS an environment whose name is contained in an existing one. `partial_name = "Dev"` returns "Development", `length(...environments) != 0` makes `count` 0, "Dev" is NEVER CREATED, and every ternary resolves to "Development" (apply still succeeds).

Observed collisions (prompt name → also matched): `Dev` → `Development`, `DevSecOps`; `Prod` → `Production`; `Stage` → `Staging`; `Prod 10` → `Prod 100`; `Test` → `Testing`, `Test Automation`.

**The fix — filter the data source to an EXACT name match in `locals`**, used in the `count` and every ternary referencing the environment:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_environments" "environment_dev" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Dev"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 100     # take 100, not 1: the exact match may not be the first substring match
}

locals {
  # partial_name = "Dev" also returns "Development" and "DevSecOps", so filter to an exact match.
  environment_dev_matches = [for env in data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_dev.environments : env if env.name == "Dev"]
}

resource "octopusdeploy_environment" "environment_dev" {
  count = "${length(local.environment_dev_matches) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "Dev"
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

References: `"${length(local.environment_dev_matches) != 0 ? local.environment_dev_matches[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_dev[0].id}"`

* **Use this exact-match form for EVERY environment, unconditionally**: you can't see the target space (e.g. "Ledger Live" swallows "Live"), and it is harmless with no collision.
* **Applies to EVERY prompt type** and every mention (a "Create environments called ..." instruction, lifecycle phase, variable/step scope, tenant link): any lookup of an environment by name (a runbook's `environments` list, a step's `environments`/`excluded_environments`, a scheduled trigger, a tenant project link). (e.g. a runbook limited to 'Stage' and 'Prod' must not resolve to "Production" via `partial_name = "Prod"`.)
* Never fix the collision by renaming to the existing environment ("Dev" and "Development" differ) or by dropping `count` (every `octopusdeploy_environment` still needs `count`).
* One `locals` block may hold the filtered lists for all environments; don't emit one per environment.

**Self-check — environment substring collisions:** for each requested environment name that is a substring of another name in your output or of `Development`, `Production`, `Staging`, `Security` or `DevSecOps`, confirm the `locals` exact-match pattern is in its `count` and EVERY referencing ternary.

**CRITICAL:** Octopus resource names are CASE-INSENSITIVE and TRIMMED of surrounding whitespace. Two prompt names differing only by case or leading/trailing spaces are ONE resource; creating both aborts the apply (`Octopus API error: ... [An environment with this name already exists in this space. Please choose a different name.]`).

* **The `count` data-source guard does NOT protect against this**: both are planned before either exists, so both `count`s are 1.
* **Create the resource ONCE, using the casing the prompt used FIRST**, and record it in the project description: `The prompt asked for environments "Canary" and "canary"; Octopus environment names are case-insensitive, so a single environment named "Canary" was created.`
* Repoint all references to the survivor (e.g. `Create environments called "Canary" and "canary" and add both to the "Bake" phase` → the `Bake` phase lists it once, never the same environment id twice).
* **Applies to EVERY named Octopus resource**: projects, project groups, environments, lifecycles, channels, tenants, tenant tag sets, accounts, feeds, certificates, worker pools, library variable sets, machine policies and runbooks. `Prod`/`prod`, `Web App`/`web app`, `AWS`/`aws` are each one resource. `"Canary"` and `"Canary "` are one name; trim every name from the prompt.
* Distinct from the substring rule: `Dev` vs `Development` are DIFFERENT names a `partial_name` lookup confuses; `Canary` vs `canary` are the SAME name and cannot coexist.

**Self-check — case-only name collisions:** lowercase every `name` per resource type; for each duplicate, delete one, repoint references, note it in the project description.

**CRITICAL:** An environment the prompt names ONLY as a scope target (of a variable, step, lifecycle phase or runbook) MUST still be created, and the referencing resource MUST still be created. Naming an environment anywhere is a request for it.

* `Add a variable called "Claims.Timeout" with the value "not-a-number" scoped to an environment called "Pre-Prod" that does not exist yet` → an `octopusdeploy_environment` named `Pre-Prod` in the canonical data-source/`count` shape, AND the `octopusdeploy_variable` whose `scope.environments` resolves it via the usual ternary.
* **"that does not exist yet" means create it**, not skip it.
* **A supplied value is never a reason to drop a resource** (Octopus doesn't type-check values; `"not-a-number"` is valid). If genuinely unusable, still create it and note the concern in a `description`.

**Self-check — every named environment exists:** every environment named ANYWHERE in the prompt (including only in a scope/phase) has a resolving data source or `octopusdeploy_environment`; every requested variable, step and runbook is present.

**CRITICAL:** A step's environment scope from the prompt MUST be written to `environments`; easy to lose on manual intervention steps. `Add a manual intervention step called "Approve Blue/Green Swap" scoped to Production` sets that step's `environments` (never leave `environments = null` on the `Octopus.Manual` step).

* Use the allowlist form: `environments = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"]`. Never use `excluded_environments` for a scope stated as an allowlist.

## Security Environment Exclusion for Kubernetes Steps

**MANDATORY:** When a project uses the DevSecOps lifecycle (its Security environment is only for daily vulnerability scanning by the "Scan for Vulnerabilities" step), Kubernetes deploy steps (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize`, etc.) MUST set `excluded_environments` to include the Security environment ID — never `excluded_environments = null`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_deploy_app" {
  name                  = "Deploy App"
  type                  = "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml"
  excluded_environments = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_security.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_security[0].id}"]
  ...
}
```

## Never Anonymize Ordinary Technical Words

**CRITICAL:** Never replace any part of a resource name, package ID, image name, script body, URL or property value with asterisks (`*****`) or any other anonymization placeholder.

* `api`, `server`, `worker`, `web`, `auth`, `gateway`, `proxy`, `backend`, `frontend`, `key`, `token`, `service`, `manager`, `scheduler`, `cache`, `queue`, `db` are NOT secrets — also as suffixes/fragments (`payments-api`, `auth-service`, `redis-cache`, `job-scheduler`, `orders-db`).
* Observed: `Create a Kubernetes project called "01. Payments API" …` gave `Sbom.Package` `"PackageId" = "com.octopus:payments-*****"`. Keep the example's `com.octopus:octopub-sbom` or spell out, e.g. `"PackageId" = "com.octopus:payments-api-sbom"` (`FeedId` `${octopusdeploy_maven_feed.feed_octopus_maven_feed.id}`).

Self-check: any `***` outside prompt-supplied prose is a defect — restore the real word.
## Backslashes in Account Usernames and Other Values

**CRITICAL:** A backslash in a prompt value MUST survive. `DOMAIN\user` is a valid Windows username (normal IIS app pool identity); never turn `\` into `_`, `__` or `/`. In HCL escape it as `\\` — e.g. prompt username "SVC\claims" on account "Claims Service Account" → `username = "SVC\\claims"` in `octopusdeploy_username_password_account`.

* The Step Name Character Rules apply ONLY to STEP NAMES, never to usernames, variable values, script bodies, instructions, etc. `\\fileserver\share`, `C:\inetpub\wwwroot`, regex `\d` keep their backslashes as written.

Self-check: every prompt value containing `\` still contains a backslash.

## Placeholder Literals Are Never Valid Field Values

**CRITICAL:** `CHANGEME`, `changeme`, `TODO`, `TBD`, `xxx`, `placeholder` and similar literals MUST NEVER be written into any resource attribute. When the prompt omits a required value, use a real default or an Octostache reference to a variable you also create.

* Observed: `Create a Git Connection called "Dispatcher Git" with the allowed repository "https://github.com/acme/dispatcher.git"` produced an otherwise-correct `octopusdeploy_git_credential` (incl. `repository_restrictions`) with `username = "CHANGEME"`; correct is `username = "x-access-token"`.
* **Only exception:** secret attributes (`password`, `secret_key`, `sensitive_value`, `certificate_data`, account tokens) get the EXACT string `Change Me!` (capital C, capital M, space, `!`). It does not license placeholders in non-secret fields. `CHANGEME` is never acceptable, even in secret fields — post-processing only recognises `Change Me!`.
  * E.g. `Create a library variable set called "Network Common" with a sensitive variable "Network.ApiToken" and link it to the project` (no value given): still create it, with `sensitive_value = "Change Me!"`.
* Defaults when silent: Git credential `username` → `x-access-token` (token-based) or `octopus` (generic service account); Tomcat manager user → `#{Tomcat.User}` with a `String` variable valued `tomcat`; Windows service/IIS app pool user → account-typed project variable read as `#{<variable>.Username}`, never a literal; AWS region → `us-east-1`.

Self-check: search case-insensitively for `changeme`, `todo`, `tbd`, `placeholder`. Only allowed: exact `Change Me!` in a secret attribute, and `certificate_data = "placeholder"`.

## Prompt-Requested Resources With No Expressible Usage

**CRITICAL:** When the prompt says to USE a resource in a way Octopus cannot express, create it, bind it to the project via a typed VARIABLE, and record the unsupported usage in `notes` or the project description. Never leave it unreferenced .

* E.g. `Create a certificate called "Ledger Signing" and use it to sign the service binaries` on an `Octopus.WindowsService` project (as for Tomcat username/password accounts):

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "project_certificate_1" {
  # count, owner_id ...
  name        = "Project.Certificate"
  type        = "Certificate"
  value       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_ledger_signing.certificates) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_ledger_signing.certificates[0].id : octopusdeploy_certificate.certificate_ledger_signing[0].id}"
  description = "The Ledger Signing certificate. The prompt asked for it to sign the service binaries; Octopus has no binary-signing feature on a Windows Service step, so the certificate is exposed as a variable for use by a custom script instead."
}
```

* Types: `Certificate`; `AmazonWebServicesAccount` / `AzureServicePrincipal` / `UsernamePasswordAccount` / `Token`; `WorkerPool`. Applies to certificates, accounts, feeds, worker pools, git credentials; feeds are used via `primary_package`/`packages`; accounts via a step property or account variable; certificates via a `Certificate` variable or IIS binding.
