Self-check: every prompt-requested `octopusdeploy_certificate`, `octopusdeploy_*_account`, `octopusdeploy_*_feed`, `octopusdeploy_git_credential` is referenced by a step property, `primary_package`, `git_dependencies` entry, or typed `octopusdeploy_variable`.

**Workers:** examples contain only `octopusdeploy_ssh_connection_worker` and `octopusdeploy_static_worker_pool`. A Windows (Tentacle) worker needs an unknowable thumbprint and URI.
* For `Create a Windows worker called "Ledger Worker" in a worker pool called "Ledger Pool"`: create the pool; do NOT invent a Tentacle worker resource type/attributes (risks `Unsupported block type` at PLAN, losing everything); record the shortfall in the project description, e.g. `The prompt asked for a Windows worker called "Ledger Worker" in the "Ledger Pool" worker pool. The worker pool has been created; the worker itself requires a Tentacle thumbprint and URI that cannot be known here, so it must be registered against the pool by hand.`
* SSH/Linux worker → `octopusdeploy_ssh_connection_worker` with non-empty `account_id`, `fingerprint`, `host` (Worker Instructions).


## Variable Scoping for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL:** `actions` in a `scope` block MUST always be `null`. `octopusdeploy_process_step.<name>.id` is a step ID, not the action ID `Scope.Actions` needs: `actions = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_drain_connections.id]` shows `Creation complete`/`Apply complete!` but the variable is silently discarded.

* For `scoped to the "<Step Name>" step`: no `actions`; scope to any environments/channels/tenant tags/target tags also named. If the step was the ONLY scope, OMIT the `scope` block — an empty or all-`null` block aborts the apply (`.scope: block count changed from 1 to 0`). Still create the variable; never drop it.
* **ONE VALUE PER STEP for one name → ONE unscoped variable with the FIRST value; record the rest in `description`** (list-shortfall rule). E.g. `Add a project variable "Canary.Weight" with the value "10" scoped to the "Shift 10%" step, "50" scoped to "Shift 50%", and "100" scoped to "Shift 100%"` → `value = "10"` (not `100`), description like `The prompt also asked for 50 on "Shift 50%" and 100 on "Shift 100%". Octopus cannot scope a variable to a step, so a single unscoped variable holding the first value is created; per-step values must be set inside each step's script instead.`

**CRITICAL:** Environment-scoped variables MUST list those environment IDs using the ternary:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "my_project_connection_string_production" {
  # count, owner_id, name, type, value ...
  scope {
    environments = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"]
  }
  # lifecycle, depends_on ...
}
```

Unscoped variable → no `scope` block (never `scope {}`). Use only correct, existing environment names.

**CRITICAL:** A scope dimension (`processes`, `environments`, `channels`, `roles`, `tenant_tags`) appears only when the prompt asks for it. Set `processes` only when the prompt explicitly ties the variable to a runbook ("a variable used only by the 'Restart Server' runbook", "scoped to the runbook"); in a runbook-focused prompt, "Add a project variable X with the value Y" is unscoped (observed wrong: `processes = ["${octopusdeploy_runbook.runbook_web_app_restart_server.id}"]` for `Project.Restart.Timeout`).

**CRITICAL:** NEVER scope to the project's built-in "Default" channel, and no scope list may contain `""`. The Default channel doesn't exist yet during the apply, so the `data "octopusdeploy_channels"` ternary yields `channels = [""]` and the apply dies (`These variables have the same name and scope`).
* Default channel scope → ignore it; use the other named scopes, or no `scope` block if it was the only one.
* Channel-scope only to a named `octopusdeploy_channel` created in the same output (e.g. "Hotfix", "Canary"): `channels = ["${octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_hotfix[0].id}"]`.
* Lists (`environments`, `channels`, `tenant_tags`, `roles`, `machines`) contain only non-empty values; drop any dimension whose ternary could yield `""`.

**CRITICAL:** Same NAME + identical SCOPE is rejected and kills the apply. Emit such a variable ONCE with the LAST value given (later supersedes earlier). E.g. `Add a variable called "Checkout.Timeout" with value "30" scoped to the Gold tag, and another with value "60" also scoped to the Gold tag` → one `Checkout.Timeout` = `60` (not two with `tenant_tags = ["Tier/Gold"]`).
* Identical = all of `environments`, `channels`, `tenant_tags`, `roles`, `machines`, `processes` equal (absent = empty, incl. unscoped). Differing in ANY dimension (Gold vs Silver) → emit both. NEVER create same-`name`, same-owner variables without a distinguishing scope.
* Never invent a scope or rename to keep both values.

Self-check: compare every variable's (`name`, sorted resolved scope) — no identical pairs, no `""`, no `data.octopusdeploy_channels` "Default" lookup.

## Environment-Scoped Variables — API GET Response Shape

Per-environment values appear in `GET /projects/{id}/variables` as separate `Variables` entries (own `Id` and `Value`, same `Name`, different `Scope.Environment`). Standard model, not duplication — count as one logical variable when verifying.

## Phase-Level Retention Policy Omitted = Inherit from Lifecycle

An `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` `phase` accepts its own `release_retention_with_strategy`/`tentacle_retention_with_strategy`; omitted, it inherits the lifecycle-level policy. When the prompt gives per-phase retention (observed lost: Argo Staging 30 items/30 days, Argo Prod 50 items/180 days), each `phase` MUST set them explicitly:

```hcl
  phase {
    name                                  = "Argo Prod"
    # optional_deployment_targets, is_optional_phase, ...
    release_retention_with_strategy {
      strategy         = "Count"
      quantity_to_keep = 50
      unit             = "Items"
    }

    tentacle_retention_with_strategy {
      strategy         = "Count"
      quantity_to_keep = 180
      unit             = "Days"
    }
  }
```

## Runbook `retention_policy_with_strategy` `strategy = "Default"` = Keep Forever

`strategy = "Default"` keeps unlimited runs (observed: "keep 5 runs" lost). Only `Default`, `Count`, `Forever` are valid (`stringvalidator.OneOf`; e.g. `"Quantity"` fails at plan). Finite: `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = 5`, `unit = "Items"` (`"Items"` for RUNS, `"Days"` only for days). `"Default"`/`strategy = "Forever"` take NO other attributes (`quantity_to_keep must not be set when the strategy is '"Default"'`).

## Channel "Properties" = CustomFieldDefinitions, Not Description

A channel "property" (e.g. `PromotionLevel = manual`) → `custom_field_definitions` on `octopusdeploy_channel`: ``[{ description = "The promotion level", field_name = "PromotionLevel" }]``. Never use `Description` instead. Values (`manual`) are not persisted.

## `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` Ambiguity: "0 Days" Does Not Mean Immediate Deletion in the Way Intended

`"Default"` + `quantity_to_keep = 0` keeps forever; `"Count"`/`"Days"`/`0` is ambiguous. For "0 days"/"delete immediately"/very short, use `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = 1`, `unit = "Days"`. Any finite retention requires `strategy = "Count"`; `"Default"`/`"Forever"` keep indefinitely whatever `quantity_to_keep` says.

## Kubernetes Step Worker Pool for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL:** Kubernetes steps (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, `Octopus.Kubernetes.Kustomize`, etc.) with no prompt-specified worker pool: set `worker_pool_variable = "Octopus.Project.WorkerPool"` and `worker_pool_id = null` (never `worker_pool_id` directly). Every Kubernetes project MUST have this variable, and these data sources whenever any Kubernetes step exists:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" "workerpool_hosted_ubuntu" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Hosted Ubuntu"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

data "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" "workerpool_default_worker_pool" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Default Worker Pool"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "my_project_octopus_project_workerpool_1" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  owner_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) == 0 ? octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id : data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id}"
  value        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_hosted_ubuntu.worker_pools[0].id : data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools[0].id}"
  name         = "Octopus.Project.WorkerPool"
  type         = "WorkerPool"
  description  = "Using a variable to define the worker pool used by steps allows the worker pool to be easily changed in a central location."
  is_sensitive = false
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [sensitive_value]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

## Trigger Type Is Set by the Resource, Not by the Description

A trigger's `description` doesn't change its behaviour; the resource (and schedule block) does.
- **Package push / CI** ("deploy when a new package is pushed", "on a new package matching *pattern*") → `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger` with `package { deployment_action_slug, package_reference }` plus `channel_id` (no channel named → create an `Application` channel and link it). Never `once_daily_schedule`.
- **Time-based** ("nightly", "every Monday at 6am") → `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger` with `once_daily_schedule`.

`description` must state what the trigger ACTUALLY does; if inexpressible (e.g. no package step for `deployment_action_slug`), use the closest valid trigger and disclose the approximation there.

# Bash Instructions

* Use this loop form, never `for i in $(seq 1 30); do`:

```
for i in {1..5}; do
    echo "Number: $i"
done
```

* Avoid the dollar sign `$` (e.g. bash variables) in simple bash scripts — hard to escape in Terraform.

# Patterns

The following instructions provide default guidance on how to implement common deployment patterns, processes, or strategies.

## Run a runbook

* Unless the prompt specifies otherwise, you must use the "Run Octopus Deploy Runbook" community step template from "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/0444b0b3-088e-4689-b755-112d1360ffe3" to run a runbook from a deployment process.

## Blocking release progression

* Unless the prompt specifies otherwise, you must use the "Block Release Progression" community step template from "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/78a182b3-5369-4e13-9292-b7f991295ad1" to block a release from progressing through environments.

## Kubernetes target

**Apply the canonical pattern below (often under-triggered)** when the project has ANY Kubernetes step (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, or any step with `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` meant for a cluster) unless the prompt gives an actual cluster URL with real credentials, or explicitly says to use an existing named target — only that is "a specific target configuration". Still apply it when the prompt:
* only names a target tag/role (e.g. "deploy to targets tagged `Kubernetes`", "using targets tagged `octopub/frontend`");
* says "deploy to a Kubernetes cluster", "deploy to EKS/AKS/GKE", or names a cluster without a URL;
* asks for an account (Token, AWS, Azure, username/password) but no cluster URL — if it names its own account (e.g. a Token account called "K8s"), use THAT on the "Mock K8s" target and skip "Mock Token";
* is a canary, blue/green, progressive, wave, or rollout prompt implying a cluster.

**CRITICAL:** `cluster_url` on `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` MUST be a valid absolute URL (else `The cluster URL must be a valid URL e.g. https://mycluster`, apply dies). If the prompt's URL is invalid (e.g. "not a url" for target "fleet-k8s"), use `https://mockk8s.octopusdemos.com` and record the original text in the target description; never pass it through or drop the target. (`octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_uri` is not validated.)

**Allowed properties per step type** (from `everystep.tf`; never add to a "no" type):

| Property | `KubernetesDeployRawYaml` | `Kubernetes.Kustomize` | `HelmChartUpgrade` | `KubernetesRunScript` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck` | yes | yes | yes | no |
| `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled` (and `.ForceConflicts`) | yes | yes | no | no |
| `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait` | yes | no | no | no |

* "use client side apply"/"disable verification checks" with Kustomize/Helm: `ResourceStatusCheck = "False"` on raw-YAML, Kustomize AND Helm; `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "False"` on raw-YAML and Kustomize only.

* Canonical pattern (every Kubernetes step type, incl. `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` and `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, requires an `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target`):
    * YOU MUST create a token account called "Mock Token", link it to any tenants, and authenticate the target with it (never another account type):
    ```
     authentication {
        account_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_token.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_token.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_token_account.account_token[0].id}"
      }
    ```
    * YOU MUST create a feed called "Docker Hub" pointing to "https://index.docker.io", anonymous (no credentials).
    * YOU MUST add a target called "Mock K8s", with the tag "Kubernetes", using the token account, pointing to "https://mockk8s.octopusdemos.com", health check image "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04" from the "Docker Hub" feed, worker pool "Hosted Ubuntu", linked to any tenants.
    * **CRITICAL:** if the steps use any target tag other than "Kubernetes", name it `Mock K8s <target tag>` (e.g. "Mock K8s llm-cluster"); `roles` = every tag the Kubernetes steps use plus `"Kubernetes"`; the paired `data "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets"` uses the same `partial_name`. Otherwise an existing "Mock K8s" (observed: roles `["Kubernetes", "storefront-cluster"]`) makes `count` 0 and no matching target exists.
    * Every "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" step MUST override template defaults in `execution_properties` ("Mock K8s" supports no verification, deployment wait or server side apply; `ResourceStatusCheck = "True"`/`ServerSideApply.Enabled = "True"` fail): `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck = "False"`, `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait = "NoWait"`, `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled = "False"`. Raw-YAML ONLY — never on "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript" or "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade" steps.

## Blue/Green deployment pattern

* When Blue/Green is required, add these IN ADDITION to template steps (even if the prompt lists its steps "and no others"):
    * First: step template "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/72db001f-ae7f-4a0f-b952-5f80e2fc4cd2" ("Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment"). Output `SequentialDeploy` is `True` if the same blue/green environment got two deployments in a row, `False` if alternating correctly. Set `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name` = "Blue Production" and `BlueGreen.Environment.Green.Name` = "Green Production" (different, neither "Production"). `BlueGreen.Octopus.Api.Key` references a project variable (conventionally `#{Project.Octopus.Api.Key}`) that you MUST create as a sensitive `octopusdeploy_variable` with a placeholder value.
    * Second: a manual intervention step. **CRITICAL:** gate it with step-level `condition = "Variable"` and `"Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" = "#{Octopus.Action[Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment].Output.SequentialDeploy}"` — an `#{if Octopus.Action[...].Output.SequentialDeploy}…#{/if}` in `Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions` does not gate; `condition = "Success"` is wrong. A template's manual step (e.g. `Approve Production Deployment` in the VM Blue/Green template) is no substitute — condition it or add another. Self-check: some step has `condition = "Variable"`.
    * **CRITICAL:** Replace "Production" with "Blue Production" and "Green Production" — no plain `Production` environment or phase entry (`["Production", "Blue Production", "Green Production"]` is wrong); "containing the production environments" means the pair.
    * **CRITICAL:** `Blue Production` and `Green Production` each get their OWN `phase` (one environment in `optional_deployment_targets`), BOTH `is_optional_phase = true`, never merged (see the "parallel production stacks" rule and "Example Octopus VM Blue/Green Terraform Configuration"). Keep a pre-production phase (e.g. `Development`/`Test`) `is_optional_phase = false`.

## Debugging

* When debugging is required without specifics: add project variables "OctopusPrintVariables" (description "Prints variable values to the verbose logs") and "OctopusPrintEvaluatedVariables" (description "Prints evaluated variable values to the verbose logs"), both value "True"; set `default_guided_failure_mode` to `On` on `octopusdeploy_project`.

## Rollback or redeployments

* When only some steps must run on rollback, or redeployments are required, without specifics: first step = community template "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/d166457a-1421-4731-b143-dd6766fb95d5" ("Calculate Deployment Mode"); gate steps on its outputs.

### Deployment Mode
From release numbers (not packages): `Deploy` (newer), `Rollback` (older), `Redeploy` (same).

### Version Difference
`Identical`, or first differing part of 2021.1.2.10: `Major`, `Minor`, `Build`, `Revision`.

### Manual or Automatic Trigger
`True` (trigger: scheduled or deployment target) or `False` (manual).

### Output Variables

**DeploymentMode**, **Trigger**, **VersionChange** (values above).

##### Variable Run Condition Output Variables
##### Variable Run Condition Usage
Run conditions are _always_ evaluated, even after errors; **Truthy** runs the step. Always: `#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}`; Success only: `#{unless Octopus.Deployment.Error}#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}#{/unless}`; Failure only: `#{if Octopus.Deployment.Error}#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}#{/if}`. Substitute any variable below for **RunOnDeploy**.

##### Deployment Mode Run Conditions
**RunOnDeploy**, **RunOnRollback**, **RunOnRedeploy**, **RunOnDeployOrRollback**, **RunOnDeployOrRedeploy**, **RunOnRedeployOrRollback** (match **DeploymentMode**).

##### Version Change Run Conditions
**RunOnMajorVersionChange**, **RunOnMinorVersionChange**, **RunOnMajorOrMinorVersionChange**, **RunOnBuildVersionChange**, **RunOnRevisionVersionChange** (match **VersionChange**).

### Usage

Deployment processes only, not runbooks. Run on a worker (or the Octopus Server); on targets it just wastes compute.
